(1) المقدمة | Introduction — Start Smart with Baccalaureate
==========================================================================================

◆ ابدأ رحلتك مع البكالوريا بخطوات واضحة ومهارات عملية

ابدا رحلتك مع البكالوريا بخطوات واضحه ومهارات عمليه مع كتاب الــمُعاصــر

فكرة عن المنهج

صمم هذا المنهج ليجعل تعلم اللغة الإنجليزية رحلة متكاملة تجمع بين الفهم والتذكير والتطبيق.
- ستتعلم كيف تستخدم اللغة للتعبير عن أفكارك، وتحليل المعلومات، وتكوين آراء مدعومة بالأدلة.
- فان الهدف ليس فقط حفظ اللغة، بل استخدامها بثقة ووعي في مواقف جديدة ومختلفة.

- تأخذ كل وحدة من وحدات هذا المنهج في رحلة تبدأ باكتشاف الفكرة الرئيسية وتكوين رأي أول، ثم بناء اللغة من خلال المفردات والتراكيب والقواعد في سياقات حقيقية.

كيفية التعامل مع المنهج

المرحلة الأولى

المرحلة الأولى: Explore the Idea

قبل دراسة الكلمات أو قراءة النص، توقف أمام عنوان الدرس وسؤال الاستقصاء.
اكتب إجابة أولية قصيرة تعبر عن رأيك الحالي، حتى لو لم تكن متأكدًا منها.

مثال

Inquiry Question:
How does social media affect teenagers' self-image?

My First Thought:
I think social media can affect teenagers negatively because it encourages comparison.

• احتفظ بهذه الإجابة؛ لأنك ستعود إليها في نهاية الدرس لتطورها باستخدام ما تعلمته.

المرحلة الثانية

المرحلة الثانية: Build Your Language

الكلمات والقواعد في الوحدة ليست أجزاء منفصلة!
إنها الأدوات التي ستستخدمها للتعبير عن أفكارك.
عند دراسة أي كلمة، تعلمها من خلال:

Meaning → Form → Collocation → Context → Use

مثال

influence:

Meaning : التأثير في شخص أو شيء

Word family: influence – influential

Collocation: have an influence on

Context: social media and self-image

Use: Social media may have a strong influence on teenagers' choices.

• الهدف ليس أن تذكر معنى الكلمة فقط، بل أن تستطيع استخدامها في إجابة أو فقرة.

المرحلة الثالثة

المرحلة الثالثة:
لا تقرأ النص مرة واحدة وتحاول الإجابة عن جميع الأسئلة. اقرأه في ثلاث جولات:

First Round Get the Message

اقرأ بسرعة لتحديد:
• موضوع النص.
• هدف الكاتب.
• الفكرة الرئيسية لكل فقرة.

Second Round Find the Evidence

ارجع إلى النص واقرأ مرة أخرى وحدد:
• الأسباب والنتائج.
• الحقائق والأرقام.
• الأمثلة.
• آراء الكاتب.
• الكلمات التي تكشف التحيز.

Third Round Think Beyond the Text

• قارن الأفكار.
• اختشف الافتراضات.
• قيم قوة الأدلة.
• كوّن رأيك الشخصي.

افهم لغت السؤال

قبل الإجابة، ضع دائرة حول Command Word (المطلوب منك):
لأنها تحدد مستوى التفكير المطلوب.

إذا بدأ السؤال ب | فالمطلوب منك

Identify — استخراج معلومة محددة.
Explain — توضيح الفكرة وسببها.
Analyze — تفكيك الفكرة وربط السبب بالنتيجة.
Compare — توضيح أوجه التشابه والاختلاف.
Evaluate — إصدار حكم مدعوم بمعايير وأدلة.
Justify — الدفاع عن إجابتك بسبب ودليل.
To what extent — تحديد درجة اتفاقك مع مناقشة الجانب الآخر.

المرحلة الثالثة
تابع المرحلة الثالثة:
لاحظ هذا المثال
Example: How does exercise affect teenagers' well-being?

Identify
Exercise releases endorphins.

Explain
Exercise improves well-being because it
releases endorphins that reduce stress.

Analyze
Exercise improves teenagers' physical
and mental well-being by reducing stress,
improving mood, and supporting better sleep.

Evaluate
Exercise is one of the most effective ways
to improve teenagers' well-being because it
supports both physical and mental health.
However, it should be combined with
sufficient sleep and balanced nutrition.

كما تغيّرت كلمة السؤال،
تغيّر عمق الإجابة المطلوبة.

ابن إجابتك بطريقة JRE
استخدم هذه الخريطة في أسئلة التحليل والرأي:

J — J - Justify
ما موقفك الواضح من السؤال؟

R — R — Reason
لماذا تتبنى هذا الموقف؟

E — E — Evidence
ما الدليل من النص؟

→ — Extend
كيف تفسّر الدليل؟
وهل توجد وجهة نظر أخرى؟

تطبيق عملي

Question: .........……
To what extent does social media present a realistic picture of life?

Judgment: .........……
Social media presents only a partly realistic picture of life.

Reason: .........……
Users usually select the moments they want others to see.

Evidence: .........……
The text describes this selective presentation as the "highlight-reel effect."

المرحلة الثالثة
تابع المرحلة الثالثة:
However, social media can present real experiences when users
communicate honestly and responsibly.

استخدم عبارات الربط:
• This shows that …
• This suggests that …
• This indicates that …
• This supports the idea that …

ذكر الدليل وحدده لا يصنع إجابة قوية.
بعد كل دليل، اشرح علاقته بفكرتك

الإجابة النهائية
Social media presents only a partly realistic picture of life because users usually select
their most successful moments. The text describes this as the "highlight-reel effect,"
which can create unrealistic expectations. However, social media may reflect real
experiences when it is used honestly and responsibly.

مهارة الكتابة
نموذج + JRE
• دليل من النص أو التسجيل.
..........
إذا أصبحت إجابتك أكثر
وضوحًا، وأفضل تنظيمًا،
وأقوى دليلًا، فقد تحقق
التعلم الحقيقي.

القاعدة الذهبية للوحدة
Don't just give
an answer.
Build an argument.

لا تقدّم إجابة فقط؛
ابنِ موقفًا، وفسّره،
وأثبته بالدليل.

عندما تصل إلى Writing Task، لا تبدأ الكتابة مباشرة. ابنٍ فكرتك باستخدام:
Claim Reason Evidence Explanation Other View Judgment

واسأل نفسك قبل التسليم:
• هل أجبت عن السؤال مباشرة؟
• هل أوضحت السبب؟
• هل استخدمت دليلًا مناسبًا؟
• هل شرحت قيمة الدليل؟
• هل عرضت وجهة نظر أخرى؟
• هل وصلت إلى حكم نهائي واضح؟
• هل استخدمت مفردات الدرس وأحد التراكيب اللغوية الجديدة.

==========================================================================================
(2) الوحدة 1 | CONCEPT 1: Well-being | Unit 1: Living Well in a Complex World
==========================================================================================

◆ النواتج التعليمية ونظرة عامة على الوحدة | Learning Outcomes & Unit Overview

◆ النواتج التعليمية | Learning Outcomes

• Reading
identify bias in media texts, recognize rhetorical devices such as rhetorical questions and intensifiers, and understand their effects.

◆ نظرة عامة على الوحدة | Unit Overview
This unit explores how the concept of "well-being" is shaped by personal choices, social influences, and cultural contexts. It focuses on how young people understand themselves while facing modern challenges such as lifestyle habits, social media, and mental health.

You will examine how ideas of a "healthy" or "successful life" are influenced by media and society, and how these can create bias or unrealistic expectations. Through inquiry and discussion, you can develop the ability to question assumptions, evaluate evidence, and consider different perspectives.

• Listening
compare peers' perspectives on cultural and psychological aspects of social media and respond to them.

• Speaking
express and justify opinions on topics such as social media responsibility, platform redesign, and cultural influences.

• Writing
use neutral and hedging language in evidence-based sentences and write reflective responses.

Content Objectives | Language Objectives

You will:
• identify key concepts related to lifestyle and well-being.
• explain factors affecting physical and mental well-being.
• analyze how media influences perceptions of well-being.
• evaluate arguments and evidence related to health and lifestyle.
• construct structured arguments using Justify - Reason - Evidence [JRE].

You will:
• use participial phrases to present evidence.
• use relative clauses to support arguments.
• use "If" conditionals.
• use hedging techniques in writing.
• use persuasive and evaluative language.
• participate in structured discussions.
• write analytical paragraphs using JRE.

◆ تدريبات المفردات | Exercises on Vocabulary

• Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

◆ المفردات الرئيسية | Key Vocabulary

1. The lecturer said "Who wants to live under pressure?" He asked a/an .......... question to make the students think; not to receive an answer.
a. selective    b. rhetorical    c. unrealistic    d. filtered

2. In "extremely popular", the meaning became stronger because of the ........... .
a. acquaintance          b. version          c. symptom          d. intensifier
3. She said, "Online life is never real." This comment sounds ........... .
a. absolute          b. ordinary          c. hedged          d. social
4. The article praised influencers and ignored their negative effects, so readers noticed its ........... .
a. reality          b. treasure          c. bias          d. research
5. The comment became ........... when the writer called the photo "terrible".
a. psychological          b. hedged          c. evaluative          d. ordinary
6. The lesson asked us to make a/an ........... between online photos and real life.
a. expectation          b. comparison          c. symptom          d. version
7. The lesson links online posts to students' friendships and online interactions, so it discusses ........... effects.
a. psychological          b. ordinary          c. distant          d. social
8. The report was ........... because it supported one opinion and attacked the other.
a. neutral          b. ordinary          c. distant          d. biased

2 Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

9. Khalid reported every foul committed by one team but ignored similar fouls by its rival. Readers criticized him for bias. Which situation best demonstrates "bias"?
a. A film reviewer applies the same standards when judging films from different studios.
b. A film reviewer changes a rating after discovering that an earlier fact was wrong.
c. A film reviewer gives equally strong films similar scores regardless of who produced them.
d. A film reviewer regularly praises one studio while judging equally good films from its rival much more harshly.
10. SB A podcast host invited only guests who agreed with his views and dismissed opposing opinions without discussing them fairly. Listeners described the program as biased. Which situation is the clearest example of being "biased"?
a. A judge scores every performance according to the same published rules.
b. A judge gives a friend a higher score even though another performer clearly did better.
c. A judge asks another official to review a result that is extremely close.
d. A judge changes a score after noticing a genuine mistake in the first calculation.
11. Rodayna was choosing between traveling by train or bus. She considered the travel time, comfort and cost of both; this comparison helped her decide. Which situation best demonstrates "comparison"?
a. A family describes the rooms in the flat they already live in.
b. A family asks a landlord when a broken window will be repaired.
c. A family checks the rent, location and size of two flats before choosing one.
d. A family chooses the first flat they visit without looking at another one.

12. A book club meets every Friday, and members often stay afterwards to talk and get to
know one another. It has become an important social activity in the neighborhood.
Which situation best reflects the meaning of "social"?
a. A teenager spends Saturday repairing his bicycle alone in the garage.
b. A large family gathering gives relatives who rarely meet a chance to talk and reconnect.
c. A runner follows an individual training programme at home every evening.
d. A reader spends the weekend finishing a novel quietly in her room.

◆ التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

تنويه هذا الجزء هام جداً سواء على مستوى تعلُّم اللغة أو التميز في الامتحان.

1 Definitions التعريفات

Word | Definition
absolute word (n) كلمة قاطعة - لفظ بات | a word that states an idea with full confidence
bias (n) تحيّز | a strong opinion or preference that makes judgement unfair
biased language (n) لغة متحيزة | words that show a strong opinion and are not fair or neutral
evaluative adjective (n) صفة تقييمية | an adjective that expresses a judgement or opinion (e.g. amazing, toxic, unrealistic)
hedging word (n) لفظ مُتحفِّظ / كلمة احترازية | a word that shows uncertainty or caution
highlight-reel effect (n) تأثير اللقطات البرزة والمثالية | the tendency to post only the best moments online, creating a false image of reality
intensifier (n) أداة تقوية / مكثِّف | a word that makes an idea stronger
neutral language (n) لغة محايدة | balanced, objective language that is free from bias
rhetorical question (n) سؤال بلغي | a question that does not need an answer and is used to make the reader think
social comparison (n) مقارنة اجتماعية | evaluating yourself by comparing yourself to others

2 Synonyms المترادفات

Word | Synonym
absolute (adj) مطلق - بات | certainty, categorical term, unconditional statement
bias (n) تحيّز | prejudice, partiality, one-sidedness
biased (adj) متحيز | prejudiced, partial, one-sided
evaluative (adj) تقييمي | judgmental, opinion-based, assessment-based
hedging (n) تحوّط - مواربة - تحشُّب | cautious language, softening language, qualifying expression

highlight-reel effect (n)	جذاذة
intensifier (n)	أداة تقوية / مكثف
neutral (adj)	محايد

3 Antonyms المتضادات

Word	Antonym
absolute (adj)	مُطلق - بات
bias (n)	تحيّز
biased (adj)	متحيّز
evaluative (adj)	تقييمي
hedging (adj)	مُتحوّط - مُتخشّب
highlight-reel effect (n)	تأثير اللقطات البارزة والمثالية
intensifier (n)	مُقوّي / مُكثّف
neutral (adj)	محايد
rhetorical question (n)	سؤال بلاغي

◆ تدريبات التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Exercises on Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

◆ التعريفات | Definitions

Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

1. After reading the novel, Mariam wrote, "The theme is strong, but the ending is disappointing." Her teacher asked her to identify the evaluative adjectives. What does "evaluative adjective" mean in this context?
a. An adjective that states a factual quality such as size or color
b. An adjective that expresses a judgment about quality or value
c. An adjective that makes a statement less certain or definite
d. An adjective that identifies when or where something happened

2. Using a hedging word, Dina changed "This habit improves sleep" to "This habit may improve sleep." What does "hedging word" mean in this context?
a. A word that makes a claim less certain or more cautious
b. A word that makes a claim stronger and more definite
c. A word that expresses approval or disapproval of something
d. A word that introduces a comparison between two things

3. You see only influencers' holidays, celebrations and achievements online and begin to
believe that their lives are always perfect. You are experiencing the highlight-reel effect.
Which definition describes the meaning of "highlight-reel effect"?
a. The habit of comparing prices before buying something
b. The practice of sharing every detail of daily life online
c. The tendency to show only the best moments and create an unrealistic picture of life
d. The habit of judging online information by checking several sources
4. The travel review described the old market as "extremely crowded" and the
atmosphere as "incredibly lively." The words extremely and incredibly are intensifiers.
In this context, what does "intensifier" mean?
a. A word that reduces the certainty of a statement
b. A word that introduces a different point of view
c. A word that removes personal judgement from language
d. A word that increases the strength or degree of an expression
5. The news article used neutral language to describe what both teams did without
praising or attacking either one.
Based on the sentence above, what does "neutral language" mean?
a. Strongly emotional language used to influence readers
b. Balanced, objective language that is free from bias
c. Cautious language used to show uncertainty
d. Descriptive language used to make an idea stronger
6. During a talk about wasting water, Rokaya asked the rhetorical question, "Do we
really want a future without enough clean water?"
What does "rhetorical question" mean in this context?
a. A question used for effect or reflection rather than to obtain an answer
b. A question asked to obtain specific factual information from someone
c. A question asked to compare two possible choices directly
d. A question asked to check whether a previous statement is accurate
7. After seeing his friends' posts about expensive vacations, Youssef began to judge his
own life against theirs. This kind of social comparison affected how satisfied he felt.
Which meaning best matches "social comparison" as used in this sentence?
a. Examining how two communities organize their daily lives
b. Discussing similarities between people in the same family
c. Evaluating yourself by comparing yourself with other people
d. Studying how people communicate in different social situations

◆ المترادفات | Synonyms

◆ المتضادات | Antonyms

1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :
1. "The article showed clear bias." The antonym of the underlined word is ..........
a. prejudice b. one-sidedness c. neutrality d. partiality
2. "The speaker used an intensifier before the adjective." The antonym of the underlined word is ..........
a. amplifier b. strengthening word c. emphasis word d. softener
3. "The post created a highlight-reel effect." The antonym of the underlined phrase is ..........
a. complete picture b. curated reality
c. selective presentation d. idealized snapshot effect
4. "The writer asked a rhetorical question." The antonym of the underlined phrase is ..........
a. persuasive question b. emphatic question
c. question for effect d. direct question

2 Find the words or phrases in the text that are antonyms of the words below.
A careful reader should look for the complete picture, not just perfect scenes. A good article shows neutrality and avoids taking one side unfairly. Instead of judgmental language, it may use descriptive details. A writer can also choose a softener instead of a word that makes meaning stronger. In discussion, a teacher may ask a direct question to get real information from students. This helps readers notice whether language is fair, clear, and balanced.

3. evaluative → ..........
4. intensifier → ..........
5. rhetorical question → ..........

◆ دراسة المفردات | Vocabulary Study

1 Verbal Collocations المتلازمات اللفظية

create unrealistic expectations يخلق توقعات غير واقعية
report symptoms يُبلغ عن أعراض
draw ... into the topic يجذب ... إلى الموضوع
reshape self-image يعيد تشكيل الصورة الذاتية
inspire healthy habits يُشجع علي عادات صحية
shape how ... think يشكّل طريقة تفكير ...
play a major role يلعب دورًا رئيسيًا
treat ... as a complete picture يتعامل مع ... كصورة كاملة

2 Expressions & Idioms التعبيرات والمصطلحات

a false image of reality صورة زائفة للواقع
low self-esteem انخفاض تقدير الذات
highly curated stages مراحل منتقاة ومنسقة بعناية شديدة
no excuses لا أعذار
perfectly balanced diet نظام غذائي متوازن مثالي
contribution to مساهمة في
identity formation تكوين الهوية
warrant guidelines for يستدعي وضع إرشادات لـ......
image-based platforms منصات قائمة علي الصور

3 Verb + Preposition فعل + حرف جر

be linked to يكون مرتبطًا ب
start with يبدأ ب
compare (...) to/with يقارن (...) ب
transform from يتحول من
draw (...) into يجذب ... إلى
transform into يتحول إلي
fail at يفشل في
treat ... as يتعامل مع ك
present ... asيعرض علي أنه
tend to يميل أن
reshape (...) into يعيد تشكيل إلي
wake (...) up يستيقظ - يُوقظ ...

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

absolute word (n) كلمة قاطعة - لفظ بات

Goes with:
• use absolute words يستخدم مفردات قاطعة
• avoid absolute words يتجنب المفردات القاطعة
• absolute language لغة حاسمة لا تقبل التأويل

Word Family

Word Example
absolute (adj) مطلق - لا يقبل التأويل “Always” is an absolute word.
absolutely (adv) بشكل بات The post speaks absolutely.
absolutism (n) القطعية - الصرامة الجارة Absolutism in language can sound biased.

Don't mix
- absolute word = gives full certainty.
- hedging word = shows caution or uncertainty.

bias (n) تحيّز

Goes with:
• show bias يُظهر تحيُّزًا
• identify bias يتعرّف على التمييز
• reduce bias يُقلِّل التحيّز

Word Family

Word | Example
bias (n) تحيّز | The post shows bias.
biased (adj) متحيز | The language is biased.
unbiased (adj) غير متحيز | The report is unbiased.

Don't mix
- bias = unfair judgment
- opinion = personal view, which may be fair or unfair

biased language (n) لغة متحيزة

Goes with:
• use biased language يستخدم لغة غير محايدة
• rewrite biased language يعيد صياغة اللغة غير المحايدة

Don't mix
- biased language = unfair or one-sided
- neutral language = balanced and objective

evaluative adjective (n) صفة تقييمية

Goes with:
• use evaluative adjectives يستخدم صفات تقييمية
• avoid strong evaluative adjectives تجنب الصفات التقييمية القوية

Word Family

Word | Example
evaluate (v) يقيّم | - Viewers evaluate posts in their comments.
evaluation (n) تقييم | - The evaluation of the research was fair.
evaluative (adj) تقييمي | - "Amazing" is an evaluative adjective.

Don't mix
- evaluative adjective = shows judgment
- descriptive adjective = gives factual description

hedging word (n) لفظ متحفّظ/فوارب/متّخشب
Goes with:
- neutral and hedging language لغة محايدة ومتحفظة
Word Family
Word | Example
hedge (v) يخفف - يتحفظ في - يتحوط من Writers hedge strong claims.
hedging (n) تحوط - تخشب Hedging makes writing more careful.
hedged (adj) مُلطّف - متحفّظ A hedged claim is cautious.
Don't mix
- hedging word e.g. may, might, could
- absolute word e.g. always, never, every
highlight-reel effect (n) تأثير اللقطات المثالية
Goes with:
- highlight-reel effect online يُبرز فقط اللحظات المثالية على الإنترنت.
- experience the highlight-reel effect يتأثر بظاهرة عرض اللحظات المثالية فقط.
Word Family
Word | Example
highlight (v) يبرز - Posts highlight perfect moments.
highlighted (adj) بارز - Highlighted moments may seem perfect.
highlight (n) لحظة بارزة - A highlight is one of the best moments.
Don't mix
- highlight-reel effect = only best moments are shown
- real life = includes success, failure, and ordinary moments
intensifier (n) أداة تقوية / مُكثّف
Word Family
Word | Example
intense (adj) شديد - The post mentions intense exercise.
intensify (v) يقوي - These words intensify the message.
intensifier (n) أداة تقوية - "Extremely" is an intensifier.
Don't mix
- intensifier = makes meaning stronger
- hedging word = makes meaning more cautious

neutral language (n)                                      لغة محايدة

Word Family

Word                                        Example
neutral (adj)                محايد         - The sentence is neutral.
neutrality (n)               حيادية        - Neutrality improves fairness.
neutralize (v)               يُحَيِّد - يُخَفِّف     - Hedging can neutralize a strong claim.

Don't mix
- neutral language = balanced and fair
- weak language = unclear or unsupported

rhetorical question (n)                      سؤال بلاغي

Goes with:
• open with a rhetorical question              يبدأ بسؤال بلاغي

Word Family

Word                                        Example
rhetoric (n)        بلاغة - خطاب مؤثر        - Rhetoric affects the audience.
rhetorical (adj)              بلاغي         - This is a rhetorical question.
rhetorically (adv)            بلاغياً        - The question is used rhetorically.

Don't mix
- rhetorical question = used just for effect
- real question = expects an answer

social comparison (n)                      مقارنة اجتماعية

Goes with:
• mechanism of social comparison              آلية المقارنة الاجتماعية
• avoid social comparison                     يتجاش المقارنة الاجتماعية

Word Family

Word                                        Example
compare (v)                 يقارن         - Don't compare a child to other children.
comparison (n)              مقارنة        - Comparison can create pressure.
comparable (adj)            قابل للمقارنة     - These situations are not comparable.

Don't mix
- social comparison = comparing yourself to others
- self-reflection = thinking about yourself without comparison

◆ تدريبات المفردات | Exercises on Vocabulary

1 Collocations, Expressions, Idioms & Prepositions

1 Choose the correct answer from a, b, c or d :

1. I usually wake ............ early when I have an important journey.
a. with          b. into          c. to          d. up
2. Over time, the area transformed ............ a quiet village into a busy town.
a. from          b. with          c. into          d. at
3. People tend ............ check their phones when they hear a notification.
a. at          b. into          c. to          d. with
4. Poor sleep can be linked ............ heavy phone use at night.
a. as          b. to          c. at          d. into
5. The advertisement presents the phone ............ the perfect choice for teenagers.
a. as          b. at          c. into          d. with
6. My article compares city life ............ village life in several ways.
a. from          b. as          c. with          d. into
7. An artist reshaped the old piece of wood ............ a beautiful decoration.
a. at          b. as          c. from          d. into
8. That empty building was transformed ............ a modern community center.
a. from          b. as          c. into          d. with
9. Teachers often ............ a major role in shaping their students' characters.
a. treat          b. play          c. avoid          d. find
10. Doing sports regularly ............ healthy habits.
a. neglects          b. discourages          c. inspires          d. destroys
11. The doctor helped her follow a/an ............ balanced diet.
a. perfectly          b. identity          c. ability          d. silly

Word Family

2 Complete the sentences using the correct form of the word in brackets.
1. The article revealed a clear ............ (biased) in favour of one side of the argument.
2. Before she bought her phone yesterday, Mariam ............ (comparison) its price and features with those of another model.
3. The reviewer used several ............ (evaluate) adjectives, such as "excellent" and "disappointing."
4. In the final draft, the writer ............ (hedging) the claim by changing will to might.
5. In the phrase "extremely important", the word "extremely" is used to ............ (intensifier) the meaning of the adjective.
6. A fair article should describe both sides ............ (neutral) without favouring either one.
7. The speaker asked the question ............ (rhetorical) to make the audience reflect rather than reply.
8. The speaker was ............ (absolute) certain that his solution was the only possible one.
9. The article's ............ (neutral) helped readers trust that both sides were treated fairly.
10. The final ............ (compare) between the two adverts showed how differently they presented the same product.
11. The critic's careful ............ (evaluative) of the film included both its strengths and its weaknesses.

◆ القواعد اللغوية | Grammar Focus

Relative Clauses    عبارات الوصل

◆ البداية | Getting Started

١ تبدأ عبارات الوصل بأحد ضمائر/ظروف الوصل التالية:

who, whom, which, that, where, when, whose, what

e.g.

Relative pronoun    Relative clause

The little girl who is playing over there is my daughter.

٢ عبارات الوصل تعطي معلومات عن شخص أو شيء أو مكان أو زمان أو سبب أو فكرة:

e.g. - I know the man who/that looks after the garden.    (person)
- A lion is an animal which/that is endangered.    (thing)
- Cairo is a city where millions of people live.    (place)
- Do you remember the day when we first met?    (time)

٣ قد تكون عبارات الوصل تحديدية، أي إن المعلومات ضرورية لتحديد المقصود وفي هذه الحالة لا نستخدم معها الفواصل:

e.g. - The bird which is on the branch is an eagle.    (✓)
- The eagle, which is a bird, is a symbol of strength.    (✗)

٤ قد تكون عبارات الوصل غير تحديدية أي إن المعلومات إضافية وشأنة ويمكن حذفها دون التأثير على المعنى، وفي هذه الحالة نستخدم معها الفواصل:

e.g. - Egypt, which has a rich history, attracts tourists.    (✓)
- Egypt which has a rich history attracts tourists.    (✗)

2 In Detail: Relative pronouns

1 who/that    ١ الذي/التي/الذين/اللاتي (للعاقل)

Subject

١. نستخدم (who/that) بعد اسم عاقل كفاعل، وفي هذه الحالة يأتي بعدها فعل ويمكن حذفها ونستخدم الفعل مضافا له (ing) في المبني للمعلوم أو تصريف ثالث في المبني للمجهول:

e.g. - The nutritionist who works at the clinic gave us useful advice.    (✓)
= The nutritionist working at the clinic gave us useful advice.    (✓)

e.g. - The baby who/that was examined yesterday is still ill.    (✓)
= The baby examined yesterday is still ill.    (✓)

٢. تُستخدم (who/that) كمفعول بعد اسم عاقل ، وفي هذه الحالة يأتي بعدها فاعل ويمكن حذفها:
e.g. - The baby who/that I examined yesterday is still ill. (✓)
= The baby I examined yesterday is still ill. (✓)
With prepositions
٣. لا تُستخدم حرف جر قبل ضمير الوصل (who/that)، لكن يتم وضعه في المكان المناسب في عبارة الوصل.
e.g. - The doctor to who I spoke recommended more whole grains. (✗)
= The doctor who I spoke to recommended more whole grains. (✓)
2 whom/that
٢ الذي/التي/الذين/اللاتي (للعاقل)
١. تُستخدم (whom/that) بعد اسم عاقل كمفعول، ويأتي بعدها فاعل ويمكن حذفها:
e.g. - The baby whom/that I examined yesterday is still ill. (✓)
= The baby I examined yesterday is still ill. (✓)
With prepositions
٢. تُستخدم حروف الجر قبل ضمير الوصل (whom)، وفي اللغة غير الرسمية يمكن وضع حرف الجر في المكان المناسب في عبارة الوصل:
e.g. - The doctor to whom I spoke recommended more whole grains. (formal)
= The doctor whom I spoke to recommended more whole grains. (informal)
٣. يمكن أن يأتي فعل بعد ضمير الوصل (whom) إذا جاء قبلها تعبيرات مثل:
one, two, ..., all, some, ... + of + whom + verb ... فعل
e.g. - Two of my many friends are doctors. (whom)
= I have many friends, two of whom are doctors.
3 which/that
٣ الذي/التي/الذين/اللاتي (لغير العاقل)
Subject
١. تُستخدم (which/that) كفاعل بعد اسم غير عاقل، وفي هذه الحالة يأتي بعدها فعل ويمكن حذفها واستخدام الفعل مضافًا له (ing) في المبني للمعلوم أو تصريف ثالث في المبني للمجهول:
e.g. – Most animals which/that live in forests eat plants. (✓)
= Most animals living in forests eat plants. (✓)
e.g. - The deer which/that was hunted by the lion had a broken leg. (✓)
= The deer hunted by the lion had a broken leg. (✓)
٢. تُستخدم (which/that) كمفعول بعد اسم غير عاقل، وفي هذه الحالة يأتي بعدها فاعل ويمكن حذفها واستخدام التصريف الثالث (p.p.):
e.g. - The deer which/that you hunted had a broken leg. (✓)
= The deer you hunted had a broken leg. (✓)

With prepositions

أُستخدم حرف جر قبل ضمير الوصل (which)، ويمكن إبقاؤها وضعه في المكان المناسب في عبارة الوصل:

e.g. - This is the tool with which I do a lot of jobs. (✓)
= This is the tool which I do a lot of jobs with. (✓)

Choose the correct answer.
1. Ahmed asked me a difficult question, ............ I replied, "I don't know".
a. which            b. to which        c. at which        d. for which
2. I made a big mistake, ............ I apologized.
a. which            b. to which        c. at which        d. for which
3. I made a big mistake, ............ I apologized for.
a. which            b. to which        c. at which        d. for which

Sentential relative clause

يُمكن أن تُستخدم (which) كضمير وصل يعود على معنى الجملة التي تسبقها بالكامل:

e.g. - Students often use screens late at night, which can affect their sleep.
which = the fact that students often use screens late at night.

4 that        الذي/التي/الذين/اللاتي (مع العاقل وغير العاقل)

كما سبق، تُستخدم (that) مثل (who/whom/which) مع العاقل وغير العاقل، لكن لا يسبقها حرف جر ولد تأتي في الجملة الاعتراضية بعد (,):

e.g. - My friend with that I walk to school is crazy about football. (✗)
= My friend that I walk to school with is crazy about football. (✓)
e.g. - Egypt, that is an African country, has a rich history. (✗)
= Egypt, which is an African country, has a rich history. (✓)

5 Whose        الذي له / التي لها / الذين لهم / الخاص ب (مع العاقل وغير العاقل)

Rule

١. نستخدم (whose) للتعبير عن الملكية. ويأتي بعدها اسم دائمًا. ويمكن أن تشير إلى العاقل وغير العاقل.
e.g. - Students whose sleep routines are regular often concentrate better.
= The charity whose manager is my mother supports many families.
٢. نستخدم (whose) عندما نتجاج إلى ربط شخص أو شيء بشيء يخصه.
e.g. - The nutritionist whose advice we followed explained the importance of lean protein.

With prepositions

٣. في الإنجليزية الرسمية، يمكن أن يأتي حرف الجر قبل (whose + noun) وهذا التركيب أقل شيوعًا لكنه صحيح.
e.g. - The boy about whose diet the doctor was concerned needs support.

Omission
٤. لا نحذف (whose) لأنها تحمل معنى الملكية لكن يمكن استخدام تراكيب تؤدي معنى ضِمائره مثل:
→ - ... noun + whose + noun ...
→ = ... noun + who/which/that + have/has/had + noun ...
→ = ... with + noun ...
e.g. - The man who has gray hair is my uncle. (whose) (with)
= The man whose hair is gray is my uncle
= The man with gray hair is my uncle.

Tricky point
الأفعال لا تُستخدم بعدها (whose) لكن هناك بعض الأفعال التي تُستخدم كأسماء أيضًا مثل:

answer, attack, call, end, export, help, import, increase, influence,
plan, reply, start, stay, support, visit, ...

Choose the correct answer.
1. Sama ............ visits her grandmother regularly is a nice girl.
a. who                    b. whose                    c. whom                    d. why
2. Sama ............ visits to her grandmother are regular is a nice girl.
a. who                    b. whose                    c. whom                    d. why

3 In Detail: Relative adverbs

1 When          الوقت الذي / حينما / حين (مع الاسم الدال على الزمن)

Rule
١. نستخدم (when) للإشارة إلى توقيت / يوم / سنة / فترة / فترة. وهي من النّاصبة اللغوية ظرف وصل وليست ضمير وصل

e.g. - Friday is the day when most of us have a holiday.

When to use
٢. نستخدم (when) بعد الأسماء التي تعبّر عن الزمن، خاصة:

day, time, year, period, moment, occasion, ...

e.g. - There was a time when people did not know much about mental health.

Prepositions
٣. لا نستخدم حرف جر تشير لنفس الاسم الزمني بعد أو قبل (when) لكن في حالة الضرورة نستبدل (when) بـ
(which) مع حرف جر كالتالي:

when ——→ = prep. حرف جر + which ...
or ——→ = which/that + ... prep. حرف جر

e.g. - The day when I receive guests is always busy. (which)
= The day on which I receive guests is always busy.
= The day which/that I receive guests on is always busy.

٤. تذكّر أن حرف الجر المُستخدم مع (which) يتوقف على الاسم الزمني نفسه:

e.g. - I remember the day when we first met.
= I remember the day on which we first met.

e.g. - I remember the time when we first met.
= I remember the time at which we first met.

e.g. - I remember the evening when we first met.
= I remember the evening in which we first met.

Omission

٥. يمكن حذف (when) عندما لا يُؤثّر ذلك على المعنى:

e.g. - The day when I receive guests is always busy. (which)
= The day I receive guests is always busy.

Tricky point

١. لا نستخدم فعل بعد (when) مباشرة، وفي حالة وجود فعل نستخدم (which/that) بدلاً من (when):

Choose the correct answer.
1. August is the month ............ the weather is very hot.
a. when          b. which          c. that          d. b & c
2. August is the month ............ is very hot.
a. when          b. which          c. that          d. b & c

٢. نستخدم ضمير الوصل (which) وليس ظرف الوصل (when) عندما يكون الاسم الزمني مفعول لفعل غير الوصل. لاحظ المثال التالي:

e.g. - I will never forget the wonderful week when I spent with you. (✗)
- I will never forget the wonderful week which I spent with you. (✓)

التفسير: الاسم في هذا المثال (wonderful week) هو مفعول للفعل (spent) الموجود في عبارة الوصل.

- بالنسبة لهذه الفكرة، هناك طريقة سهلة للتأكد من الاختيار الصحيح في الامتحان:

اسأل نفسك: هل يمكن استبدال ضمير الوصل بأي من حروف الجر التالية:

in which, on which, at which

- إذا كانت الإجابة (نعم) استخدم (when)
- إذا كانت الإجابة (لا) استخدم (which)

Choose the correct answer.
1. Friday and Saturday are the days ............ I go to the countryside.
a. when          b. which          c. that          d. b & c
2. Friday and Saturday are the days ............ I wait for to go to the countryside.
a. when          b. which          c. that          d. b & c

2 where          ٢ المكان الذي / حيث / حيثُما (مع الاسم الدال على مكان)

Rule

١. نستخدم (where) للإشارة إلى مكان. وهي من الناصبة اللغوية ظرف وصل وليست ضمير وصل.

e.g. – I like my garden where I spend most of my free time.

٢. نستخدم (where) بعد الأسماء التي تعبّر عن مكان:

e.g. - There is a nearby hotel where you can stay for the night.

Prepositions

3. لا تُستخدم حرف جر تشرح لاسم المكان بعد أو قبل (where) لكن في حالة الضرورة نستبدل (where) بـ (which) مع حرف جر كالتالي:

where ——> = prep. حرف جر + which ...
or ——> = which/that + ... حرف جر prep.

e.g. - The office where I work is in the city centre. (which)
= The office which I work in is in the city centre.
= The office in which I work is in the city centre.

Omission

4. يمكن حذف (where) وإضافة حرف جر مناسب في المكان المناسب في عبارة الوصل:

e.g. - The room where I sleep is well-decorated.
= The room I sleep in is well-decorated.

Tricky point

1. لا نستخدم فعل بعد (where) مباشرةً، وعند وجود فعل نستخدم (which/that) بدلاً من (where):

Choose the correct answer.
1. Aswan is the city ............ I live.
a. where    b. which    c. that    d. b & c
2. Aswan is a city ............ is very hot in summer.
a. when    b. which    c. that    d. b & c

2. نستخدم ضمير الوصل (which) وليس ظرف الوصل (where) عندما يكون اسم المكان مفعول لفعل عبارة الوصل. لاحظ المثال التالي:

e.g. - This is the villa where my father built. (X)
- This is the villa which my father built. (✓)

التفسير: اسم المكان في هذا المثال (the villa) هو مفعول الفعل (built) الموجود في عبارة الوصل.
- بنسبة لهذه الفكرة، هناك طريقة سهلة للتأكد من الاختيار الصحيح في الامتحان:
اسأل نفسك: هل يمكن استبدال ضمير الوصل بأي من حروف الجر التالية:

in which, on which, at which, ...

- إذا كانت الإجابة (نعم) استخدم (where)
- إذا كانت الإجابة (لا) استخدم (which)

Choose the correct answer.
1. Assiut is the governorate ............ I was born.
a. where    b. which    c. that    d. b & c
2. Assiut is the governorate ............ I visit every year.
a. where    b. which    c. that    d. b & c

◆ الاستماع والقراءة | Listening & Reading

استخدم صلاح Pre-Assessment لإثبات فهم مهارات المنهج

Social Media and Self-Image

In today's fast-changing(1) world, social media plays a major role(2) in shaping(3) how teenagers(4) think, feel, and see themselves. While it can inspire(5) healthy habits and positive change, it can also create unrealistic expectations(6) and pressure(7).

Listening Text

Over the past decade(8), social media platforms(9) have transformed(10) from simple communication tools(11) into carefully chosen stages where individuals perform carefully constructed(12) versions of their lives. For teenagers, who are at a particularly sensitive(13) stage of identity formation(14), this change carries significant psychological(15) results. Research(16) published by the American Psychological Association in 2023 found that teenagers who spent more than three hours daily on image-based(17) platforms such as Instagram and TikTok were twice as likely to report symptoms(18) of low self-esteem(19) and body dissatisfaction(20) compared to those who used social media for less than one hour per day.

What makes social media especially powerful, and especially dangerous, is the mechanism(21) of social comparison(22). Unlike traditional media, where the gap(23) between ordinary people and celebrities is obvious, social media has hidden this gap completely. Teenagers are now comparing themselves not only to distant celebrities, but to classmates, acquaintances, and influencers(24) who present their lives as easily perfect.

Psychologists use the term highlight-reel effect(25) to describe this phenomenon: what appears online is a selective, filtered, and strategically timed part of reality, yet viewers instinctively treat it as a complete and accurate picture.

(2) دور رئيسي
(3) تشكيل

(6) توقعات غير واقعية

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Persuasive, Biased, Neutral Language & Hedging Words
لغة الإقناع، والتحيّز، والحياد، والحفظ في التعبير [تخفيف درجة التأكيد]

• What is Persuasive Language؟ ما المقصود بلغة الإقناع؟
- Persuasive language is language used to affect the reader's opinion, feelings, or actions.
لغة الإقناع هي اللغة التي تستخدم للتأثير في رأي القارئ أو مشاعره أو تصرفاته.
- Writers may use strong words to make their ideas sound powerful. They may also use careful words to sound balanced and fair.
قد يستخدم الكاتب كلمات قوية ليجعل أفكاره مؤثرة. وقد يستخدم الفاظ متحفظة ليبدو متوازنًا ومنصفًا.
لاحظ المثال التالي:

e.g. —→ Strong / Biased:
- Social media destroys teenagers' confidence.
—→ Neutral + Hedging:
- Some studies suggest that social media may affect some teenagers' confidence.

Explanation
- الجملة الأولى قوية جدًا لأنها تستخدم كلمة حادة وهي destroys
- أما الجملة الثانية فهي أكثر توازنًا لأنها تستخدم:
- Some studies suggest
- affect
- some teenagers

➨ Here, we focus on persuasive writing techniques such as:
وسوف نركز هنا على أدوات الكتابة الإقناعية مثل :

1. Rhetorical questions الأسئلة البلاغية
2. Intensifiers أدوات تقوية المعنى
3. Hedging words ألفاظ التحفظ
4. Absolute words الألفاظ القطعية
5. Biased language اللغة المتحيزة
6. Neutral language اللغة المحايدة

Persuasive Writing Techniques أدوات الكتابة الإقناعية

1 Rhetorical Questions الأسئلة البلاغية

Definition
A rhetorical question is a question that does not need a direct answer. It is used to make the reader or listener think.
السؤال البلاغي هو سؤال لا يحتاج إلى إجابة مباشرة. يُستخدم لجعل القارئ أو المستمع يفكر.

Examples
• Do we really need likes to feel valued?
• Can a perfect photo show the whole truth?
• Should teenagers compare themselves to filtered images?

Effect on reader
Rhetorical questions attract attention and make the topic feel personal.
تجذب الأسئلة البلاغية الانتباه وتجعل الموضوع يبدو قريبًا وشخصيًا.

2 Intensifiers أدوات التقوية / المكثّفات

Definition
Intensifiers are words that make an idea stronger.
أدوات التقوية هي كلمات تجعل المعنى أقوى.

Common intensifiers
- very جدًا - extremely للغاية
- really حقًا - significantly بشكل واضح
- completely كُلّيًا - utterly تمامًا
- absolutely بشكل مُطلق

Examples
• Social media can be extremely stressful.
• Many teenagers feel very pressured online.
• Filtered photos can significantly affect self-image.

Effect on reader
Intensifiers make the writer's point sound stronger and more emotional.
تجعل أدوات التقوية رأي الكاتب أقوى وأكثر تأثيرًا أو عاطفية.
Note ملاحظة
Using too many intensifiers can make writing sound exaggerated.
الإكثار من أدوات التقوية قد يجعل النصوب يبدو مبالغًا فيه.

3 Hedging words ألْفاظ التحفظ من التعبيــر (تحفــيف درجــة التأكيــد)

Definition
Hedging words make a statement sound careful, balanced, and less absolute.
ألفاظ التحوط تجعل الجملة أكثر حذرًا وتوازنًا وأقل قطعية.

Common Hedging Words
may, might, could, some, many, certain, suggest, appear to, seem

Examples
• Social media may affect teenagers' self-image.
• Some posts might create unrealistic expectations.
• Certain types of content could increase pressure.

on reader
Hedging makes writing sound fair, careful, and academic.
يجلع الحفظ في التعبير الكتابة تبدو منصفة وحذرة وأكاديمية.
⚠ Important Note ملحوظة مهمة
Hedging does not make the writer weak. It shows that the writer is careful and avoids exaggeration.
الحفظ في التعبير لا يعني أن الكاتب ضعيف. بل يدل على أن الكاتب حذر ويتجنب المبالغة.

4 Absolute Words الكلمات القاطعة

Definition
Absolute words express complete certainty. They leave little or no room for exceptions.
الكلمات القاطعة تعبر عن اليقين الكامل، ولا تترك مجالًا كبيرًا للاستثناءات.

absolute Words
always, never, everyone, all, no excuses, completely, every

Examples
• Social media always harms teenagers.
• Everyone compares themselves online.
• There are no excuses for unhealthy habits.

on reader
Absolute words can make writing sound powerful and confident, but they may also make it sound biased or exaggerated.
قد تجلع الكلمات القاطعة الكتابة قوية وواثقة، لكنها قد تجلعها أيضًا متحيزة أو مبالغًا فيها.

5 Biased Language اللغة المتحيزة

Definition
Biased language shows an unfair or one-sided opinion.
اللغة المتحيزة تُظهر رأيًا غير عادل أو من جانب واحد.

Examples
• Social media always has negative effects on teenagers.
• Online posts are completely unreliable.
• Every influencer presents an unrealistic picture of life.

on reader
⚠ Why is this biased? لماذا تُعد هذه اللغة متحيزة؟
These sentences make unfair generalizations. They do not allow for different situations or balanced opinions.
هذه الجممل تعمم بشكل غير عادل. فهي لا تترك مجالًا لاختلاف الحالات أو الآراء المتوازنة.

6 Neutral Language اللغة المحايدة

Definition
Neutral language is balanced, objective, and free from unfair judgment.
اللغة المحايدة هي لغة متوازنة وموضوعية وخالية من الحكم غير العادل.

Examples
• Some teenagers may be affected by social media.
• Certain online posts can create pressure.
• Some influencers present carefully selected versions of their lives.

Effect on reader
Neutral language makes writing sound fair, balanced, and more believable.
تجعل اللغة المحايدة الكتابة أكثر إصافًا وتوازنًا ومصداقية.

7 Biased language vs Neutral + Hedging
اللغة المتحيزة مقابل اللغة المحايدة مع التحفظ

Biased / Too Strong
متحيزة / قوية جدًا
Neutral + Hedging
محايدة مع تحفظ

Social media destroys teenagers' confidence.
Some studies suggest that social media may affect some teenagers' confidence.

All teenagers believe everything they read online.
Some teenagers may trust online content without checking it carefully.

Influencers are misleading.
Some influencers may present carefully selected versions of their lives.

Social media is completely harmful.
Certain types of social media content could have negative effects.

• How can we Rewrite Biased Sentences? كيف نعيد صياغة الجمل المتحيزة؟
To make a biased sentence more neutral: لجعل الجملة المتحيزة أكثر حيادًا:
1. Remove unfair generalizations. احذف التعميمات غير العادلة.
2. Replace emotional words with objective words. استبدل الكلمات العاطفية بكلمات موضوعية.
3. Add hedging words. أضف كلمات تحوط.
4. Make the sentence more balanced. اجعل الجملة أكثر توازنًا.

Summary

- Persuasive writing is not only about what the writer says. It is also about how the writer says it.
الكتابة الإقناعية لا تعتمد فقط على ما يقوله الكاتب، بل تعتمد أيضًا على الطريقة التي يقول بها ذلك.
- A good writer can use strong language to persuade, but can also use neutral and hedging language to sound fair and balanced.
الكاتب الجيد يستطيع استخدام لغة قوية للإقناع، لكنه يستخدم أيضًا لغة محايدة ومتحوطة ليبدو منصفًا ومتوازنًا.

◆ تدريبات Writing | Exercises On Writing

1 Choose the correct answer from a, b, c, or d:

1. Which persuasive technique is used in the sentence, "Why ignore healthy sleep when it supports both body and mind?"
a. Hedging                    b. Intensification
c. Rhetorical question        d. Absolute language

2. Which word acts as an intensifier in the sentence, "A balanced diet is extremely important for well-being"?
a. Extremely        b. Balanced        c. Important        d. Well-being

3. Which word makes the following claim sound cautious rather than completely certain? "Regular physical activity may improve a person's mood."
a. Regular        b. May        c. Improve        d. Mood

4. Which word expresses complete certainty in the sentence, "Skipping breakfast never affects everyone in the same way"?
a. Skipping        b. Same        c. Affects        d. Never

5. Which sentence uses the most neutral language?
a. Junk-food lovers make terrible choices about their health.
b. Everyone who eats processed food will become unhealthy.
c. Some processed foods may contain high levels of sugar, salt, or fat.
d. Processed food is absolutely the worst part of modern life.

6. A writer changes "Screen-based habits damage teenagers' concentration" to "Screen-based habits may affect teenagers' concentration." Why is may used?
a. To make the claim sound completely certain
b. To make the effect sound more dramatic
c. To make the claim more cautious and balanced
d. To turn the statement into a question

7. After a concert, a reviewer wrote that the singing was impressive but the sound quality was disappointing. The review was clearly evaluative. Which situation best illustrates "evaluative" writing?
a. A museum guide states that an exhibition contains forty paintings.
b. A film critic describes the acting as powerful but the ending as unconvincing.
c. A sports report gives the final score and the time of each goal.
d. A travel guide states when a historic building was constructed.

8. A food blogger described the meal as "excellent." In the review, excellent functions as an evaluative adjective. Which situation contains a similar type of adjective?
a. A guide describes a temple as "ancient."
b. A weather presenter describes the sky as "cloudy."
c. A shop assistant describes a table as "wooden."
d. A critic describes a documentary as "remarkable."

9. A nutrition writer avoided claiming that a new eating plan would work for everyone.
He wrote that it may help some people, using hedging.
Which situation best illustrates "hedging"?
a. A transport writer says a new road will definitely remove all congestion.
b. A product review claims that a device always produces perfect results.
c. A citizen says new bus lanes could reduce delays in some areas.
d. A newspaper states that one new rule will completely solve the problem.
10. In a report, Omar changed "The plan will reduce traffic" to "The plan might reduce traffic." Might is a hedging word.
Which situation contains a word serving the same function?
a. A health article says that regular walking may improve sleep quality.
b. A travel article says that the new railway will certainly transform every journey.
c. A product review says that the battery always lasts for two days.
d. A speaker says that the new system will definitely solve the problem.
11. A restaurant review said that the service was "extremely slow." In this sentence, extremely is an intensifier.
Which situation best illustrates the use of an "intensifier"?
a. A writer says that the view may be attractive in good weather.
b. A tourist describes the mountain view as incredibly beautiful.
c. A guide describes the mountain as beautiful in spring.
d. A visitor says that the view is not beautiful from every direction.
12. Two neighbors disagreed about the use of a shared parking space. In a community notice, I reported what each person said without praising or blaming either one, using neutral language.
Which situation best demonstrates "neutral language"?
a. A match report states the final score and describes the key events without praising or insulting either team.
b. A match report calls one side "fearless heroes" and the other "careless losers."
c. A match report says one team "deserved everything" while questioning the character of its opponent.
d. A match report describes the referee as "hopeless" while praising every decision made for the home team.
13. During a talk about wasting water, Mariam asked, "Can we afford to waste every drop?" and continued speaking without pausing. She had used a rhetorical question.
Which situation best illustrates a "rhetorical question"?
a. A traveller asks, "When does the next bus arrive?" and checks the answer.
b. A customer asks, "Does this phone come with a charger?" before buying it.
c. A doctor asks, "Did you take your medicine this morning?" to obtain information.
d. A coach asks, "How can we expect to win if we never train?" and immediately continues his talk to the team.
14. A writer wants to make this statement more balanced: "Processed food always causes poor health." Which revision best achieves this purpose?
a. Processed food definitely causes poor health.
b. Processed food causes extremely poor health.
c. A high intake of some processed foods may contribute to health problems.
d. Processed food never supports a healthy lifestyle.

15. Read the sentence: "Getting enough sleep is extremely important for physical recovery." What is the main effect of extremely?
a. It makes the statement less certain.
b. It strengthens the importance expressed by the adjective.
c. It makes the statement more objective.
d. It changes the statement into a generalisation.
16. A writer wants to replace the biased sentence, "Sedentary teenagers simply do not care about their health," with neutral language. Which revision is best?
a. Sedentary teenagers are clearly careless about their health.
b. All sedentary teenagers ignore the importance of good health.
c. Sedentary teenagers definitely make unhealthy choices.
d. Some teenagers may have sedentary habits for a variety of reasons.
17. A writer begins an article on well-being with: "Do we really give sleep the attention it deserves?" What is the main purpose of this rhetorical question?
a. To attract readers' attention
b. To prove that all readers have poor sleep habits
c. To make the writer's claim less certain
d. To ask readers to provide a factual answer
18. A student wants to persuade readers that sleep deserves more attention without exaggerating the claim. Which sentence is most effective?
a. Sleep is always the most important part of everyone's health.
b. People who neglect sleep completely destroy their well-being.
c. Regular, sufficient sleep may play a significant role in supporting well-being.
d. Sleep is extremely, absolutely, and completely essential for every healthy person.
19. A writer originally writes: "Mindfulness is absolutely amazing and completely solves everyone's stress." Which revision would make the claim more credible while preserving its positive message?
a. Mindfulness always removes all stress from people's lives.
b. Mindfulness may help some people manage stress more effectively.
c. Mindfulness is extremely powerful because it completely ends stress.
d. Everyone should practise mindfulness because it never fails.
20. Which sentence uses both hedging and an intensifier appropriately?
a. Regular activity may have a very positive effect on one's mood.
b. Physical activity might completely cure every mental-health problem.
c. Physical activity always improves everyone's mental health.
d. Physical activity definitely creates possibly better well-being.

2 Write a paragraph of about (150–180) words on the following topic:
"Why are teenagers more affected by social media than others?"
..........
..........

2 Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

9. Hala recorded how many minutes she walked each day and compared the totals at the end of the month. Her progress was measurable.
Which situation best illustrates the meaning of the word "measurable"?
a. A man feels that his health has improved but keeps no record of any changes.
b. A woman hopes her new routine will help her feel better in the future.
c. A shop owner compares this month's sales figures with last month's figures.
d. A football fan believes his team is playing better because the matches seem more exciting.

10. After Ahmed became worried about his eating habits, he visited a nutritionist, who examined what he usually ate and suggested healthier choices. The advice helped him improve his daily diet.
Which situation demonstrates the role of a "nutritionist"?
a. A coach shows a runner how to improve his speed before an important race.
b. A doctor treats a patient who has broken his arm in a fall.
c. A counsellor helps a teenager deal with feelings of anxiety and loneliness.
d. A specialist advises a family how to choose balanced meals that meet their dietary needs.

11. Nour compared plain oats with a colorful breakfast cereal containing added sugar, flavoring, and several other ingredients. She chose the oats because she wanted to eat less processed food.
Which situation best illustrates "processed food"?
a. A family slices fresh watermelon and serves it after lunch.
b. A man boils an egg and eats it with fresh tomatoes and flavors.
c. A woman washes some grapes and puts them in a bowl for her children.
d. A boy eats frozen chicken nuggets made from chicken mixed with coating, salt, and flavors.

12. During the holiday, Youssef spent hours gaming, traveled even short distances by car, and rarely did anything physically active. His routine had become sedentary.
In which situation is someone leading a similarly "sedentary" lifestyle?
a. During her long holiday, Salma spends most days drawing at a table, ordering what she needs online, and rarely going outside.
b. During her long holiday, Dina reads for several hours but walks every morning and goes shopping on foot.
c. During her long holiday, Huda rests for two days after a hiking trip and then returns to her usual active routine.
d. During her long holiday, Mona watches films in the evening after spending each afternoon swimming or cycling.

13. Everyone in Adel's family paid close attention to food and exercise, but they regularly cut their sleeping time when they became busy. After several weeks of tiredness and poor concentration, they realized that sleep had been underestimated.
Which situation best reflects the meaning of "underestimated"?
a. A family forgets to bring water on a journey because nobody puts it on the packing list.
b. Football fans expect a new player to score in every match before they have even seen him play.
c. Organizers spend most of their money on decorations, then discover from visitors' complaints that comfortable seating mattered far more than they had expected.
d. Travellers check the distance, weather, and cost carefully before deciding whether a trip will be difficult.

¢ Choose the correct answer froma,b,cord:

1. Sara recorded her walking distance each week, so the improvement in her fitness was
measurable. Based on the sentence above, what does ““measurable” mean?

a. Able to be tracked or counted
c. Likely to change without warning

b. Difficult to notice or compare
d. Impossible to judge with evidence

2.Before going to bed, Karim practises mindfulness by focusing quietly on his breathing instead
of worrying about tomorrow. Which definition describes the meaning of “mindfulness”?
a. Planning carefully for future events
b. Paying careful attention to the present moment
c. Remembering past experiences in detail
d. Concentrating on several activities at once

3. [had been feeling tired, so a nutritionist looked carefully at what I usually ate and
suggested adding more vegetables, drinking more water, and reducing sugary snacks.
Based on the sentence above, what does “nutritionist” mean?

a. A health specialist who advises people about suitable food and eating habits
b. A health specialist who treats injuries to muscles, bones, and joints

c. A fitness specialist who plans exercise routines to improve physical strength
d. A mental-health specialist who helps people deal with emotional difficulties

4. We began eating less processed food, replacing packaged snacks and ready-made meals
with fresher choices. Which meaning best matches “processed food” as used here?
a. Food grown without using any machines
b. Food prepared and eaten immediately at home
c. Food chosen mainly for its nutritional value
d. Food changed from its natural state before being sold

floor, and usually went everywhere by car. His routine had become sedentary.
What does “sedentary” mean in this context?
a. Involving frequent changes from one activity to another
b. Involving strict control over daily eating habits
c. Involving very little physical movement
d. Involving regular participation in energetic outdoor activities

diet and exercise than to getting enough rest. What does “underestimated” mean in this

a. Considered more important than it really is
b. Completely ignored or never noticed
c. Valued less than it deserves

d. Carefully examined before being judged

person’s well-being. Based on the sentence above, what does “well-being” mean?
a. The ability to succeed in difficult tasks
b.A state of being healthy, comfortable, and happy
c. The habit of following a strict daily routine
d.A feeling of excitement caused by achievement

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

measurable (adj) قابل للقياس/ملحوظ

✓ Goes with:
- measurable reduction انخفاض ملحوظ
- measurable progress تقدم ملحوظ
- measurable results نتائج ملموسة

Word Family

Word Example
measure (v) يقيس Scientists measure stress levels.
measurement (n) قياس The measurement showed improvement.
measurable (adj) قابل للقياس The results were measurable.

✗ Don't mix
- measurable = can be counted or tracked
- noticeable = can be seen or felt easily

mindfulness (n) اليقطة الذهنية

✓ Goes with:
- mindfulness practice تدريبات اليقظة الذهنية
- improve mindfulness يُحسّن اليقظة الذهنية

Word Family

Word Example
mind (n) عقل / ذهن A calm mind helps learning.
mindful (adj) واعٍ / منتبه Be mindful of your habits.
mindfulness (n) يقظة ذهنية Mindfulness can reduce stress.

✗ Don't mix
- mindfulness = present-moment attention
- concentration = focusing on a task

nutritionist (n)    أخصائي تغذية

Goes with:
- consult a nutritionist        يستشير أخصائي تغذية
- advice from a nutritionist        نصيحة من أخصائي تغذية

Word Family

Word                                Example
nutrition (n)            تغذية    Good nutrition supports health.
nutritional (adj)        غذائي    Fruits have nutritional value.
nutritionist (n)    أخصائي تغذية    A nutritionist advises teenagers.

Don't mix
- nutritionist = food and diet expert
- psychologist = mental health expert

processed food (n)    طعام مصنّع

Goes with:
- avoid processed food        يتجنب الطعام المصنّع
- intake of processed food        مقدار التناول من الطعام المصنّع

Word Family

Word                                Example
process (v)            يعالج    Factories process food.
processed (adj)        مُصنّع    Processed food may contain sugar.
processing (n)        معالجة    Food processing can add chemicals.

Don't mix
- processed food = changed from natural state
- junk food = unhealthy food, often high in fat, sugar, or salt

sedentary (adj)    قليل الحركة / خامل

Goes with:
- sedentary lifestyle        نمط حياة يُسيء بالخمول
- sedentary screen-based habits    عادات الخمول المتصلة بالشاشات

Word Family

Word                                Example
sedentary (adj)        قليل الحركة    Many teenagers have sedentary habits.
sedentariness (n)        قلة الحركة    Sedentariness can harm health.

Don't mix
- sedentary = involving little movement
- lazy = unwilling to work or move

underestimated (adj) غير مُقدَّر كفاية

Goes with:
• seriously underestimated غير مُقدَّر بشدة
• underestimated factor عامل غير مُقدَّر بما يكفي

Word Family

Word | Example
underestimate (v) يقلل من تقدير | People underestimate sleep.
underestimated (adj) غير مقدَّر كفاية | Sleep is often underestimated.
underestimation (n) تقليل التقدير | The underestimation of sleep is harmful.

Don't mix
- underestimated = not valued enough
- ignored = not noticed or considered at all

well-being (n) الرفاهية / العافية

Goes with:
• overall well-being الرفاهية العامة
• support well-being يعزز السلامة النفسية والجسدية

Word Family

Word | Example
well (adj/adv) جيد / بشكل جيد | Teenagers need to sleep well.
well-being (n) رفاهية / عافية | Exercise supports well-being.
wellness (n) عافية | Wellness programs teach healthy habits.

Don't mix
- well-being = overall health and happiness
- happiness = feeling pleased or satisfied

◆ تدريبات المفردات | Exercises on Vocabulary

1 Collocations, Expressions, Idioms & Prepositions

1. Choose the correct answer from a, b, c or d :
1. After twenty years in the city, the family moved .......... Cairo to a smaller town.
a. with b. from c. to d. towards
2. In your paragraph, support your opinion .......... one clear example.
a. of b. to c. with d. from

3. Mona coped .......... the long journey by taking short breaks.
a. with b. of c. to d. from
4. Please, limit your answer .......... three sentences.
a. with b. from c. to d. of
5. The hall quickly filled .......... excited fans before the match began.
a. in b. from c. of d. with
6. The meal consists .......... rice, vegetables, and grilled chicken.
a. with b. of c. from d. to
7. Too much screen time before bed can lead .......... poor sleep.
a. with b. from c. in d. to
8. We arrived early and set .......... our tent before it got dark.
a. up b. with c. from d. to
9. It's important to .......... our intake of processed food to stay healthy.
a. support b. boost c. fill d. limit

◆ القواعد اللغوية | Grammar Focus

Participial Phrases

Participial Phrases — العبارات التي تبدأ باسم الفاعل واسم المفعول

• لاحظ الجملة التالية:
- When she looked in the mirror, she didn't like her new blouse.
→ Looking in the mirror, she didn't like her new blouse.

- الجملة مبنية للمعلوم. — تم حذف الرابط (when)
- تم حذف الفاعل (she) — تم البدء بصيغة (V-ing)

- يسمى هذا النوع من الصيغ: عبارة اسم الفاعل

• لاحظ أيضا الجملة التالية:
- After the thief was arrested by the police, he was taken to prison.
→ Arrested by the police, the thief was taken to prison.

- الجملة مبنية للمجهول — تم حذف الرابط (After)
- تم حذف الفاعل (The thief) — تم البدء بصيغة التصريف الثالث (P.P.)

- يسمى هذا النوع من الصيغ: عبارة اسم المفعول
• وفيما يلي شرح مبسط لعبارات اسم الفاعل واسم المفعول:

1 Present Participial Phrases → (V-ing) — عبارات اسم الفاعل

تبدأ هذه العبارات بـ (V-ing)، وتستخدم في حالة المبني للمعلوم، ويمكن أن تستخدم بدلا من الروابط التالية:

1 While / As + active clause مبني للمعلوم → = V-ing .
- While I was walking in the street, I lost my keys.
→ Walking in the street, I lost my keys.

2 When + active clause مبني للمعلوم → = V-ing .
- When I opened the door of the room, I realised that Ahmed was asleep.
→ Opening the door of the room, I realised that Ahmed was asleep.

3 After / As soon as / Once + active clause مبني للمعلوم → = V-ing .
- As soon as Sama arrives home, she takes a rest.
→ Arriving home, Sama takes a rest.

4 Because + active clause مبني للمعلوم → = V-ing .
- Because I was very ill, I didn't go to school.
→ Being very ill, I didn't go to school.

5 By + V-ing / By the use of + noun . → = V-ing .
- By wearing a lot of clothes, I kept warm in the cold weather.
→ Wearing a lot of clothes, I kept warm in the cold weather.

6 who / which / that + active clause مبني للمعلوم ← = V-ing .
- The man who is wearing a black jacket is my uncle.
→ The man wearing a black jacket is my uncle.
- The factory which produces a lot of pollution must be closed.
→ The factory producing a lot of pollution must be closed.
في حالة النفي نضع (not) قبل (V-ing) .
- Because I didn't arrive in time, I had to apologize to the manager.
→ Not arriving in time, I had to apologize to the manager.
NOTE انتبه!
لا بد أن يكون الفاعل واحذا في الجملتين حتى يمكن استخدام العبارات التي تبدأ بصيغة اسم الفاعل (V-ing).
- As mum was waiting for us to arrive, she fell asleep in the chair.
→ Waiting for us to arrive, mum fell asleep in the chair.

2 Past Participial Phrases → (p.p.)        عبارات اسم المفعول

تبدأ هذه العبارات بـ (p.p.)، وتستخدم في حالة المبني للمجهول، ويمكن أن تستخدم بدلاً من الروابط التالية:

1 When / As + passive clause مبني للمجهول ← p.p.
- When my car was repaired by the mechanic, it worked better.
→ Repaired by the mechanic, my car worked better.

2 After / As soon as / Once + passive clause مبني للمجهول ← p.p.
- After lunch was prepared by the cook, it was served to the guests.
→ Prepared by the cook, lunch was served to the guests.

3 Because + passive clause مبني للمجهول ← p.p.
- Because Nada is supported by her family, she feels confident.
→ Supported by her family, Nada feels confident.

في حالة النفي نضع (not) قبل (p.p.) .
- Because he was not punished for his mistakes, he repeated them.
→ Not punished for his mistakes, he repeated them.

NOTE انتبه!
لا بد أن يعود اسم المفعول على نفس الاسم الذي يصبح فاعلاً في الجملة الرئيسية حتى يمكن استخدام العبارات التي تبدأ بصيغة اسم المفعول (p.p.).

- When food is cooked well, it is both healthy and delicious.
→ Cooked well, food is both healthy and delicious.

3 Perfect Particial Phrases → (Having +p.p.)

الصيغة التامة من عبارات اسم الفاعل

1 After / As soon as / Once / When / The moment + past active clause مبني للمعلوم في الماضي
= Having + p.p.
- After I had done my homework, I went to bed.
→ Having done my homework, I went to bed.
- As soon as Ayman finished the report, he took a rest.
→ Having finished the report, Ayman took a rest.

2 After / As soon as / Once / When / The moment + past passive clause
مبني للمجهول في الماضي
= Having + been + p.p.
- After my flat had been decorated, it looked fantastic.
→ Having been decorated, my flat looked fantastic.

NOTE انتبه!
لا بد أن يعود التركيب (Having been + p.p.) على نفس الاسم الذي يصبح فاعلًا في الجملة الرئيسية.
- After the car had been checked by the mechanic, it used less petrol.
→ Having been checked by the mechanic, the car used less petrol.

◆ الاستماع والقراءة | Listening & Reading

استخدم صحيح
Pre-Assessment
لانقان وقهمر
مهارات المنهج

Exploring Modern Lifestyles

Modern life moves faster than ever. Technology⁽¹⁾ saves us time,
yet we fill that time with more tasks, screens, and pressure⁽²⁾. How we
choose to live – our daily habits⁽³⁾, our diet⁽⁴⁾, and our sleep – shapes not
only our physical health⁽⁵⁾, but also our identity⁽⁶⁾ and sense of self⁽⁷⁾.

(1) التكنولوجيا
(2) ضغط
(3) عادات
(4) نظام غذائي
(5) صحة بدنية
(6) هوية
(7) إحساس بالذات
(8) معتدل
(9) سريع
(10) اندورفين
(11) قلق
(12) ثقة بالنفس
(13) تركيز
(14) قليل الحركة
(15) أخصائيون تغذية
(16) أطعمة مصنعة
(17) تعافي / استشفاء

Reading Text

1. Exercise and Physical Activity

Health experts regularly recommend that teenagers do at least
60 minutes of moderate⁽⁸⁾-to-hard physical activity every day. This
doesn't necessarily mean competitive sport. Activities such as cycling
to school, swimming, or even a brisk⁽⁹⁾ walk, all count towards the
daily target. What makes exercise particularly powerful is its effect
on the brain: physical activity causes the release of endorphins⁽¹⁰⁾,
chemicals that reduce feelings of stress and anxiety⁽¹¹⁾ at the same
time as boosting mood and self-confidence⁽¹²⁾. Furthermore, teenagers
who exercise regularly tend to sleep more deeply
and report higher levels of concentration⁽¹³⁾ during
school hours. Despite these well-documented
benefits, a landmark WHO-led study found that
more than 80% of teenagers worldwide fail to meet
the recommended daily activity levels, largely due
to sedentary⁽¹⁴⁾ screen-based habits.

2. Nutrition, Sleep, and Recovery

A balanced diet is widely seen as the foundation
of physical and mental well-being. Nutritionists⁽¹⁵⁾
advise that teenagers consume a varied range of
fruits, vegetables, whole grains, lean proteins,
and healthy fats, while limiting their intake of
processed foods⁽¹⁶⁾, too much sugar, and high-
caffeine drinks. Poor nutrition has been directly
linked to fatigue, difficulty concentrating, and
mood changes, all of which affect academic
performance. Sleep is equally important, but it
is often underestimated . Teenagers require from eight to ten hours of sleep per night
to support brain development, emotional control, and physical recovery⁽¹⁷⁾. Research
published in the journal Sleep Medicine found that teenagers who slept fewer than seven
hours per night were more likely to report symptoms of anxiety and depression. Setting
up a regular bedtime routine, such as avoiding screens for one hour before sleeping, has
been shown to improve sleep quality.

3. Mindfulness, Social Connection, and Mindful Consumption

(18) نقطة ذهنية
(19) قابلة للقياس
(20) مرونة

In recent years, mindfulness(18) has moved from specialist wellness programs into common education. Mindfulness involves paying deliberate attention to the present moment, and can take many forms: meditation, slow breathing exercises, yoga, or simply sitting quietly without a device. Studies suggest that even ten minutes of daily mindfulness practice leads to measurable(19) reductions in stress hormones over a period of four to six weeks. Alongside mindfulness, social connection plays a very important role in emotional health. Spending quality time with friends and family — face to face rather than through screens — has been shown to reduce loneliness and build the emotional resilience(20) needed to cope with life's pressures. Finally, many psychologists advise us to be conscious not only of what we eat, but also of the media, products, and information we absorb daily.

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Analytical Paragraph
الفقرة الإنشائية التحليلية

What is an analytical paragraph
An analytical paragraph presents a clear claim, explains the reason behind it, supports it with evidence, and shows how the evidence proves the claim.
تعرض الفقرة الإنشائية التحليلية رأياً واضحاً، وتشرح سببه، وتدعمه بدليل، ثم توضح كيف يبرهن الدليل على الرأي.

An analytical paragraph is used to answer a question, explain a viewpoint, support an opinion, and show understanding through evidence and explanation.
وُتستخدم الفقرة الإنشائية التحليلية للإجابة عن سؤال وتشرح وجهة نظر ودعم رأي وإظهار الفهم من خلال الدليل والتشرح.

Main Parts of an Analytical Paragraph
الأجزاء الرئيسية للفقرة التحليلية

1 Topic Sentence / Claim     الأسئلة البدئية

- Start with a clear opinion that answers the question.
ابدأ برأي واضح يجيب عن السؤال.

Example
Physical activity is more influential than diet in improving overall well-being.

2 Reason     السبب

Explain why you believe your claim is well supported.
اشرح لماذا تعتقد أن رأيك مدعوم جيداً بالأدلة.

Example
This is because exercise directly affects both physical health and mental well-being.

3 Evidence     الدليل

Support your idea using information from the text, examples, or facts.
ادعم فكرتك بمعلومات من النص أو أمثلة أو حقائق.

Example
According to the text, physical activity releases endorphins which reduce stress and improve mood.

4 Explanation الشرح

Explain how your evidence supports your claim.

اشرح كيف يدعم دليلك رأيك.

Example
This shows that exercise not only improves physical health but also has a strong impact on mental well-being.

5 Concluding Sentence الجملة الختامية

End by reinforcing your main idea.

اختم بتعزيز فكرتك الرئيسية.

Example
Therefore, physical activity plays a more significant role than diet in shaping overall well-being.

Important Expressions
تعبيرات مهمة للفقرة التحليلية

A: Stating a Clear Claim تحديد رأى واضح
• It can be argued that ... • In my view, ...
• The most important factor is ... • ... plays a major role in ...

B: Giving a Clear Reason إعطاء مبرر واضح
• This is because ... • The reason is that ...
• This can be explained by ... • One reason for this is ...

C: Giving Evidence إعطاء دليل
• According to the text, ... • The article states that ...
• The text shows that ... • For example, ...

D: Explaining Evidence تفسير الدليل
• This shows that ... • This proves that ...
• This means that ... • As a result, ...

E: Concluding (restating and strengthening your idea) الفقرة الختامية (تعيد صياغة وتقوية فكرتك)
• Therefore, ... • For this reason, ...
• Overall, ... • This clearly shows that ...

◆ تدريبات Writing | Exercises On Writing

1 Choose the correct answer from a, b, c, or d:

1. A paragraph begins with the claim, "Physical activity plays a major role in improving teenagers' well-being." Which sentence best gives a reason for this claim?
a. According to the text, teenagers should exercise for at least 60 minutes each day.
b. This shows that exercise should be part of a healthy lifestyle.
c. Therefore, physical activity deserves an important place in daily life.
d. This is because regular exercise benefits both physical and mental health.

2. Which sentence is the clearest example of giving evidence in an analytical paragraph?
a. According to the text, physical activity releases endorphins which reduce stress and improve mood.
b. The most important factor in well-being is regular physical activity.
c. This is because exercise can improve how teenagers feel.
d. This shows that exercise affects more than physical fitness.

3. A writer has just presented evidence that exercise reduces stress and improves mood. Which sentence best explains the evidence?
a. For example, many teenagers enjoy walking, cycling, or swimming.
b. The reason is that physical activity should be taken seriously.
c. This shows that exercise can support emotional as well as physical well-being.
d. In conclusion, all teenagers should exercise every day.

4. Which sentence would work best as the concluding sentence of an analytical paragraph arguing that physical activity strongly affects well-being?
a. According to the text, exercise releases endorphins.
b. Therefore, physical activity plays a significant role in supporting overall well-being.
c. The text also explains that some teenagers have sedentary habits.
d. This is because exercise can affect both body and mind.

5. A writer claims, "A regular sleep routine is essential for teenagers' well-being." Which sentence best develops the claim with a clear reason?
a. This is because sufficient sleep supports the brain, emotions, and physical recovery.
b. The text states that teenagers need eight to ten hours of sleep.
c. Therefore, teenagers should never stay awake late.
d. This shows that sleep is mentioned several times in discussions of health.

6. Which sentence would work best as the topic sentence / claim of an analytical paragraph answering the question, "Which habit has the greatest effect on teenagers' well-being?"
a. According to the text, teenagers need eight to ten hours of sleep each night.
b. In my view, getting enough sleep plays the most important role in teenagers' overall well-being.
c. This is because sleep supports brain development and physical recovery.
d. Therefore, teenagers should give healthy sleep habits greater attention.

7. Which sentence provides the strongest evidence for the claim that mindfulness can help reduce stress?
a. Mindfulness is becoming more common in modern life.
b. In my view, mindfulness is one of the best habits for everyone.
c. This means that people should always make time for mindfulness.
d. The text shows that even ten minutes of daily practice can lead to measurable reductions in stress hormones.
8. A writer makes this claim:
"Physical activity can support teenagers' emotional well-being."
Which piece of evidence most directly supports this specific claim?
a. According to the text, teenagers should complete at least sixty minutes of physical activity each day.
b. The text states that more than 80% of teenagers fail to meet recommended activity levels.
c. According to the text, physical activity releases endorphins that reduce stress and anxiety while boosting mood and self-confidence.
d. The text explains that cycling, swimming, competitive sport, and fast walking all count towards the daily activity target.
9. A writer makes this claim:
"Sufficient sleep supports several areas of teenagers' well-being."
The writer then gives this evidence:
"According to the text, teenagers need eight to ten hours of sleep to support brain development, emotional control, and physical recovery."
Which sentence best explains how this evidence supports the claim?
a. This shows that teenagers should simply spend more time in bed.
b. This means that sufficient sleep supports mental, emotional, and physical aspects of well-being.
c. This proves that sleep is more important than exercise and nutrition.
d. As a result, teenagers often have busy routines and many responsibilities.
10. A paragraph argues that regular physical activity supports several areas of well-being. It gives evidence that exercise reduces stress, improves mood, supports deeper sleep, and improves concentration.
Which concluding sentence best reinforces the main idea without introducing an unsupported new claim?
a. Therefore, regular exercise should replace sleep and healthy eating in every teenager's routine.
b. Overall, competitive sport is the only form of activity that can improve teenagers' well-being.
c. For this reason, teenagers who exercise regularly will never suffer from stress or poor concentration.
d. Therefore, regular physical activity can support several important areas of teenagers' overall well-being.

• conclude with a clear judgment or recommendation

structural barrier (n) عائق بنيوي - حاجز هيكلي

◆ المفردات الرئيسية | Key Vocabulary
1. A person with strong .......... can recover quickly after difficulties.
a. pressure
b. facility
c. resilience
d. policy
