a. nudge
b. outcome
c. brand
d. journal

a. affordable
b. internal
c. accessible
d. disempowering
4. Lack of safe parks can be a .......... to healthy living.
a. responsibility
b. preference
c. structural barrier
d. brand
5. Someone with an internal .......... believes effort can change results.
a. campaign
b. measure
c. force
d. locus of control
6. Students need .......... to make choices about their own learning.
a. barrier
b. agency
c. campaign
d. restriction
7. Students with high .......... believe they can succeed.
a. community
b. self-efficacy
c. adolescent
d. background
2 Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions
8. "When her family redecorated their flat, Laila chose how to arrange her own room and which belongings to keep. Having this agency made her feel more involved in the change." Which situation best illustrates "agency"?
a. Before joining a sports center, Hany compares the available activities and chooses the program that suits his own goals.
b. Hany is placed in a program chosen entirely by someone else and is not offered another option.
c. Hany is given a fixed program and told that members are not allowed to change any part of it.
d. Hany is moved to a different program without being consulted about the change.
9. At home, Tarek's older brother took over even simple tasks that Tarek could manage himself and often answered questions for him. Over time, Tarek found this disempowering and became less willing to act on his own.
Which situation best reflects the meaning of "disempowering"?
a. A coach criticises a player's weak performance but asks him to suggest how he could improve.
b. A coach sets strict safety rules but allows players to make decisions during the match.
c. A coach refuses to trust his players with even simple decisions and constantly takes control whenever they try to act for themselves.
d. A coach replaces the team captain after repeated lateness but explains the reason clearly to everyone.
10. After missing the bus twice, Taha stopped blaming traffic and began leaving home earlier. His response showed an internal locus of control because he focused on what he could change himself.

Which situation reflects the same kind of "locus of control"?
a. A shop owner says poor sales are entirely a matter of luck and sees no reason to change anything.
b. A footballer believes every poor result is caused by referees and never examines his own performance.
c. A traveler says delays are beyond anyone's control, so preparation makes no difference.
d. A runner studies what went wrong in his preparation and adjusts his routine before the next race.
11. A supermarket placed fruit near the checkout, where customers would notice it easily, while sweets remained available on another shelf. Fruit sales increased after this nudge was introduced.
Which situation best illustrates a "nudge"?
a. A phone blocks all social-media apps after one hour and does not allow the user to reopen them that day.
b. A phone displays a gentle "Time for a break?" message after an hour, but the user can dismiss it and continue.
c. A phone gives users shopping points whenever they spend less than thirty minutes online.
d. A phone shows users a detailed weekly record of their screen time without suggesting any action.
12. Huda's first handmade products did not sell well, but she adjusted her plans and continued working instead of abandoning the idea. Her resilience helped her deal with the disappointment.
Which situation is the clearest example of "resilience"?
a. After moving to a new town, Salma keeps comparing everything with her old home and refuses all invitations to go out.
b. After moving to a new town, Salma waits for her family to solve every difficulty she faces there.
c. After moving to a new town, Salma pretends the change does not affect her but stops doing the activities she once enjoyed.
d. After moving to a new town, Salma gradually builds a new routine, joins a local art group, and begins adjusting to her new surroundings.
13. After successfully preparing several meals with her aunt, Sama offered to cook dinner by herself for the first time. Her growing self-efficacy influenced her decision to take on the task.
Which situation best demonstrates "self-efficacy"?
a. After successfully repairing two bicycle punctures with help before, Ahmed starts fixing his own flat tyre when the problem happens again.
b. Ahmed buys the most expensive repair tools because his friends tell him that good equipment guarantees success.
c. Ahmed tells everyone he is the best bicycle mechanic although he has never repaired a bicycle.
d. Ahmed refuses to touch the bicycle unless his brother stands beside him and tells him every step.

14. A public service required all applications to be completed online, although some
residents had no reliable internet access. This created a structural barrier for people
who could not use the system easily.
Which situation best illustrates a "structural barrier"?
a. A visitor cannot enter a community centre because he forgot to bring the membership
card required that day.
b. A visitor decides to leave a community centre because the entrance is crowded and
she does not want to wait.
c. A community centre can be entered only by stairs, preventing wheelchair users from
reaching it independently.
d. A visitor misses an activity at a community center because he misunderstood its
starting time.

III  DEFINITIONS, SYNONYMS & ANTONYMS

تنبيه  هذا الجزء هام جدًا سواء على مستوى تعلُّم اللغة أو التميز في الامتحان.

1 | Definitions التعريفات

Word | Definition
agency (n)
قدرة على الاختيار والتصرف | the power to make choices and take independent action
disempowering (adj)
مضعِف للقدرة - مفقِد للسيطرة | making someone feel less capable or in control
locus of control (n)
موضع التحكم - مركز التحكم | a person's belief about whether they control their own life
nudge (n)
دفعَة لطيفة - توجيه غير مباشر | a gentle push toward a certain behaviour without force
resilience (n)
مرونة - قدرة على التعافي | the ability to recover and adapt in the face of difficulty
self-efficacy (n)
الكفاءة الذاتية | the belief in one's own capacity to succeed
structural barriers (n)
عوائق بنيوية - حواجز هيكلية | an obstacle in the environment that limits genuine choice

2 Synonyms المرادفات

Word | Synonym

agency (n)
قدرة على الاختيار والتصرف
power, capacity, control

disempowering (adj)
مضعف للقدرة - مفقد للسيطرة
discouraging, weakening, limiting

locus of control (n)
موضع التحكم - مركز التحكم
sense of control, internal control, personal control

nudge (n)
دفعة لطيفة - توجيه غير مباشر
gentle push, prompt, encouragement

resilience (n)
مرونة - قدرة على التعافي
toughness, adaptability, ability to recover

self-efficacy (n) الكفاءة الذاتية
self-belief, confidence, belief in ability

structural barrier (n)
عائق بنيوي - حاجز هيكلي
systemic obstacle, institutional barrier, built-in obstacle

3 Antonyms المتضادات

Word | Antonym

agency (n)
قدرة على الاختيار والتصرف
helplessness, powerlessness, dependence
عجز - انعدام قوة - تواصل

disempowering (adj)
مضعف للقدرة - مفقد للسيطرة
empowering, encouraging, enabling
ممكن - مشجع - مساعد على القدرة

locus of control (n)
موضع التحكم - مركز التحكم
loss of control, lack of control
فقدان السيطرة - غياب التحكم

nudge (n)
دفعة لطيفة - توجيه غير مباشر
force, pressure, compulsion
إجبار - ضغط - إكراه

resilience (n)
مرونة - قدرة على التعافي
weakness, fragility, vulnerability ضعف - هشاشة - قابلية للتأثر

self-efficacy (n)
الكفاءة الذاتية
self-doubt, insecurity, low confidence
شك في الذات - انعدام أمان - انخفاض الثقة

structural barrier (n)
عائق بنيوي - حاجز هيكلي
support, opportunity, access
دعم - فرصة - وصول

◆ تدريبات التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Exercises on Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

◆ التعريفات | Definitions

• Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

1. When he decided to change his study plans without asking anyone for permission first, he was exercising his own agency.
Based on the sentence above, what does "agency" mean?
a. a strong desire to please one's family members
b. the ability to make personal decisions and act independently
c. a formal permission granted by an authority figure
d. a business that manages travel or bookings on someone's behalf

2. After receiving some discouraging comments about her cooking, she found the feedback disempowering and stopped trying new recipes for a while.
What does "disempowering" mean in this context?
a. making someone feel proud and encouraged to try harder
b. causing someone to become more skilled through practice
c. making someone feel less capable and unwilling to try again
d. giving someone extra motivation to prove others wrong

3. Whenever Laila does badly on a test, she immediately reviews her study habits and plans to work differently next time, rather than blaming the exam questions or her teacher; her friends often say she has a strong internal locus of control.
Which definition describes the meaning of "locus of control"?
a. a person's belief about how much control they have over the outcomes in their life
b. a fixed opinion that cannot be changed regardless of new evidence
c. a habit of blaming others whenever something goes wrong
d. a skill that is developed only through years of professional training

4. The reminder notification on Hana's phone was a small nudge that encouraged her to drink more water throughout the day.
In the sentence above, "nudge" most nearly means ................ .
a. a strict rule that must be followed immediately
b. a loud alert meant to alarm someone into action
c. a gentle suggestion that guides someone toward an action without forcing it
d. a physical reminder device worn on a person's wrist

5. Even after losing the final match, the young swimmer showed great resilience by training harder the following season.
Which meaning best matches "resilience" as used in this sentence?
a. a strong desire to win every competition
b. the ability to recover quickly and keep going after a setback
c. a feeling of anger after an unfair loss
d. a natural talent that needs no practice

6. Because she had baked several difficult cakes expertly before, Mariam began preparing the elaborate wedding cake with strong self-efficacy, confident she could manage each tricky step.
Based on the sentence above, what does "self-efficacy" mean?
a. a fear of failing at new or difficult tasks

b. a belief in one's own ability to complete a task successfully
c. a natural talent that requires no effort or practice
d. a habit of asking for help before attempting something alone
7. Even though Omar wanted to open a small shop, the high registration fees acted as
a structural barrier that most young people in his village could not overcome.
What does "structural barrier" mean in this context?
a. an obstacle built into a system that limits people's real choices
b. a personal fear that stops someone from trying something new
c. a temporary shortage of products in local markets
d. a person's limited experience in running a business

1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :
1. "Students need agency to make independent choices." The underlined noun 'agency'
here is closest in meaning to ................. .
a. helplessness          b. power          c. dependence          d. weakness
2. "Constant blame can be disempowering for young people." The underlined adjective
'disempowering' here can be replaced by ................. .
a. empowering          b. encouraging          c. discouraging          d. enabling
3. "A reminder message can be a useful nudge toward better habits." The underlined noun
'nudge' here has a similar meaning to ................. .
a. force          b. pressure          c. compulsion          d. prompt
4. "Teenagers need resilience when they face difficulties." In this utterance, the
underlined noun 'resilience' is near in meaning to ................. .
a. adaptability          b. weakness          c. fragility          d. vulnerability
5. "'Self-efficacy' is to '.....' as 'resilience' is to 'adaptability'."
a. insecurity          b. confidence          c. self-doubt          d. low confidence

2 Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below.
A healthy community gives young people more control over their lives. Schools
can offer a gentle prompt toward better habits without using force. When students face
pressure, adaptability helps them recover and continue. Encouraging feedback also builds
confidence, so learners believe they can succeed. However, some families face
a systemic obstacle, such as expensive food or wrong habits. A negative message can be
discouraging, especially when it makes students feel unable to change.

Word / Phrase          Answer from the text
1. agency              → ..........
2. nudge               → ..........
3. resilience          → ..........
4. self-efficacy       → ..........
5. structural barrier  → ..........
6. disempowering       → ..........

◆ المتضادات | Antonyms

1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :
1. “Students need agency to make real choices.” The underlined noun ‘agency’ here is opposite in meaning to ..........
a. power          b. control          c. helplessness          d. capacity
2. “A negative message can be disempowering.” The underlined adjective ‘disempowering’ here is antonymous with .......... .
a. limiting          b. empowering          c. weakening          d. discouraging
3. “A school reminder can be a useful nudge.” The underlined noun ‘nudge’ here has an antonymous meaning to .......... .
a. prompt          b. encouragement          c. gentle push          d. force
4. “Young people need resilience during hard times.” In this utterance, the underlined noun ‘resilience’ is the opposite of .......... .
a. fragility          b. toughness          c. adaptability          d. ability to recover

2 Find the word or phrase in the text that are antonyms of the words below.
Some students feel powerlessness when adults make every choice for them. Others lose motivation when they hear an empowering message too late after years of blame. In some schools, strict pressure replaces gentle guidance. When young people face stress without support, weakness and fear may appear. Still, better facilities can create opportunity and help students make healthier choices.

Word / Phrase          Answer from the text
1. agency              → ..........
2. disempowering       → ..........
3. nudge               → ..........
4. resilience          → ..........
5. structural barrier  → ..........

◆ دراسة المفردات | Vocabulary Study

1 Verbal Collocations المتلازمات اللفظية

abandon     responsibility يتخل عن المسؤولية          maintain    healthy behaviours
يحافظ على سلوكيات صحية
attribute   choices to outside forces          prioritize   fresh vegetables
ينسب الاختيارات إلى قوى خارجية            يعطي الأولوية للخضروات الطازجة
build       confidence         يبني الثقة      remove      individual freedom يفيد الحرية الفردية
determine   outcomes           يحدد النتائج    reshape     the environment     يعيد تشكيل البيئة
exert       pressure           يمارس ضغطظ     restrict    genuine choice   يفيد الاختيار الحقيقي
make        healthy living possible            target      teenagers         يستهدف المراهقين
يجعل الحياة الصحية ممكنة

2 | Expressions & Idioms | التعبيرات والمصطلحات

a sense of purpose | شعور بوجود هدف
at the heart of | في صميم / في قلب
either-or choice | اختيار إما هذا أو ذاك
external forces | قوى خارجية
genuine choice | اختيار حقيقي
internal locus of control | مركز تحكم داخلي

public health policies | سياسات الصحة العامة
shared responsibility | مسؤولية مشتركة
socioeconomic background | خلفية اجتماعية اقتصادية
take control of | يسيطر على
taking ownership | تحمل المسؤولية
well-lived life | حياة جيدة

3 | Verb + Preposition | فعل + حرف جر

attribute .... to | يُسبب ... إلى

Whose Responsibility Is It? لس ees é

Modern Health مضعف للقدرة (12)

1. The Case for Personal Responsibility قوى خارجية (13)
Many health professionals and philosophers argue that personal مرونة (14)
responsibility lies at the heart of a well-lived life. From this علم الاجتماع )13(

ٍ perspective, every individual possesses the agency”
إٍْ — the capacity to choose and act, and it is through
ْ the exercise of this agency that identity is formed.
Choosing to exercise, to eat nutritiously, to sleep
well, and to manage stress are not simply habits; they
are expressions of who we are and what we value.
Psychologists refer to this as having an “internal
locus of control”” — a belief that one’s own actions,
rather than external forces, determine outcomes.
إٍْ Research often supports the benefits of taking
إٍْ personal ownership. A landmark study® published
in the Journal of Health Psychology found that
individuals who scored highly on self-efficacy”
measures — that is, those who believed in their ability
to make positive changes — were significantly more
likely to maintain healthy behaviors” over time,
regardless of their socioeconomic background"”.
Furthermore, advocates of personal responsibility argue that framing health as
someone else’s problem can be disempowering'’”. When individuals attribute their
choices entirely to outside forces‘), they risk becoming passive. Taking ownership, |
by contrast, builds confidence, resilience” , and a sense of purpose, qualities that are |
' central to a positive identity.

| 2. Social and Environmental Influences

However, the idea that individuals are entirely responsible for their own health is
increasingly challenged by social scientists and public health experts. Research in
sociology” demonstrates that where we grow up, how much money our family has, |
and the cultural rules we are raised with profoundly shape the choices available to us, :

a الوتحاصر لنة إنجليزية Living Well in a Complex World

A teenager growing up in a neighborhood” with no safe parks or jet, sae
affordable sports facilities cannot simply “choose” to exercise easily. دخل معدو )17[

A family with limited income” may struggle to prioritize fresh قوة إرادة (18)
vegetables over cheaper processed foods. These are not failures of عوائق بنيوية (19)
willpower ®; they are structural barriers”, obstacles built into the اختيار حقيقي (20)
environment that restrict genuine choice. تفصيلات )2([(
Moreover, advertising and social media exert enormous مراهقون (22)

: ee : : JWI سريع (23)

pressure on young people. Brands invest billions in campaigns متاح اسل لوصول )24(
designed to shape preferences™” and habits, often targeting سيانسنات )39(
teenagers” who are at a particularly impressionable stage of إجراءات (26)
development. دفعة لطيفة (27)

(28) سياق إٍْ
)25 بن / هياكل The Role of Government and Shared Responsibility .3

A third perspective argues that responsibility is not an either-or
choice; it does not belong entirely to the individual or entirely to society, but is shared. |
: Governments, schools, families, and communities all have a role in creating the
' conditions that make healthy living possible and accessible“.
In recent decades, many governments have introduced public health policies®
intended to guide and encourage better choices: taxes on high-sugar drinks,
restrictions on junk food advertising near schools, and national campaigns promoting
physical activity. Proponents argue that these measures“ do not remove individual
freedom — they reshape the environment so that the healthier choice becomes the
easier choice.
Economist Richard Thaler’s concept of the “nudge”” captures this idea precisely:
small, well-designed changes to the context in which decisions are made can
shift behavior without restricting freedom. From this viewpoint, responsibility is not
abandoned by the individual — it is supported and shared by the structures”
around them.

2 Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

11. When the neighborhood water pump broke, residents contributed what they could because everyone depended on it. Their response reflected collectivist values.
Which team displayed the same collectivist values?
a. The players followed the coach's plan because they trusted his experience.
b. The players chose separate routines suited to their personal fitness needs.
c. The players accepted shorter turns so that every member could take part.
d. The players chose matching shirts because they preferred the same color.
12. Salma liked two phones equally until she discovered that one battery lasted twice as long. Battery life played a decisive role in her choice.
Which event played a similarly decisive role?
a. Choosing window seats made the long journey more comfortable.
b. Bringing cold water kept everyone refreshed during the journey.
c. Playing quiet music helped the children remain calm on the bus.
d. Spotting the wrong platform in time enabled the family to catch their train.
13. Ahmed rejected the club's shared routine and designed a program around his personal goals. His approach was individualistic.
Which behavior best reflects an individualistic attitude?
a. Reem adjusted her plan so that all her relatives could join.
b. Reem planned her day without consulting her friends and followed the activities she preferred.
c. Reem copied her older cousin's schedule without making any choices.
d. Reem asked her relatives to choose every activity for her.

14. A sports magazine ranked the teams after each round. Wins and points affected their positions.
Which situation uses rank in the same sense?
a. A music app arranged its songs alphabetically by title.
b. A music app displayed the newest songs before the older ones.
c. A music app listed its songs from the most played to the least played.
d. A music app grouped songs by singer without placing them in order.
15. An online cooking course was targeted at beginners. Its first lessons focused on basic safety and very simple dishes.
Which situation is the clearest example of something being targeted?
a. The library displayed its opening hours at every entrance for all visitors.
b. The library placed returned books on shelves according to their reference numbers.
c. The library added more chairs to the reading room for everyone to use.
d. The library recorded audio guides for visitors who struggled to read printed signs.
16. Amr's urban neighborhood gives residents quick access to hospitals, markets, and several forms of public transport. Services remain available until late.
Which destination is also urban?
a. The bus passed widely separated homes beside crop fields and irrigation canals.
b. The bus entered streets lined continuously with tall buildings, traffic lights, and crowded roads.
c. The bus crossed open desert where no permanent homes or paved streets were visible.
d. The bus reached a small fishing village many kilometres from the nearest town.

◆ التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

1 Definitions التعريفات

Word | Definition
collectivist (adj) | giving priority to the interests and needs of the group rather than those of the individual
ذو نزعة جماعية
counseling (n) | professional advice and support given to help someone deal with personal or emotional problems
الإرشاد النفسي
decisive (adj) | having a very important influence on the final result of a situation
حاسم — مؤثر في النتيجة
individualistic (adj) | giving priority to personal independence, choices, and needs
فردي النزعة
interact(ed) (v) يتفاعل | to act on, communicate with, or affect one another
rank(ed) (v) يرتب — يصنف | to place people or things in order according to importance, quality, or success
rural (adj) ريفي | connected with the countryside rather than a town or city
targeted (adj) | directed at a particular person, group, or purpose
موجه — مستهدف
urban (adj) | connected with a town or city
حضري — متعلق بالمدينة
variable (n) متغير | a factor, amount, or condition that can change and may affect a result

individualistic (adj) فردي النزعة | individual-centred, self-directed
interact(ed) (v) يتفاعل | interrelate, engage, communicate
rank(ed) (v) يرتب — يصنف | rate, grade, classify, order

rural (adj) ريفي | country, countryside-based, non-urban
targeted (adj) موجّه - مستهدف | directed, aimed, focused
urban (adj) حضري - متعلق بالمدينة | city-based, metropolitan
variable (n) متغيّر | factor, element, changing quantity

غير مهم - غير مؤثر - غير ذي أثر

◆ التعريفات | Definitions

Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :
1. Although Nabil wanted a new phone, his collectivist family first repaired the water tank everyone needed.
What does "collectivist" mean in this context?
a. giving priority to instructions from older relatives
b. giving priority to saving money for future needs
c. giving priority to shared welfare over personal wishes
d. giving priority to traditions from earlier generations
2. After weeks of anxiety, she began counseling and gradually learned to discuss her feelings calmly.
Which definition describes the meaning of "counseling"?
a. professional support for personal or emotional difficulties
b. medical treatment for a physical health problem
c. practical instruction for improving a sporting skill
d. friendly conversation intended mainly for entertainment

3. The final penalty was decisive: when the player scored it, his team won the match.
In the sentence above, “decisive” most nearly means ............ .
a. having no real effect on the final outcome
b. causing a temporary delay in the activity
c. creating difficulty without changing the outcome
d. determining the final outcome of the situation
4. When the cousins planned a day out, individualistic Hassan ignored their joint
schedule and arranged his day without them.
Which meaning best matches “individualistic” in this sentence?
a. depending on others to choose what should happen
b. preferring personal choices to arrangements made by a group
c. copying decisions made by friends and relatives
d. putting collective needs before personal preferences
5. At the market, buyers and sellers interact; their discussions change what several people
finally buy. What does “interact” mean in this context?
a. affected one another through mutual contact
b. performed similar actions without communicating
c. followed identical instructions without exchanging ideas
d. observed what happened without becoming involved
6. After three races, I was ranked second in my age group, just behind the fastest runner.
What does “ranked” mean in this context?
a. selected to lead all the other runners
b. placed in a category because of age alone
c. given a position according to performance
d. prevented from entering another race
7. The bus entered a rural area where fields stretched between widely separated homes
and traffic almost disappeared.
Which definition describes “rural” as used here?
a. connected with coastal communities and fishing activities
b. connected with the countryside beyond towns and cities
c. connected with factories and industrial activities
d. connected with densely populated city neighbourhoods
8. The online programming course was targeted at complete beginners. Its first lessons
introduced basics. What does “targeted” mean in this context?
a. created without considering the intended users
b. changed after an earlier version caused complaints
c. presented to every possible user in the same form
d. designed for people with a particular level of experience
9. Nour's urban neighborhood has crowded roads, tall buildings, and public transport
running late into the evening.
What does “urban” mean in this sentence?
a. connected with isolated areas far from public services
b. connected with developed towns and cities
c. connected with places visited mainly by tourists
d. connected with farming communities in the countryside

10. The amount of water the garden needs is a variable: it is higher in hot weather and
lower after rain.
Which meaning best matches "variable" in this context?
a. a quantity that changes when conditions differ
b. a fixed quantity used in every situation
c. a typical quantity calculated from several figures
d. a quantity predicted before any facts are checked

◆ المترادفات | Synonyms
1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :
1. "Karim chose an individualistic routine, training alone and setting goals without joining

a. general b. random c. unfocused d. focused

a. urban b. metropolitan c. non-urban d. city-based
4. "The goalkeeper's final save was decisive, as it prevented an equalizing goal in the last

improving their own homes."
The adjective "collectivist" is closest in meaning to .......... .
a. individual-centred b. group-oriented c. self-focused d. individualistic

individualistic style, choosing solitary walks, private diaries, or personal exercise routines.
The program included cooking demonstrations, simple games, and free counseling
for anyone struggling with worry. Several sessions were targeted at teenagers, especially
those about sleep and sensible phone use. At first, visitors stayed close to people they
already knew. Before long, however, they interacted while preparing food and taking part

◆ المتضادات | Antonyms

A local radio station recently produced a series on how families manage stress. Others preferred an individualistic approach and made decisions without consulting the wider family.

Several episodes were targeted at teenagers, while later ones dealt with the concerns of parents and older people. Listeners interacted with the presenters through phone calls and voice messages. 4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

collectivist (adj) ذو نزعة جماعية
✔ Goes with:
• collectivist values قيم ذات نزعة جماعية
• collectivist culture ثقافة ذات نزعة جماعية
Word Family

Word | Example
collective (adj) جماعي / مشترك | The community took collective responsibility for the problem.
collectivism (n) النزعة الجماعية | Collectivism encourages people to support their group.
collectivist (adj) ذو نزعة جماعية | Collectivist cultures often value family support.

Don't mix
- collectivist = giving priority to the group and its shared interests
- collective = done, owned, or shared by all the members of a group

counseling (n) الإرشاد النفسي

Goes with:
• school counseling الإرشاد النفسي المدرسي • counseling services خدمات الإرشاد النفسي

Word Family

Word | Example
counsel (v) ينصح / يقدم المشورة | Trained teachers counsel students who are experiencing stress.
counseling (n) الإرشاد النفسي | Counseling can help adolescents manage stress.
counselor (n) مرشد نفسي | The school counselor listened carefully to the student.

Don't mix
- counseling = professional support that may continue over a period of time
- advice = an opinion or suggestion about what someone should do

decisive (adj) حاسم

Goes with:
• a decisive factor عامل حاسم • play a decisive role يؤدي دورا حاسما

Word Family

Word | Example
decide (v) يقرر | The committee will decide which programme to support.
decision (n) قرار | Exercising regularly was a wise decision.
decisive (adj) حاسم | Family support was a decisive factor in her recovery.

Don't mix
- decisive = capable of determining the final result
- important = having great value, but not necessarily determining the result

individualistic (adj) فردي الذعة

Goes with:
• individualistic approach نهج فردي الذعة

Word Family

Word | Example
individual (n/adj) فرد / فردي | Each individual has a free opinion.
individualism (n) الذعة الفردية | Individualism values personal decisions.
individualistic (adj) فردي الذعة | Some people are individualistic.

Don't mix
- individualistic = valuing personal independence and individual choice
- selfish = caring only about yourself and not about other people

interact (ed) (v)  يتفاعل

Goes with:
• interact with others  يتفاعل مع الآخرين  • factors interact  تتفاعل العوامل

Word Family

Word | Example
interact (v)  يتفاعل | Students interact with one another politely.
interaction (n)  تفاعل | Social interaction can improve emotional well-being.
interactive (adj)  تفاعلي | The teacher used an interactive learning activity.

Don't mix
- interact = communicate with or affect one another
- react = respond to an action, event, or situation

rank(ed) (v)  يرتب / يصنف

Goes with:
• rank something first  يصنف شيئا في المرتبة الأولى
• rank according to importance  يرتب حسب الأهمية

Word Family

Word | Example
rank (v/n)  يرتب او يصنف - مرتبة | Students ranked academic pressure as the main cause of stress.
ranking (n)  ترتيب - تصنيف | The school improved its national ranking.
ranked (adj)  مرتب - مصنف | The report contained a ranked list of the main causes.

Don't mix
- rank = place several people or things in a particular order
- rate = judge the quality or value of something, often by giving it a score

rural (adj)  ريفي

Goes with:
• rural areas  المناطق الريفية  • rural schools  المدارس الريفية

Word Family

Word | Example
rural (adj)  ريفي | Some rural schools lack counselling services.
rurally (adv)  في منطقة ريفية | Rurally located communities may be difficult to reach.
rurality (n)  الطابع الريفي | The area's rurality can make transport difficult.

Don't mix
- rural = connected with the countryside
- remote = far away from towns, cities, or other people
A rural place is not necessarily remote.

targeted (adj) موجَه / مستهدف

Goes with:
• targeted support دعم موجَه • a targeted campaign حملة موجَّهة

Word Family
Word | Example
target (n) هدف / فئة مستهدفة | Teenagers were the main target of the campaign.
target (v) يستهدف | The programme targets students experiencing stress.
targeted (adj) موجَه / مستهدف | Rural schools need targeted mental-health support.

Don't mix
- targeted = directed at a particular group, problem, or purpose
- general = intended for everyone and not directed at one particular group

urban (adj) حضري

Goes with:
• urban areas المناطق الحضرية
• urban development التنمية الحضرية

Word Family
Word | Example
urban (adj) حضري | Urban schools had better access to counseling services.
urbanize (v) يحول إلى منطقة حضرية | The government plans to urbanize the developing area.
urbanization (n) التحضر / التوسع العمراني | Rapid urbanization can place pressure on public services.

Don't mix
- urban = connected with a town or city
- suburban = connected with a residential area on the edge of a town or city

variable (n) متغير

Goes with:
• a key variable متغير رئيسي • a dependent variable متغير تابع

Word Family

| Word | Example |
|---|---|
| vary (v) يختلف / يتغير | Stress levels vary from one student to another. |
| variable (n/adj) متغير / قابل للتغير | Sleep was an important variable in the study. |
| variation (n) اختلاف / تفاوت | The survey revealed considerable variation in stress levels. |

Don't mix
- variable = a factor or quantity that can change
- variety = a collection or range of different types of things

1 Collocations, Expressions, Idioms & Prepositions

1. Successful people often ............ stress in difficult situations.

d. seek

a. in total

c. by chance
d. on average

a. even after

c. long before
d. immediately before

a. As a result
b. In other words
c. In contrast
d. For example

◆ القواعد اللغوية | Grammar Focus

Conditional Structures (Hedging)

First, Second & Third conditionals الحالة الأولى، الثانية والثالثة

1 First conditional الحالة الأولى

Statement الجملة الخبرية

If/Unless + مضارع بسيط , .... will / can / may + inf. ....

ex. - If he works hard, he'll pass the next exam.
= He'll pass the next exam if he works hard.

Yes / No Q. السؤال بـ (هل)

Will/Can/May + subj. + inf. .... + if + ... مضارع بسيط ?

ex. - Will he pass the next exam if he works hard?
= If he works hard, will he pass the next exam?

'Wh–' Q. السؤال بأداة استفهام

Q.W. + will/can/may + subj. + inf. .... + if + ... مضارع بسيط ?

ex. - What will happen if he works hard?
= If he works hard, what will happen?

◆ ملاحظات هامة | Important Notes

١ تعبر الحالة الشرطية الأولى عن مواقف قابلة للحدوث في المستقبل:

ex. - If I have enough money, I will buy a car. (I believe I'll have enough money).

٢- يمكن أن تَدْلَ (can - may - might - must - should) مَحْلُ (will) في جملة جواب الشرط في الحالة الشرطية
الأولى - لَدَخْ المقال التالي :

ex. - If I feel hungry at work, I will have a snack. (مَتَأكِد sure)
- If I feel hungry at work, I may have a snack. (من المُحْتَمِل probably)
- If I feel hungry at work, I might have a snack. (من المُمْكِن possibly)
- If I feel hungry at work, I must have a snack. (ضَرورَة necessity)
- If you can't do the job, you should apologize. (نَصِيحة) (advice) أو اقتراح) (suggestion)
- If I feel hungry at work, I can have a snack. (قَدْرَة / إِمْكانِيَّة) (ability / possibility)

٣- لا نستخدم المستقبل بعد (if) مباشرة لَكِن نستخدم المُضارِع البسيط:

ex. - If I (will follow - follow) a diet, I will get fit.
- What will you buy if you (have — will have) a million dollars?

ملحوظات تساعدك على حل سؤال الاختيار من متعدد

١- استخدام المُضارِع البسيط بعد (if) إذا كانت جملة جواب الشرط تتكون من أحدى الصيغ التالية :

1. Inf. / Don't + inf. / Never + inf. ............ (جملة أمر أو نهي)
ex. - Don't eat too much fat if you (want – wanted – had wanted)
to lose weight.
- If one of your friends (has – had – had had) a problem, help him/her.
2. Let's + inf. ..........
ex. - Let's take a rest and have a drink if you (are – were – had been) tired.
3. ........... had better ('d better) + inf. ...........
ex. - You'd better work hard if you (hope – hoped – had hoped) to succeed.
4. ........... would rather ('d rather) + inf. ...........
ex. - I'd rather stay at home if the weather (is – was – had been) windy.

٢- استخدام (will + inf.) في جملة جواب الشرط إذا كان فعل الشرط أحد الصيغ التالية :

1. If + subj. (would like / would love /would prefer ............)
ex. - If you would like to attend the party, I (will go – would go – would have gone)
with you.
2. If + subj. + am / is / are + inf. + ing ............ (مُضارِع مُستَمِرّ)
ex. - She (won't go – wouldn't go – wouldn't have gone) out if she is revising for her exams.
3. If + subj. + have / has + p.p. ............ (مُضارِع تَامّ)
ex. - If he has left home, you (won't be – wouldn't be – wouldn't have been) able to
meet him.

٣- الفرق بين (if / in case) :

تَعْني (if) أن الحدث سيقع إذا توفر شرط معين، فإذا لم يتوفر الشرط لن يحدث شيءًا:

ex. - I'll stay at home if it rains. (But I won't stay at home if it doesn't rain)

تستخدم (in case) بمعنى (في حالة) لَدَل على أننا سنقوم بشيء ما تحسبًا لحدوث شيء معين:

ex. - I'll stay at home in case it rains. (I'll stay at home because it may rain)

2 Second conditional الحالة الثانية

If/Unless + ماضي بسيط (تصريف ثان) , would/could/might + inf.

Statement الجملة الخبرية
→ ex. - If he trained hard, he'd win the match.
= He'd win the match if he trained hard.

Would/Could/Might + subj. + inf. + if + ماضي بسيط ؟

Yes / No Q. السؤال بـ (هل)
→ ex. - Would he win the match if he trained hard?
= If he trained hard, would he win the match?

Q.W. + would/could/might + subj. + inf. + if + ماضي بسيط ؟

' Wh–' Q. السؤال بأداة استفهام
→ ex. - What would he do if he trained hard?
= If he trained hard, what would he do?

◆ ملاحظات هامة | Important Notes

١ تعبر الحالة الثانية عن مواقف تخيلية أو غير محتملة الحدوث في الوقت الحاضر والمستقبل:
ex. - If he were taller, he'd be good at basketball.
٢ إعطاء النصيحة بالصيغة ( If I were … ) :
ex. - If I were you, I would see a doctor. You look very ill.
- لاحظ إمكانية استخدام (were) مع المفرد في حالة التخيل والنصيحة في الحالة الثانية:
ex. - Nada is ill today. If she were / was healthy, she would help you.

ملاحظات تساعدك على حل سؤال الاختيار من متعدد

١ الأفعال (cut – put – hit – shut – read…) لا تتغير عند تصريفها وتعتبر في الماضي البسيط إذا لم يضاف لها (s)
ex. - If he shut the farm door, the animals (won't – wouldn't) go out. : مع (he – she – it) : (s)
٢ إذا كان فعل الشرط (had + noun) تكون الجملة حالة ثانية ونختار (would + inf.) :
ex. - If Abdu had a camera, I (will borrow– would borrow– would have borrowed) it from him.
٣ إذا كان فعل الشرط (had to + inf.) تكون الجملة حالة ثانية ونختار (would + inf.) :
ex. - If we had to work at the weekend, we (will get– would get– would have got) a reward.
٤ إذا كان جواب الشرط (would have+ noun) تكون الجملة حالة ثانية ونختار ماضي بسيط :
ex. - If Sama (win – won – had won) the competition, she would have a prize.

إذا كان جواب الشرط (would have to+ inf.) تكون الجملة حالية ثانية واختار ماضي بسيط :

ex. - If Omar (spends – spent – had spent) all his money, he would have to borrow.

من الممكن استخدام التصريف الثالث للفعل كصفة

ex. - If the company had motivated employees موظفين متحمسين, it would make more profits.
- If I had written work, I would do it before going to bed.

3 Third conditional الحالة الثالثة

Statement الجملة الخبرية
If + had + p.p.…. , would/could/might + have + p.p. …. .
ex. - If he had won the competition, he'd have got the prize.
= He'd have got the prize if he had won the competition.

Yes / No Q. السؤال بـ (هل)
Would + subj. + have + p.p. + if + had + p.p. ..... ?
ex. - Would he have got the prize if he had won the competition?

'Wh–' Q. السؤال بأداة استفهام
Q.W. + would + subj. + have + p.p. + if + had + p.p. ..... ?
ex. - What would he have got if he had won the competition?
= If he had won the competition, what would he have got?

- تستخدم الحالة الثالثة في حالة الندم أو تخيل عكس ما حدث في الماضي :

ex. - I didn't feel ill yesterday. If I had felt ill, I would have stayed at home.

Notes for advanced level ملاحظات للمتفوقين

1 If = as long as طالما = providing (that) بشرط أن = provided (that) بشرط أن + جملة
ex. - If Malak does exercise regularly, she will keep fit.
- As long as Malak does exercise regularly, she will keep fit.

2 If + جملة = with / by / in case of + noun / (inf. + ing)
ex. - If he works hard, he will achieve his goals in life.
= In case of working hard, he will achieve his goals in life.

3 Unless + جملة = Without / But for بدون / لولا + (inf. +ing) / noun
ex. - Unless he earns enough money, he will have to sell his car.
= Without earning enough money, he will have to sell his car.
4 Unless + جملة ماضي بسيط = If it weren't for + (inf. + ing) / noun
ex. - Unless he had enough time, he wouldn't help me.
= If it weren't for having enough time, he wouldn't help me.
5 Unless + جملة ماضي تام = If it hadn't been for + (inf. + ing) / noun
ex. - Unless he had arrived early at the station, he would have missed the train.
= If it hadn't been for arriving early at the station, he would have missed the train.
6 If + جملة مضارع / ماضي بسيط = Should + subj. + inf. ..........
ex. - If he has a lot of money, he will help poor children.
= Should he have a lot of money, he will help poor children.
7 If + جملة ماضي بسيط = Were + subj. + (to +inf. ) .......... / Were + subj.+ ..........
ex. - If you phoned me, I would come. = Were you to phone me, I would come.
- If I were taller, I would join a basketball team.
= Were I taller, I would join a basketball team.
8 If + subj. + had + اسم = Had + subj. + اسم ..........
ex. - If I had a car, I would drive you home.
= Had I a car, I would drive you home.
9 If + subj. + had + p.p. = Had + subj. + p.p. ..........
ex. - If he had finished the report, he would have left the office.
= Had he finished the report, he would have left the office.

Exercises On Grammar Focus

1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

◆ البداية | Getting Started

1. If ice gets hot, it ........... .
a. melts b. won't melt c. would melt d. would have melted
2. If you look at the sky tonight, you ........... the stars.
..........
3. If metals ........... , they expand.
a. heating b. are heated c. will heat d. heated
4. When you leave iron in the open air, it ........... .
a. will rust b. rust c. rusts d. would rust

5. If he read the newspaper, he ........... the latest news.
a. would know          b. will know          c. knows          d. would have known
6. If I ........... free yesterday, I would have met you.
a. were          b. had been          c. am          d. would be
7. I would buy that mobile if I ........... enough money.
a. have          b. had          c. had had          d. will have
8. If he had broken his leg, he ........... to work.
a. could walk          b. couldn't have walked  c. can walk          d. walks
9. If I were you, I ........... a plan.
a. followed          b. had followed          c. would follow   d. will follow
10. If I were tall enough, I ........... the basketball team.
a. join          b. would join          c. will join          d. would have joined
11. He is sorry he hurt you. He ........... to you like that.
a. should talk                    b. shouldn't talk
c. should have talked             d. shouldn't have talked
12. I wish I ........... medicine when I was at university.
a. study          b. studied          c. had studied     d. would study
13. If I ........... my time last year, I would have passed.
a. had wasted          b. hadn't wasted          c. wasted          d. didn't waste
14. Would you have helped him if you ........... about his problem?
a. had known          b. knew          c. would know    d. have known
15. ........... happy if you achieved your goal?
a. You would be       b. Would you have been  c. You will be    d. Would you be
16. If my money ........... enough, I would have bought the laptop.
a. were          b. was          c. has been          d. had been

2 Special Cases

17. ........... our goalkeeper, we would have lost the match.
a. In case of                    b. If it weren't for
c. Unless                        d. If it hadn't been for
18. Without the taxi, we ........... late.
a. arrive                        b. will arrive
c. would have arrived            d. had arrived
19. ........... he have enough money, he will buy the book.
a. Should          b. Were          c. Had          d. Unless
20. If it ........... for your mobile, I couldn't call the police.
a. isn't          b. weren't          c. hadn't been    d. hasn't been
21. Write your name inside the book ........... you lose it.
a. if          b. in case          c. in case of     d. unless
22. ........... Adel taller, he would join the basketball team.
a. If          b. Should          c. Were          d. Had

a. If          b. Were          c. Should          d. Had
24. ........... he a car, he would give me a lift.
a. Were          b. Had          c. Should          d. If

◆ اختبر فهمك | Check Your Understanding

25. Abdulrahman succeeds because he is intelligent. This means ............ .
a. if he is intelligent, he succeeds
b. if he weren't intelligent, he wouldn't succeed
c. if he were intelligent, he would fail
d. if he hadn't been intelligent, he would have succeeded

26. I'll take some medicine in case I feel sick. This means ............ .
a. only if I feel sick                    b. when I feel sick
c. because I might feel sick              d. because I feel sick

27. "If you do not charge the phone tonight, it will switch off during the journey." Which of the following correctly rewrites the sentence using "unless"?
a. Unless you do not charge the phone tonight, it will switch off during the journey.
b. Unless you charge the phone tonight, it would switch off during the journey.
c. Unless you charge the phone tonight, it will switch off during the journey.
d. Unless you charged the phone tonight, it would have switched off during the journey.

28. "The weather may become cold, so Mariam will take a jacket with her."
Which of the following correctly combines the ideas using "in case"?
a. Mariam will take a jacket with her in case the weather becomes cold.
b. Mariam will take a jacket with her if the weather becomes cold.
c. Mariam would take a jacket with her in case the weather became cold.
d. Mariam will not take a jacket with her unless the weather becomes cold.

29. "If you need any help during the trip, you can call me."
Which of the following correctly transforms the if-clause using "should"?
a. Should you need any help during the trip, you would call me.
b. Should you have needed any help during the trip, you could have called me.
c. Should you need any help during the trip, you will have called me.
d. Should you need any help during the trip, you can call me.

30. "Hassan does not own a bicycle, so he cannot cycle to the farm."
Which of the following correctly expresses the same meaning using a conditional sentence?
a. If Hassan had owned a bicycle, he could have cycled to the farm.
b. If Hassan owned a bicycle, he could cycle to the farm.
c. If Hassan owns a bicycle, he can cycle to the farm.
d. If Hassan owned a bicycle, he could have cycled to the farm.

31. "Thanks to Salma's directions, we reached the village on time."
Which of the following correctly rewrites the sentence using "if it hadn't been for"?
a. If it hadn't been for Salma's directions, we would not reach the village on time.
b. If it hadn't been for Salma's directions, we had not reached the village on time.
c. If it hadn't been for Salma's directions, we would not have reached the village on time.
d. If it hadn't been for Salma's directions, we will not have reached the village on time.

32. Which sentence uses the first conditional to express a realistic future plan that depends on the weather?
a. If the rain stopped this afternoon, we would walk to the market.
b. If the rain had stopped by noon, we would have walked to the market.
c. If the rain stops, we usually walk to the market.
d. If the rain stops this afternoon, we will walk to the market.
33. Which sentence uses the second conditional to give advice about a present problem?
a. If I were you, I would switch off the phone and let it cool down.
b. If you switch off the phone now, it will cool down.
c. If you had switched off the phone, it would have cooled down.
d. If the phone becomes too hot, switch it off immediately.
34. Which sentence uses the third conditional to express regret about a missed opportunity in the past?
a. If I reach the station earlier tomorrow, I will catch the early bus.
b. If I reached the station earlier, I would catch the early bus.
c. If I had reached the station earlier, I would have caught the early bus.
d. Whenever I reach the station early, I catch the early bus.
35. Which sentence uses "as long as" to introduce a condition that must be met?
a. The coach journey lasted as long as three hours because of the traffic.
b. You can join the overnight trip as long as you bring written permission from a parent.
c. The queue for the trip was as long as the station platform.
d. The guide continued speaking for as long as the journey lasted.

2 Complete the following texts with the appropriate structure.

A. Karim is preparing for a day trip with his cousins at the weekend. He wants to stay energetic and avoid unnecessary problems. He knows that if he (1) .......... (skip) breakfast, he feels tired before noon. If he (2) .......... (have) enough food with him tomorrow, he will not need to buy snacks. He has promised that if the bus is delayed, he will (3) .......... (calls) his family immediately. His mother adds, "If you feel dizzy, do not (4) .......... (continued) walking; stop and rest." She also puts a light jacket in his bag (5) .......... (case) the weather turns cold.

B. Several families hope to turn an empty piece of land into a community garden. The work will not begin unless enough people (1) .......... (be) available on Saturday. Children may use the garden as long as an adult (2) .......... (be) with them. At present, the site has no water tap. (3) .......... (have) the site a water tap, caring for the plants would be much easier. A neighbor has grown vegetables for years. "If I (4) .......... (be) you, I would ask for her advice," Huda tells the organizers. They also know that without (5) .......... (work) together every week, they will not keep the garden healthy.

(take) one minute to check the bus timetable, they would not have missed the early bus.
The next bus was much later, but a local driver offered them a lift. If he had not stopped,
they would (2) .......... (arrive) after the fort had closed. If it had not (3) .......... (be) for his
help, they would have missed the guided tour. They are planning another visit. Should the
weather (4) .......... (becomes) stormy, they will postpone it. Their schedule is already full;
(5) .......... (be) they to stay an extra day, they would visit the nearby museum too.

Listening Text

Understanding Mental Health

A nationwide⁽¹⁾ school health survey⁽²⁾ done in Egypt in 2023 gathered⁽³⁾ data from 450 teenagers aged between 14 and 17, drawn from⁽⁴⁾ both urban and rural schools across five governorates⁽⁵⁾. The study aimed to identify the primary⁽⁶⁾ sources of stress⁽⁷⁾ among teenagers and to examine⁽⁸⁾ the coping strategies⁽⁹⁾ they most commonly⁽¹⁰⁾ adopted⁽¹¹⁾. Its findings show a complex picture of modern teenage life, in which academic pressure⁽¹²⁾, digital habits⁽¹³⁾, and social expectations⁽¹⁴⁾ interact⁽¹⁵⁾ in ways that are often difficult to separate. The most significant finding was that 68% of participants⁽¹⁶⁾ reported experiencing moderate⁽¹⁷⁾ to high levels of stress during examination periods. When asked to identify the main causes, students ranked academic pressure first (cited by 82% of respondents⁽¹⁸⁾), followed by social media comparison⁽¹⁹⁾ (61%), insufficient⁽²⁰⁾ sleep (57%), and family expectations (49%). Clearly, the study found that teenagers who did regular physical activity⁽²¹⁾—defined as at least 30 minutes of exercise four or more times per week—reported stress levels that were, on average, 34% lower than those of their sedentary⁽²²⁾ peers, even after controlling for⁽²³⁾ academic performance variables. Cultural background also was a significant factor in how teenagers managed stress. Students from families with strong collectivist⁽²⁴⁾ values were considerably more likely to seek emotional support⁽²⁵⁾ from parents or siblings when facing difficulties. In contrast, students from more individualistic⁽²⁶⁾ household environments tended to rely on⁽²⁷⁾ individual coping strategies such as listening to music, exercising alone, or spending time in nature. Neither approach was found to be universally more effective; rather, the study suggested that the availability and quality of support networks⁽²⁸⁾ were the decisive⁽²⁹⁾ variables. The researchers also noted a significant gap in access⁽³⁰⁾ to school counseling⁽³¹⁾ services between urban and rural institutions⁽³²⁾. While 74% of urban schools in the sample offered some form of counseling, the figure dropped to 31% in rural schools. The study concluded that targeted⁽³³⁾ mental health support programs in schools could meaningfully reduce stress levels among teenagers.

1 Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

1. During a football discussion, Reda insisted that the coach was the only reason the team lost and refused to consider any other explanation. His claim was absolute.
Which situation best reflects the meaning of “absolute"?
a. A traveller says one route is usually faster, although delays sometimes occur.
b. A reviewer suggests that two different explanations may both be reasonable.
c. A blogger insists that one diet works for every person without exception.
d. A mechanic says a noise could have several possible causes.

2. Sama wrote, "This battery never fails." I circled never as an absolute word.
Which situation best illustrates an “absolute word"?
a. A travel post says, "This route always saves time."
b. A health article says, "Walking may improve sleep."
c. A guide says, "The beach is sometimes crowded."
d. A review says, "The service appears to be improving."

3. In a product review, the writer says. "This charger never fails, even after years of use".
His editor questions whether such an absolute word is justified.
Which definition describes the meaning of “absolute word"?
a. A word that presents a claim without allowing exceptions
b. A word that makes a claim less certain or definite
c. A word that connects two contrasting points in an argument
d. A word that adds factual detail to a description

4. The journalist described one team's performance as "excellent" but the other team's performance as "awful" using clearly biased language.
Which meaning best matches “biased language" as used here?
a. Words used to describe facts without judgment
b. Words used to make an uncertain claim
c. Words used to compare two different groups
d. Words that show a strong opinion and are not fair or neutral

5. The meal ............ in the office canteen was healthy.
a. served     b. serving     c. serves     d. serve

6. Students ............ regularly usually feel more energetic.
a. exercised     b. exercising     c. are exercised     d. exercise by

7. After losing the match, the team trained again the next day; this showed their ............ .
a. concentration     b. anxiety
c. fatigue     d. emotional resilience

a. fatigue                    b. well-being                c. self-confidence    d. concentration

a. boost                      b. absorb                    c. reduce             d. maintain

season.
a. mental health                                            b. healthy fat
c. physical activity                                        d. academic performance

a. balanced diet          b. physical activity         c. sleep routine      d. mindfulness

is this sentence unsuitable for a balanced persuasive article?
a. It contains too many hedging words that make the context unnatural.
b. It asks the reader an unnecessary rhetorical question.
c. It makes an unfair generalisation and uses judgmental language.
d. It presents the claim with too much caution.

about modern lifestyles?
a. Everyone must completely change their lifestyle immediately.
b. Modern lifestyles always damage our health, so there is only one solution.

3 Read the text, then answer the questions.
Exercise is one of the few factors with a positive role in the long-term maintenance of body weight. Unfortunately, that message has not gotten through to the average American, who would rather try switching to 'light' drinks and low-calorie bread than increase physical exertion. The Centers for Disease Control, for example, found that fewer than one-fourth of overweight adults who were trying to shed pounds said they were combining exercise with their diet. In rejecting exercise, some people may be unduly discouraged by caloric expenditure charts; for example, one would have to briskly walk three miles just to work off the 275 calories in one scrumptious Danish pastry. Even exercise professionals concede half a point here. "Exercise by itself is a very tough way to lose weight," says York Onnen, program director of the President's Council on Physical Fitness and Sports. Still, exercise's supporting role in weight reduction is vital. A study at the Boston University Medical Center of overweight employees confirmed that those who dieted without exercise regained almost all their old weight, while those who worked exercise into their daily routine maintained their new weight. If you have been sedentary and decide to start walking one mile a day, the added exercise could burn an extra 100 calories daily.
In a year's time, assuming no increase in food intake, you could lose ten pounds.
By increasing the distance of your walks gradually and making other dietary adjustments, you may lose even more weight.

Social media posts can make ordinary life look perfect. A photo may show a curated

◆ المفردات في السياق: أسئلة الموقف | Vocabulary in Context: Scenario-based Questions

9. At first, Hossam played a new online game for only a few minutes at a time. Soon it
became addictive, and he found himself opening it again even when he had planned to
do something else. Which situation best reflects the meaning of “addictive”?

a. Salma drinks coffee only when relatives visit and easily goes without it on other days.

b. Nader tries several snacks before deciding which one he likes enough to buy again.

c. Reem watches one episode of a series each weekend and stops when the episode ends.

d. Tarek keeps using social media excessively even though he has repeatedly decided to
limit it.

10. As the plane was preparing to take off, Nasser felt growing anxiety. He could not relax
and kept imagining that something might go wrong. Which situation demonstrates the
same meaning of “anxiety”?

a. Karim uses the time before an appointment to finish a chapter of his book.

b. Sama paces around the room and loses her appetite while waiting for an important
phone call about her final-exam results.

c. Huda writes an appointment in her calendar so that she will remember it.

d. Nabil talks normally with his relatives while waiting for their taxi to arrive.

11. Amir sets a clear boundary when using his phone: he does not answer messages
during family meals. This helps him protect that time from unnecessary interruptions.
Which situation best illustrates the same boundary idea? .

a. Dina lends her headphones whenever anyone asks, even when she is using them herself.
b. Dina buys another pair of headphones after her old pair stops working properly.
c. Dina tells her cousins that they may borrow her things only after asking her first.

d. Dina keeps her headphones in a drawer so that they remain clean and easy to find.

12. Rokaya spent part of each weekend recording her grandmother’s old stories. She found
the activity fulfilling because memories that might have been forgotten would now
remain in the family. Which situation best illustrates the same meaning?

a. Hassan learned sign language so he could communicate more easily with his deaf
cousin, and their first long conversation left him smiling for the rest of the evening.

b. Hassan learns several keyboard shortcuts because they help him complete computer
tasks more quickly.

c. Hassan learns a card trick because he wants to surprise his friends at a family gathering.

d. Hassan memorizes football facts because he wants to answer more questions
correctly in an online quiz.

13. Laila was following a new recipe, but constant phone notifications interfered with her
concentration. Because her attention kept being drawn away, she missed two important
steps. Which situation best reflects the same meaning?

a. Hany turns on subtitles because he prefers reading the dialogue while watching movies.
b. Hany lowers the television volume because a baby in the next room has fallen asleep.
c. Loud drilling from the apartment next door makes Hany miss several minutes of

a movie he is trying to follow.
d. Hany chooses a shorter movie because he needs to wake up early the following morning.

Definitions التعريفات

Word

Definition

addictive (adj) مُسبّب للإدمان

anxiety (n) القلق

boundaries (n) حدّ - قاعدة

fulfilling (adj) guts - رض

| something

making you feel happy, satisfied, and complete

causing a very strong need or desire to keep doing or using

an uncomfortable feeling of nervousness or worry about 7
something that is happening _

limits or rules that show what is acceptable or allowed

interfere (d) (v) يتدخل - يعيق | to affect a situation in a negative way or stop something from working properly
mindful (adj) واعٍ - منتبه | paying conscious attention to the present moment
replicate (v) ينسخ - يحاكي | to copy something exactly or produce something similar
withdraw (v) يسحب - ينسحب | to take or move out or back, or to remove

2 | Synonyms المرادفات

Word | Synonym
addictive (adj) مُسبب للإدمان | habit-forming, compulsive, hard to stop
anxiety (n) قلق | worry, nervousness, unease
boundary (n) حدّ / قاعدة | limit, rule, restriction
fulfilling (adj) مُرضٍ / مُشبع | satisfying, rewarding, meaningful
interfere (v) يتدخل / يعيق | disturb, disrupt, get in the way of
mindful (adj) واعٍ / منتبه | conscious, aware, attentive
replicate (v) ينسخ / يحاكي | copy, reproduce, imitate
withdraw (v) ينسحب / يبتعد | pull back, retreat, remove oneself

3 | Antonyms المتضادات

Word | Antonym
addictive (adj) مُسبب للإدمان | non-addictive, easy to stop غير مسبب للإدمان - سهل الإيقاف
anxiety (n) قلق | calmness, confidence, relaxation, peace of mind هدوء - ثقة - سلم - استرخاء - سلم نفسي
boundary (n) حدّ / قاعدة | freedom, openness حرية - انفتاح
fulfilling (adj) مُرضٍ / مُشبع | unsatisfying, meaningless, disappointing, unrewarding غير مُرضٍ - بلا معنى - مُحبط - غير مُجزٍ
interfere (d) (v) يتدخل / يعيق | help, support, allow يساعد - يدعم - يسمح بـ
mindful (adj) واعٍ / منتبه | careless, unaware, distracted, inattentive مهمل - غير واعٍ - مشتت - غير منتبه
replicate (d) (v) ينسخ / يحاكي | create, invent, originate يبتكر - يخترع - يبدع
withdraw (v) ينسحب / يبتعد | join, participate, engage ينضم - يشارك - ينخرط

◆ تدريبات التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Exercises on Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

Definitions
° Choose the correct answer froma ,b,cord:

1. “After receiving several unkind comments about his drawings, Ibrahim began to
withdraw. He ignored messages from friends and preferred spending most evenings
alone.” In the sentence above, “withdraw” most nearly means ............ .

a. to seek support from other people after a difficult experience

b. to distance oneself from social contact and become less involved
c. to defend one’s choices when other people express disagreement
d. to become more active in a group after receiving encouragement

2. “As the time for her medical test approached, Rokaya felt growing anxiety and could
hardly relax or sleep.” What does “anxiety” mean in this context?
a. a feeling of nervousness or worry about what may happen
b. a feeling of excitement about an enjoyable experience
c. a feeling of annoyance caused by another person’s behaviour
d. a feeling of disappointment after receiving unwanted news

3.“At a family gathering, Nada made a fruit drink that everyone enjoyed. A week later,
she followed the same method and replicated it so successfully that it looked and
tasted like the first one.”” What does “replicated” mean in this context?
a. produced an earlier result again with very similar features
b. improved an earlier result by making several important changes
c. compared an earlier result with a newly developed alternative
d. replaced an earlier result with something completely different

4. “During his evening walk, Mohannad put his phone away and stayed mindful of the
sounds, movement, and fresh air around him.” Based on this sentence, what does
“mindful” mean?

a. thinking mainly about problems that may happen later

b. remembering events that happened a long time ago

c. avoiding anything that might require careful attention

d. paying conscious attention to what is happening at the present moment

5. “Loud construction noise outside interfered with Amira’s phone call, and she could
hardly hear the other person.” In the sentence above, “interfered with” most nearly
MEANS ...... ee
a. made an activity difficult to continue properly
b. made an activity more enjoyable than expected
c. made an activity finish earlier than planned
d. made an activity easier to organize successfully

6. “Moaz found a new online game addictive; even after deciding to put his phone
away, he kept going back to play another round.” What does “addictive” mean in this
context?

a. making someone interested in trying an activity for the first time
b. making someone enjoy an activity only for a short period

c. causing someone to feel a strong need to continue an activity

d. causing someone to become tired of an activity after frequent use

7. “Walid has a clear boundary with his friends: they may borrow his belongings only
after asking him first.” Which definition describes the meaning of “boundary” in this
sentence?

a. a responsibility that someone agrees to accept

b. a choice made after considering several possibilities

c. a habit that someone follows at the same time each day
d. a limit or rule that shows what is acceptable or allowed

8. “Zeinab found helping her younger brother learn to ride a bike fulfilling. Seeing him

finally ride on his own made her feel that all her time and effort had been worthwhile.”
Which meaning best matches “fulfilling” as used here?
a. making someone feel relieved because a difficult task has ended
b. giving someone a strong sense of satisfaction and personal value
c. making someone feel excited about trying an unfamiliar activity
d. giving someone an easier way to complete a demanding task

The underlined verb interfere is near in meaning to ............ .

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

addictive (adj) مُسبّب للإدمان

Goes with:
• addictive apps تطبيقات يدمنها المستخدمون
• become addictive يُدمن

Word Family

Word Example
addict (n) مُدمن - An addict cannot easily stop a harmful habit.
addiction (n) إدمان - Gaming addiction can affect sleep.
addictive (adj) مُسبّب للإدمان - Some games can become addictive.

Don't mix
• addictive (adj) مُسبّب للإدمان
= describes the activity or thing that causes addiction
• addicted (adj) مُدمن
= describes the person who suffers from addiction

anxiety (n) القلق

Goes with:
• feelings of anxiety مشاعر القلق
• anxiety and depression القلق والاكتئاب

Word Family

Word Example
anxiety (n) القلق - Screen pressure may increase anxiety.
anxious (adj) قَلِق - Some teenagers feel anxious online.
anxiously (adv) بقلق - He waited anxiously for replies.

Don't mix
- anxiety = strong worry or nervousness
- sadness = feeling unhappy

boundary (n) حد / قاعدة

Goes with:
- set boundaries — يضع حدود
- clear boundaries — حدود واضحة
- boundaries around screen use — ضوابط استخدام الشاشات

Word Family

Word | Example
boundary (n) حد / قاعدة | - Clear boundaries help control habits.
bound (v) يحد | - Rules may bound behavior.
bounded (adj) محدود | - A bounded activity has clear limits.

Don't mix
- boundary = a rule or limit
- balance = a fair amount between two sides

fulfilling (adj) مُرضٍ / مُشبِع

Goes with:
- fulfilling leisure life — حياة ترفيهية مُرضية
- fulfilling activity — نشاط مُرضي

Word Family

Word | Example
fulfil (v) يحقق / يُشبع | - Offline activities fulfil emotional needs.
fulfilment (n) رضا / إشباع | - Hobbies can bring fulfilment.
fulfilling (adj) مُرضي | - Creative hobbies are fulfilling.

Don't mix
- fulfilling = emotionally satisfying
- fun = enjoyable but may not be deep or meaningful

interfere (d) (v) يتدخل/يتطًّفل - يعيق

Goes with:
- interfere with sleep — يؤثَّر على النوم
- interfere with relationships — يؤثَّر على العلاقات

Word Family

Word | Example
interfere (v) يعيق / يتدخل - Gaming can interfere with sleep.
interference (n) إعاقة / تدخل - Avoid interference in others' affairs.
interfering (adj) مُعيق / متدخل - Interfering habits disturb daily life.

Don't mix
• interfere = affect negatively or disturb
• affect/influence = influence positively or negatively

mindful (adj) واعٍ / منتبه

Goes with:
• mindful screen use استخدام واع للشاشات
• be mindful of screen time يكون واعياً بالاستخدام الصحيح للشاشات

Word Family

Word | Example
mind (n) عقل / ذهن - The mind needs rest.
mindful (adj) واعٍ / منتبه - Mindful users control their habits.
mindfulness (n) يقظة ذهنية - Mindfulness can reduce stress.

Don't mix
• mindful = aware and careful
• careful = avoiding mistakes or danger

replicate (d) (v) يُنسخ / يُحاكي

Goes with:
• replicate benefits يُحقّق فوائد مماثلة
• replicate real experience يُحاكي تجربة حقيقية
• cannot replicate ...

Word Family

Word | Example
replicate (v) ينسخ / يُحاكي - Screens cannot replicate real social life.
replication (n) نسخ / محاكاة - Exact replication is difficult.
replicated (adj) منسوخ / مُحاكى - A replicated image may look real.

Don't mix
• replicate = copy or produce something similar
• replace = take the place of something

withdraw (v) ينسحب / يبتعد
Goes with:

» withdraw from friends ينسحب من الصداقات
e withdraw socially يتعزل اجتماعيًا

Word Family

Word Example
withdraw (v) ينسحب | - Some teenagers withdraw from real-world activities.
withdrawal (n) السحاب | - Social withdrawal harms well-being.
withdrawn (adj) منسحب / منعزل | - A withdrawn teenager avoids social contact.

© Don’t mix
• withdraw = pull back or stop joining others
° avoid = stay away from something intentionally

22 Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

7. A famous singer performed in a huge arena, with thousands of people watching from
seats surrounding the central floor. Which situation best illustrates the same meaning?
a. Passengers wait in a building beside numbered platforms for their trains.
b. Visitors move through several rooms to look at paintings and old objects.
c. Spectators fill rows of seats around a central area where a boxing event is taking place.
d. Shoppers walk through a building containing many different shops and cafés.

8. Rokaya bought an old bag from a woman whose family had made it by hand many
years earlier. She was pleased to own an authentic example of local craft. Which
situation best reflects the same meaning?

a.A museum displays a modern copy of an old statue while the original is being protected.

b. A family keeps a handwritten recipe that their grandmother actually wrote many
years ago.

c. A souvenir shop sells newly made coins designed to look like ancient ones.

d. An artist paints a new picture inspired by traditional village scenes.

9. Setting up a new phone required strong cognitive skills because the user had to follow
several instructions, remember the sequence, and decide which settings to change.
Which situation best reflects the same type of ability?

a. While assembling a cupboard, someone holds the heavy wooden pieces firmly in place.

b. While assembling a cupboard, someone stays calm when the work takes longer than
expected.

c. While assembling a cupboard, someone studies the diagram, works out the correct
order of the parts, and follows the steps accurately.

d. While assembling a cupboard, someone asks another person to hold the pieces while
the screws are tightened.

i a se Leisure Time 145

10. When heavy rain ruined our plan for an outdoor family meal, we showed flexibility

by moving the gathering indoors and changing the activities. In which situation does

someone show the same meaning?

a. Acook refuses to prepare dinner because one ingredient in the original recipe is
unavailable.

b. Acook delays dinner until someone can travel across town to buy the missing
ingredient.

c. Acook changes the planned dish and uses the ingredients already available at home.

d. Acook asks another person to decide what to make rather than changing the plan herself.

The evening before the concert, Malak and the band met for a final rehearsal, using

the stage, microphones, and lighting they would use the next day. Which situation best

illustrates the same meaning?

a. Before giving a speech at a celebration, Fouad goes through the whole speech in the
empty hall exactly as he plans to deliver it.

b. Before giving a speech at a family celebration, Fouad chooses clothes that suit the
occasion and has them prepared.

c. Before giving a speech at a family celebration, Fouad prints extra copies in case
someone asks to read it later.

d. Before giving a speech at a family celebration, Fouad checks when the event begins
and arranges his transport.

◆ التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms
تذويب: هذا الجزء هام جداً سواء على مستوى تعلُّم اللغة أو التمييز في الامتحانات

1 Definitions التعريفات

Word | Definition
arena (n) مَجال - ساحة | a place or situation where a particular activity happens
authentic (adj) حقيقي - أصيل | real and genuine, not false or copied
cognitive (adj) معرفي - ذهني | relating to mental processes such as thinking, understanding, and learning
engagement (n) اندماج - مشاركة | the act of being involved or interested in an activity
flexibility (n) مرونة | the ability to change or adapt easily to new situations
rehearsal (n) بروفة - تدريب قبل العرض | a practice session for a performance before the real event

2 Synonyms المترادفات

Word | Synonym
arena (n) مَجال - ساحة | field, setting, domain, sphere
authentic (adj) حقيقي - أصيل | genuine, real, original, trustworthy
cognitive (adj) معرفي - ذهني | mental, intellectual, thinking-related
engagement (n) اندماج - مشاركة | involvement, participation, interaction, active interest
flexibility (n) مرونة | adaptability, adjustability, openness
rehearsal (n) بروفة - تدريب قبل العرض | practice, run-through, trial performance

3 Antonyms المتضادات

Word | Antonym
authentic (adj) حقيقي - أصيل | fake, false, copied, inauthentic
مزيف - غير حقيقي - منسوخ - غير أصلي
cognitive (adj) معرفي - ذهني | physical, emotional, non-cognitive بذني - عاطفي - غير معرفي
engagement (n) اندماج - مشاركة | disengagement, withdrawal, disinterest
عدم اندماج - انسحاب - عدم اهتمام
flexibility (n) مرونة | rigidity, inflexibility, stiffness جمود - عدم مرونة - تصلب
rehearsal (n) بروفة - تدريب قبل العرض | final copy نسخة نهائي

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

arena (n) مَجال / ساحة

Goes with:
• a safe arena بيئة آمنة للتجريب والتجربة
• a non-judgmental arena بيئة داعمة لا تقوم على اللوم أو التقييم السلبي
• an arena for exploration بيئة مفتوحة للاستكشاف والتجريب

Word Family
Word | Example
arena (n)    مجال / ساحة    Art provides a safe arena for self-expression.

Don't mix
• arena = a place or situation where an activity happens
• area = a general place, subject, or part of something

authentic (adj)    حقيقي / أصيل

Goes with:
• authentic self-expression    تعبير صادق عن الذات
• authentic art    فن أصيل وصادق
• authentic experience    تجربة حقيقية وصادقة

Word Family
Word | Example
authentic (adj)    حقيقي / أصيل    Creative arts allow authentic self-expression.
authenticity (n)    أصالة    Authenticity makes art meaningful.
authentically (adv)    بأصالة / بصدق    She expresses herself authentically.

Don't mix
• authentic = real and genuine
• original = not copied from another source

cognitive (adj)    معرفي / ذهني

Goes with:
• cognitive flexibility    المرونة المعرفية / مرونة الذهنية
• cognitive development    التطور الذهني / النمو المعرفي
• cognitive skills    المهارات المعرفية / المهارات الذهنية

Word Family
Word | Example
cognition (n)    إدراك / معرفة    Cognition includes thinking and learning.
cognitive (adj)    معرفي / ذهني    Creative hobbies improve cognitive flexibility.
cognitively (adv)    معرفيا / ذهنيا    Art can support students cognitively.

Don't mix
• cognitive = related to thinking and learning
• emotional = related to feelings

engagement (n)    اندماج / مشاركة

Goes with:
• creative engagement    اندماج إبداعي
• student engagement    تفاعل الطلاب ومشاركتهم
• daily engagement    مشاركة يومية مستمرة

Word Family

Word | Example
engage (v) — يخرط / يشارك | Teenagers engage in creative activities.
engagement (n) — اندماج / مشاركة | Creative engagement can reduce stress.
engaged (adj) — مندمج / مشارك | Engaged students enjoy learning more.

Don't mix
- engagement = active involvement or interest
- entertainment = enjoyment or amusement

flexibility (n) — مرونة

Goes with:
- cognitive flexibility — المرونة الذهنية
- emotional flexibility — المرونة العاطفية
- improve flexibility — حسين القدرة على التكيف

Word Family

Word | Example
flexible (adj) — مرن | A flexible student adapts easily.
flexibility (n) — مرونة | Creative arts improve cognitive flexibility.
flexibly (adv) — بمرونة | Artists learn to think flexibly.

Don't mix
- flexibility = ability to adapt
- freedom = ability to choose without control

rehearsal (n) — بروفة / تدريب قبل العرض

Goes with:
- a school rehearsal — بروفة مدرسية
- a music rehearsal — بروفة موسيقية
- attend a rehearsal — يحضر بروفة

Word Family

Word | Example
rehearse (v) — يتدرب قبل العرض | Students rehearse before the concert.
rehearsal (n) — بروفة / تدريب قبل العرض | The rehearsal helped the orchestra prepare.
rehearsed (adj) — متدرب عليه | A rehearsed performance sounds more confident.

Don't mix
- rehearsal = practice before a performance
- performance = the real show in front of an audience
