◆ القواعد اللغوية | Grammar Focus

Gerunds & Infinitives

"-ing" forms or the infinitive

1 Verb + (inf. + ing)

١ الأفعال التالية تتبعها مصدر مضاف له (-ing):

admit يعترف/يقبر ب
appreciate يقدّر / يثمّن
avoid يتجنب
consider يفكر في
delay يؤجل
deny ينكر
dislike يكره
enjoy يتمتع ب
fancy يتوهم - يتخيل

finish ينهي
imagine يتخيل
include يشمل
keep يحظر / يستمر في
mind يمانع
miss يفوته
practice يمارس
risk يخاطر ب
suggest يقترح

- Karim admitted / denied borrowing my pen without asking me.
- The driver of the car avoided hitting the motorbike.
- He dislikes/ enjoys sleeping during the day.
- They're going home as soon as they've finished working.
- Samir and his brother practice playing tennis every day.
- My friend suggested going for a picnic on the beach.

٢ يمكن نفي الأفعال السابقة باستخدام (not + inf. + ing) :

- He suggested not going to the club and helping mom instead.

2 Verb + to + inf.

١ الأفعال التالية تتبعها (to + inf.):

afford يمكنه تحمّل تكلفة أن
agree يوافق على أن
arrange يرتب ل
choose يختار أن
decide يقرر أن
deserve يستحق أن
expect يتوقع أن
fail يفشل في أن
help يساعد في
hope يأمل أن
intend ينوي أن

learn يتعلم أن
manage يتمكن من
offer يعرض أن
plan يخطط أن
pretend يتظاهر أن
promise يعد أن
seem يبدو أن
threaten يهدد أن
try يحاول أن
want يريد أن
wish يتمني أن

- Everyone agreed / arranged / decided to meet at the airport.
- I expect / hope to finish my homework later this evening.
- I learned to swim when I was three years old.
- She offered / promised to help her mother prepare lunch.
- What do you plan / want to do in the summer?

٢ يمكن في الأفعال السابقة باستخدام (not to + inf.) :

- He intended not to go to the club with us.

3 Verb + (to + inf.) / (inf. + ing)

١ الأفعال التالية يتبعها (to + inf.) أو (-ing form) دون وجود اختلاف في المعنى:

love يحب prefer يفضل
hate بكره start / begin يبدأ
like يحب continue يستمر

- I hate/ like / love / prefer to do (= doing) exercise every day
- It has started to rain (= raining).

٢ إذا استخدمنا (would) قبل أي من الأفعال السابقة فلبد أن يأتي بعدها (to + inf.) فقط:

- I'd love to go for a swim this evening. (NOT: I'd love going)

٣ بعد (starting/beginning) نستخدم (to + inf.) فقط وليس (inf. + ing):

- It was starting to rain. (NOT: it was starting raining)

٤ الأفعال التالية يتبعها (to + inf.) أو (inf. + ing) مع وجود فرق في المعنى:

Verb | Example | Meaning
stop | - Leila stopped listening to the radio. | توقف عن القيام بشء
يتوقف | - Leila stopped to listen to the radio. | توقف لكي يقوم بشء آخر
remember | - I remembered phoning my mother. | يتذكر أنه فعل
يتذكر | - I remembered to phone my mother. | تذكر أن أفعال ثم فعلت
forget ينسي | - I forgot bringing my book. | نسيت أنى أحضرت الكتاب
| - I forgot to bring my book. | نسيت أن أحضر الكتاب
regret | - I regret arriving late. | نادم على ما فعلت
يندم / يشعر بالأسف | - I regret to say that you can't go. | أشعر بالأسف أننى مضر للقيام ر ...
try | - He tried to control the children, but they went on shouting. | يحاول /يقوم بمحاولة (بكل مجهود)
يحاول - يجرب | - Try logging off and logging on again. | يجرب (لا بكل مجهود)
go on | - He finished his English homework, and then he went on to study physics. | ينتقل من عمل لآخر
يستمر في | - He went on working for the same company. | يستمر في القيام بنفس العمل

4 to = in order to = so as to من أجل / لكى

- عندما نستخدم (to) بمعنى (لكى) يأتى بعدها الفعل فى المصدر (inf.) :
- I study hard to get good marks.
- Sama went home early in order to welcome the visitors.

5 to + (inf. + ing)

- عندما تكون (to) جزء من تعبير معين يأتى بعدها (inf. + ing) , ومن أمثلة هذه التعبيرات :

adapt to يتأقلم مع
be accustomed to معتاد على
apply .... to يطبق ... على
be opposed to يعترض على
be used to يكون معتاد على
devote... to... يخصص ... ل ...
become used to يتعود على
lead to يؤدى إلى
confess to يعترف ب
look forward to يتطلع إلى
contribute to يساهم فى
object to يعترض على
get/ grow/ become used to يتعود على
take to يعتاد على

- I look forward to (am used to) spending the holidays in Sharm.
- Rodayna took to living in Aswan.

6 Expression + (-ing form)

- التعبيرات التالية يأتى بعدها (inf. + ing):

be busy مشغول ب
feel like يود / يريد
be interested in مهتم ب
It's a waste of money مضيعة للمال
be worth يستحق
It's a waste of time مضيعة للوقت
can't help لا يستطيع أن يتوقف عن
It's no good لا فائدة من
can't stand لا يطيق
= It's no use لا فائدة من
prefer ... to ... يفضل...على...

- It's a waste of money buying this second-hand car.
- I prefer (drinking) coffee to (eating) sweets.
- I can't help feeling sorry for this poor man.

7 Special Cases حالت خاصه

1 الأفعال التالية يمكن أن يأتى بعدها المفعول ثم (to + inf.) :

advise ينصح
invite يدعو
allow يسمح
motivate يُحفِّز
ask يطلب
order يُأمر
beg يتوسل
permit يسمح
challenge يتحدى
persuade يقنع
choose يختار
promise يعد
command يُأمر
remind يُذكِّر

encourage يشجع teach يُعلّم
expect يتوقّع tell يُأمر
force يُجبر urge يدحث
help يساعد want يريد
hire يؤجر شخصا warn يُحذّر
instruct يُأمر would like يود / يريد
- I advised Ola to see a doctor.
- Rodayna allows Ahmed to play with her toys.
- Our teacher has asked us to write a letter.
- We will choose Mr Nasser to be the manager.
- في المبني للمجهول تأتي (to + inf.) بعد الفعل مباشرة:
- Ola was advised to see a doctor.
- Ahmed is allowed to use Rodayna's toys ( by her).
- We have been asked to write a letter.
- Mr Nasser will be chosen to be the manager.
٢ الأفعال التي يأتي بعدها (ing + inf.) في حالة عدم وجود مفعول ، و تأتي بعدها (to + inf.) في حالة وجود مفعول:
advise ينصح recommend يوصي ب
allow يسمح permit يسمح
forbid يمنع / يحرم encourage يشجع
- We don't allow smoking here. = We don't allow anyone to smoke here.
٣ الأفعال التية تستخدم بعدها المصدر يدل علي أننا تابعنا الحدث بالكامل ويأتي بعدها (ing + inf.) يدل علي أننا تابعنا جزء من الحدث:
hear يسمع notice يلاحظ
see يرى watch يشاهد
- I heard Mariam sing a song. = I heard the whole song.
- I heard Mariam singing a song. = I heard part of the song.

Creative Arts as Leisure Activities

: Creative Arts Are Good for Your Brain and Your Soul Jols aloail (1)

| The Psychological Benefits of “Flow” مشارقة إبداعية )2(

)3( كورتيزول (هرمون التوترا _ _ —_ i i

Ask anyone who has ever lost themselves in a painting, a song, or متام 7 سهل الوَصُول )(

a story, and they will tell you: creative activity is unlike anything else. علماء الأعصاب (5)

Time slows down, worries fade, and there is only the work in front معرفي / ذهني (6)

of you. Psychologists call this state “flow”, a condition of complete مرونة (7)

absorption‘ in a meaningful task. Research suggests that even thirty tenet عفد [عشر :)8(
minutes of creative engagement™ per day can measurably reduce cae ie ad

dou - J

levels of the stress hormone cortisol®, offering young people خط عرب )1((
a natural, accessible form of emotional recovery. ely (12)

ً Creative Hobbies and Cognitive Growth :متك )13(

The benefits of creative leisure go far beyond
simple relaxation. Neuroscientists® have found
that making art, whether drawing, playing an
instrument, or writing, activates multiple regions
of the brain. It strengthens neural connections and
improves cognitive flexibility. Unlike passive
screen-based entertainment, creative activity
demands that the brain produce rather than merely

make you smarter, and the evidence for this is
growing stronger with each decade™ of research.

' Self-Expression and Cultural Identity

For teenagers, creative arts offer something especially valuable: a space for
authentic” self-expression. During the teenage years, young people are constantly
figuring out who they are, who they want to be, and how they fit into the world. Art
provides a safe, non-judgemental arena“ for this exploration. In Egypt, traditional art
forms such as calligraphy‘), arabesque design, music, and storytelling offer additional ٠
layers of cultural meaning, connecting young people to their heritage” while allowing |
them to understand it in a new and contemporary way. إْ

|. Developing Valuable Collaborative Skills

Collaboration is another strong benefit. A school orchestra, a drama production,
a street art collective — all of these require young people to listen to each other, and
work toward a shared goal”. These are skills that no classroom test can measure, yet
employers and universities consistently rank them among the most valuable skills
a young person can possess.

Building Resilience Through Failure إٍ
as) بروفة Perhaps most importantly, creative arts teach young people to
(13) مرونة embrace failure. In art, there is no single correct answer. A painting
that does not turn out as planned is not a failure — it is a lesson. A musician who
plays a wrong note in a concert learns more from that moment than from any perfect

rehearsal”), Research by Arts Council England showed that young people who
regularly engage in creative activities show greater emotional resilience’ and are more
likely to volunteer in their communities, suggesting that the benefits extend far beyond

the individual.

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Opinion Paragraph فقرة الرأي

1. What is an opinion Paragraph? ما هي فقرة الرأي؟
An opinion paragraph is a short piece of writing in which the writer gives a clear opinion about a topic and supports it with reasons and examples.
فقرة الرأي هي قطعة كتابة قصيرة بحيث فيها الكاتب عن رأيه بوضوح في موضوع معين ويدعمه بالأسباب والأمثلة.

2. What Is an Opinion Paragraph Used For? ما هو استخدام فقرة الرأي؟
An opinion paragraph is used to:
• Express a personal view
• Give reasons
• Support the view with examples or evidence
• Finish with a clear conclusion
تُستخدم فقرة الرأي للتعبير عن وجهة نظر شخصية، وتقديم أسباب، ودعم الرأي بأمثلة أو أدلة، والانتهاء بخاتمة واضحة.

Main Parts of an Opinion Paragraph
الأجزاء الرئيسية لفقرة الرأي

Part | Function

1. Opinion / Claim الرأي / الدعاء
State your opinion clearly at the beginning.
Useful expressions:
- I believe that ... - In my opinion, ...
- I think that ... - From my point of view, ...
e.g.
- I believe that young people need more time for creative activities.

2. Reason السبب
Give a reason to support your opinion.
Useful expressions:
- This is because ... - The reason is that ...
- One reason is that - Another reason is that ...
e.g.
- This is because creative arts help teenagers express their emotions.

3. Example/ Evidence المثال / الدليل
Add an example or explanation to prove your idea.
Useful expressions:
- For example/instance, ... - The text shows that ...
- According to the article, ...
e.g.
- For example, the article says that creative activities improve cognitive flexibility.

Restate your opinion in a different way.
Useful expressions:

4. Conclusion - In conclusion, ... - To sum up, ...
aoileJ! - To conclude, ... - Overall, ...
- To put the whole matter in a nutshell, ...
e.g.

- In conclusion, creative arts are essential for teenagers’ development.

◆ تدريبات Writing | Exercises On Writing

Choose the correct answer from a, b, c, or d:

1. Which sentence is most suitable for stating an opinion clearly at the beginning of
a paragraph about creative activities?
a. For example, many teenagers enjoy drawing and music.
b. In my opinion, creative activities are important for young people.
c. This is because they can help teenagers express themselves.
d. In conclusion, creative activities have many benefits.

2. Which expression is used to introduce a reason supporting an opinion?
a. In conclusion, b. For example,
c. One reason is that ... d. From my point of view, ...

3. Which sentence functions as an example or evidence in an opinion paragraph?
a. I believe that teenagers should spend more time on creative hobbies.
b. One reason is that these activities can develop useful abilities.
c. For instance, the article says that creative activities improve cognitive flexibility.
d. To sum up, creative hobbies can play an important role in teenagers’ lives.

4, Which expression is most suitable for introducing the conclusion of an opinion paragraph?
a. Overall, ... b. This is because ...
c. According to the article, ... d. One reason is that ...

5. Read the opening sentence:
I believe that teenagers should have more opportunities to take part in creative
activities.
Which sentence would best follow it as a reason?
a. In conclusion, creative activities deserve more attention.
b. This is because they give young people ways to express their ideas and feelings.
c. For example, the article mentions drawing, music, and storytelling.
d. From my point of view, creative activities are valuable.

6. Which sentence would best provide evidence or an example after this reason?
One reason is that creative hobbies can strengthen teenagers’ thinking skills.
a. I think creative hobbies should be available to everyone.
b. Another reason is that teenagers often enjoy learning new skills.
c. According to the article, making art can improve cognitive flexibility.
d. Overall, creative hobbies support young people in different ways.

7. Which order creates the clearest opinion paragraph?

1. For example, the text shows that creative activities can improve cognitive flexibility.
2. In conclusion, creative hobbies can make teenagers’ free time more valuable.
3.In my opinion, teenagers should spend some of their free time on creative hobbies.

4, One reason is that these activities can support mental development.

8. A writer gives this opinion:

From my point of view, taking part in the arts is valuable for teenagers.
Which sentence best supports this opinion with a clear reason?

a. Many teenagers choose different hobbies in their free time.

b. For instance, some people enjoy music while others prefer drawing.

c. One reason is that creative activities allow teenagers to express themselves.
d. To conclude, the arts can have an important place in teenagers’ lives.

9. Which conclusion best restates this opinion in a different way?

I think creative activities should be an important part of teenagers’ free time.

a. Overall, teenagers should make room for creative activities in their leisure time.

b. For example, teenagers can spend their free time drawing, writing, or playing music.

c. Another reason is that creative activities can help teenagers express themselves.

d. In conclusion, I think creative activities should be an important part of teenagers’
free time.

10. A writer ends an opinion paragraph like this:

In conclusion, creative hobbies are valuable for teenagers. For example, some

teenagers enjoy painting and music.

Which revision best improves the ending according to the lesson?

a. In conclusion, creative activities can play an important role in teenagers’ development.

b. Another reason is that creative activities can help teenagers express themselves.

c. According to the article, creative activities can improve cognitive flexibility.

d. In my opinion, creative hobbies are valuable for teenagers.

Which paragraph follows the taught structure of an opinion paragraph most effectively?

a. For example, drawing can improve cognitive flexibility. Creative hobbies are useful.
One reason is that teenagers enjoy them. According to the article, art has benefits.

b. I think creative hobbies are important. In conclusion, they are important. From my
point of view, teenagers need them. Overall, they are useful.

c. In my opinion, creative activities are valuable for teenagers. One reason is that
they support self-expression. For example, the text shows that art gives young
people a way to explore their ideas and feelings. In conclusion, creative hobbies can
contribute positively to teenagers’ development.

d. One reason is that creative activities are interesting. For example, some teenagers
draw. Another reason is that music exists in many forms. According to the article,
creative activities are common.

person takes the lead, he concentrates even harder and becomes determined to finish

accurately.

the defense.

weekend.

◆ التعريفات والمترادفات والمتضادات | Definitions, Synonyms & Antonyms

تنويه هذا الجزء مهم جدًا سوف عان مستوحى تعلّم اللغة أو التميز فص الامتحان.

1 Definitions التعريفات

Word | Definition
access (n) إمكانية الوصول والاستفادة - صلاحية الدخول إلى | the right or opportunity to use, enter, or obtain something
competitive (adj) تنافسي | wanting to win or be more successful than other people
enjoyment (n) استمتاع | a feeling of happiness or pleasure from something
inactive (adj) غير نشيط - خامل - كسول | not active or not doing much activity
life skill (n) مهارة حياتية | a skill that is useful or important in everyday life
psychological (adj) نفسي - ذهني | relating to the human mind and feelings

2 Synonyms المترادفات

Word | Synonym

competitive (adj) تنافسي | ambitious, driven, striving
enjoyment (n) استمتاع | pleasure, delight, satisfaction, joy
inactive (adj) غير نشيط - خامل - كسول | idle, sedentary, sluggish
life skill (n) مهارة حياتية | practical skill, everyday skill, functional skill
psychological (adj) نفسي - ذهني | mental, emotional

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Word Families    عائلات المفردات

• What are Word Families?    ما المقصود بعائلات المفردات؟
→ Word families are groups of words that share the same root, but belong to different grammatical categories: nouns, verbs, adjectives, or adverbs.
عائلة المفردات هي مجموعة كلمات تشترك في نفس الأصل أو الكلمة الأساسية، لكنها تنتمي إلى ثلاث نحويات مختلفة مثل: اسم، فعل، صفة، أو ظرف.
→ Recognizing word families helps you understand new words faster, expand your vocabulary, and use words correctly in writing and speaking.
التعرف على عائلات الكلمة يساعدك على فهم الكلمات الجديدة بسرعة أسرع، وتوسع حصيلتك اللغوية، واستخدام الكلمات بصورة صحيحة في الكتابة والتحذث.
→ The meaning is related, but the job in the sentence changes.
المعنى يكون مرتبطا، لكن وظيفة الكلمة داخل الجملة تتغير.

Lesson word-family examples    أمثلة من عائلات مفردات الدرس

Verb | Noun | Adjective | Adverb
act  يتصرف | act  تَصْرُف - فصل | active  نشيط | actively  بنشاط
activate  يُنشّط - يُفَعّل | action  حدث | inactive  خامل
| activity  نشاط | activated  مُفَعَّل
| activation  تفعيل | deactivated  غير مُفَعَّل
compete  ينافس | competition  مسابقة | competitive  تنافسي | competitively  بتنافسية
| competence  جَذازة
participate  يشارك | participation  مشاركة | participatory  تشاركي | —
| participant  أحد المشاركين
motivate  يُحَفِّز | motivation  تحفيز | motivated  مُحَفِّز | —
| | motivating  مُحَفِّز
signify  يعني - يشير إلى | significance  معني - مَغزى | significant  ذو مَغزى | significantly بشكل ملحوظ

1 Rules to form nouns    قواعد تكوين الإسم

→ Many nouns are formed by adding a noun suffix to a verb, adjective, or base word. These endings often show that the word is a noun.
يتم تكوين أسماء كثيرة بإضافة نهاية اسمية إلى فعل أو صفة أو كلمة أساسية. وغالباً تساعد هذه النهايات على معرفة أن الكلمة اسم.
→ Do not force one ending for all words.

Suffix | Common use | Examples
-ment | action / result noun    تدل كثيرا على نتيجة الفعل أو العملية | - improve → improvement
| | - manage → management
-ion | action / process noun | - participate → participation
-tion | | - motivate → motivation
-sion | تُستخدم كثيرا لتكوين اسم يدل على عملية أو حدث | - decide → decision

-ness | quality / state noun | - kind → kindness
تحل غالباً عل اسم | - happy → happiness

-ity | quality / state noun | - active → activity
-ty | تحل غالباً عل صفة أو حالة | - able → ability

-er | person noun | - teach → teacher
-or | تحل غالباً على الشخص الذى يقوم بعمل أو له مجال | - act → actor
-ist | معين | - psychology → psychologist

2 | Rules to form Adjectives | قواعد تكوين الصفات

→ Many adjectives are formed by adding an adjective suffix to a noun, verb, or base word.
An adjective describes a noun or pronoun.
يتم تكوين صفات كثيرة بإضافة نهاية خاصة بالصفات إلى اسم أو فعل أو كلمة أساسية. والصفة تصف اسماً أو ضميراً.

Suffix | Common use | Examples

-ive | having a quality or tendency | - compete → competitive
-ative | تحل غالباً على صفة أو مبن معين | - create → creative

-al | | - education → educational
-ic | related to something | تعني غالباً: متعلق ب | - academy → academic
-ical | | - psychology → psychological

-able | can be done / suitable for | - enjoy → enjoyable
-ible | تعني غالباً: يمكن فعله أو مناسب ل | - access → accessible

-ful | with / without | تعني غالباً: ممتلئ ب / خالي من | - use → useful
-less | | - care → careless

-ed | feeling / causing feeling | - motivate → motivated
-ing | النهاية (ed) غالبا تصف الشعور والنهاية (ing) تصف الشيء المسبب للشعور | - motivate → motivating

3 | Rules to form Adverbs | قواعد تكوين الظرف

→ An adverb can describe a verb, an adjective, another adverb, or a whole sentence.
Most adverbs of manner are formed from adjectives by adding (-ly).
الظرف يمكن أن يصف فعلاً أو صفة أو ظرفاً آخر أو الجملة كلها، يتم تكوين ظروف الكيفية من الصفات بإضافة (ly).

Suffix | Examples

adj+ -ly | - careful → carefully
- graceful → gracefully

consonant + y → i + ly | - happy → happily    - easy → easily

-le → -ly | - gentle → gently    - simple → simply

-ic → -ically | - academic → academically
- dramatic → dramatically

common exceptions | - fast → fast - hard → hard
Be careful: hard ≠ hardly
- hard = with effort
- hardly = almost not

4 | Rules to form verbs | قواعد تكوين الأفعال

→ Some verbs are formed by adding verb suffixes to adjectives, nouns, or base words. However, many English verbs have no special suffix, so the dictionary is important.
يتم تكوين بعض الأفعال بإضافة نهايات فعالية إلى صفات أو أسماء أو كلمات أساسية. لكن كثيرًا من الأفعال الإنجليزية لا تحمل نهاية خاصة، لذلك يكون القاموس مفيدًا.

Suffix | Common use

-ate | - active → activate - motive → motivate
نهاية شائعة في أفعال كثيرة

-en | - strength → strengthen - short → shorten
تحل غالبًا على جعل الشيء يتصف بالمعنى

-ify / -fy | - simple → simplify - beauty → beautify
تعني غالبًا: يجعل الشيء كذا

-ise / -ize | - modern → modernise / modernize
- industrial → industrialise / industrialize
تُستخدم في أفعال كثيرة، واللصقتان مقبولتان حسب النظام

same form as noun | support → support / access → access / plan → plan
أحيانًا يكون الاسم والفعل بنفس الشكل

• How to deal with word families? | كيفية التعامل مع عائلات المفردات؟
→ Before forming or choosing a word, identify the needed form:
قبل تكوين أو اختيار كلمة، حدد الصيغة المطلوبة، لاحظ الجدول التالي:

Position | Needed form | Example

After: بعد أداة أو صفة
- adjective
- a, an, the
- this, that, these, those
- my, his, her, its, your, our, their … | + noun | a significant impact

Before a noun قبل الاسم | adjective | a competitive game

After: بعد الأفعال المساعدة التالية:
- don't, doesn't, didn't
بعد الأفعال الناقصة أو (to)
- can, could, shall, should, must …
- to | + verb | can motivate students

Describes how an action happens
إذا وصفنا طريقة حدوث الفعل فهي ظرف | adverb | played competitively

٠ Important note: ملاحظة مهمة

— Suffixes are helpful clues, but they are not perfect rules. The sentence position and
a good learner’s dictionary decide the correct form.

النهايات مؤشرات 6160 laid) ليست قواعد مطلقة. موقع الكلمة في الجملة والقاموس الجيد يحددان الصيغة الصحيحة.

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

insist (v)    يُصرّ

✓ Goes with:
- insist that + subj. + inf. / (should + inf.) ...
- I insisted that she write (should write) the paragraph again.
- insist on + noun / -ing
- Sama insisted on buying the white blouse.

Word Family

Word | Example
insist (v) يُصر | My friend insisted that I join him.
insistence (n) الإصرار | His insistence changed my plan.
insistent (adj) مُصر/عازم | She was insistent about finishing the trip.

Don't mix

insist يصر بقوة | Rodayna insisted that we travel by train.
suggest يقترح (بدون إجبار) | Rodayna suggested that we travel by train

Pedal stroke ضغطة بذال / ضغطة على الدواسة

Goes with:
• one more pedal stroke    ضغطة أخرى على البذال
• a strong pedal stroke    ضغطة قوية على البذال

Word Family

Word | Example
pedal (n) الدواسة / البدال | The pedal was hard to push
pedal (v) يضغط على الدواسة | He pedalled slowly.
pedal stroke ضغط البدال | I took one more pedal stroke

Sense of achievement شعور بالإنجاز

Goes with:
• have a sense of achievement    لديه شعور بالإنجاز
• give someone a sense of achievement    يعطي شخص شعور بالإنجاز

Word Family

Word | Example
achieve (v) ينجز | She achieved her goal.
achievement (n) إنجاز | The trip gave me a sense of achievement.
achievable (adj) ممكن تحقيقه | The goal is achievable.

shimmer (ed) (v) يلمع بخفوت

Goes with:
• shimmer softly    يلمع برقة
• shimmer beside / on    يتلألأ بجانب / على

Word Family

Word / Example

shimmer (v)	يتلألأ	The Nile shimmered beside us.
shimmer (n)	بريق خافت	The shimmer of the water was beautiful.
shimmering (adj)	ذو بريق خافت	The shimmering river looked calm.

Don't mix

shimmer	يلمع بخفوت	The light shimmered far away.
shine –shone	يلمع عمودا و بشكل أفوي أحيانا	The sun shone brightly all day.

struggle to keep up (v)	يكافح لمجاراة الآخرين

Goes with:
• struggle to keep up with someone	يكافح لمجاراة الآخرين
• struggle to keep up during a trip	يكافح ليحافظ على المستوى في الرحلة

Word Family

Word / Example

struggle (v)	يكافح - يناضل	I struggled to keep up.
struggle (n)	كفاح / محاولة جادة	The trip became a real struggle.
struggling (adj)	مُكافح	The struggling cyclist needed rest.

wear on (phr. v)	يمضي ببطء

Goes with:
• as the day wore on	بينما يمضي النهار/اليوم

Word Family

Word / Example

wear on (v)	يمضي	As the morning wore on, the sun grew stronger.

Don't mix

wear on	يمضي الوقت ببطء	As the long lecture wore on, the students became tired.
wear out	يُرهق - يُلي	Too much screen time can wear out your eyes.
wear away	يُأكل / يُزيل تدريجيا	The waves wore away the rocks over time
wear off	يزول تدريجيا	The excitement wore off after a few hours.

Not Exactly a Relaxing Day الغ

When we face unexpected situations outdoors“, we often learn lessons that contribute |
to our personal experience® and mental growth. For instance, being caught in
a sudden rainstorm” or navigating a difficult trail® forces us to be fully present in the
moment. This shift in focus moves us away from daily stressors“ and towards the
immediate environment. Ultimately, overcoming” these obstacles" provides a sense of
excitement and achievement that makes the experience truly meaningful”.

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Diary Entry مُفَكَّرَة اليوميات

1. Definition of Diary entry
A diary entry is a form of autobiographical writing in which one records their personal experience, thoughts, feelings, and observations on a regular basis.
تدوينة اليوميات هي نوع من الكتابة الذاتية يسجل فيها الشخص بانتظام تجاربه الشخصية وأفكاره ومشاعره وملاحظاته.

2. The purpose of a Diary Entry الغرض من كتابة اليوميات
A diary entry serves several functional and psychological roles.
تؤدي كتابة اليوميات عدة وظائف عملية وأدوار نفسية:

• Purpose الغرض
A "safe" outlet متنفس «آمن» للتعبير عن المشاعر
Self-reflection and clarity التأمل الذاتي ووضوح الأفكار
Creative growth تنمية الإبداع
Stress reduction الحد من التوتر

Main Parts of a diary entry
الأجزاء الرئيسية لمُفَكَّرَة اليوميات

1 Opening المقدمة

Date and salutation (e.g., Dear Diary, Friday...) Set the scene: where you were, what you had just done.
ينبغي أن تتضمن المقدمة التاريخ والتحية. ففي تساعد القارئ على معرفة الزمن والمكان وماذا فعلت.

Useful expressions:
• Dear Diary,
• Today was a day I will never forget.
• I thought it would be a normal day, but I was wrong.
• This morning, I went on a trip with my friend.
• At first, I expected the day to be relaxing.
Example:
Dear Diary,
Today was not the peaceful free day I had imagined. My friend insisted that I join him on a cycling trip along the Nile, and I agreed although I was not sure it was a good idea.

2 | The story سرد الأحداث

Recount the main events in order using narrative tenses. Include: the first hour (positive), the turning point (difficulty), the decision to keep going.
لسرد الأحداث الرئيسية بترتيب زمني مستخدماً (زمن السرد، اذكر البداية الإيجابية والصعوبة أو نقطة التحول ثم قراراتك بمواصلة ما بدأت.

Useful expressions:
• At first, ...
• We started by...
• While we were cycling, ...
• After we had ridden for an hour, ...
• Then, things began to change.
• As the morning wore on, ...
Example:
At first, everything felt calm and enjoyable. The air was cool, the road was quiet, and the Nile shimmered beside us. However, after we had cycled for some time, the sun became stronger and the road seemed much longer.

3 | Turning point نقطة التحول

Describe the moment you most wanted to stop. What stopped you? Use Past Continuous to show what was happening around you at that moment.
صف اللحظة التي رغبت فيها بشدة في التوقف، ما الذي منعك من الاستسلام؟ استخدم الماضي المستمر لوصف ما كان يحدث حولك في تلك اللحظة.

Useful expressions:
• Then, things changed.
• At that moment, I felt ...
• The hardest moment came when ...
• I almost gave up when ...
• I began to wonder if I could continue.
• I realized that I had to keep going.
Example:
The hardest moment came when my legs began to ache and my breathing became uneven. I almost gave up, but something inside me refused to stop. I wanted to know whether I was capable of finishing the trip.

4 | The ending الخاتمة

How did it feel when you finally stopped? Use a simile or vivid detail to describe your exhaustion and your satisfaction.
صف شعورك عند توقفك أخيراً. استخدم تشبيهاً أو تفاصيل وصفية مؤثرة للتعبير عن إرهاقك وشعورك بالرضا.

..........

Useful expressions:
• Finally, ...
• In the end, ...
• When we stopped, ...
• By the end of the day, ...
• I felt exhausted but proud.
• I was tired, but I felt satisfied.
Example:
Finally, we stopped beside the Nile. I was completely exhausted, but I felt proud of myself. The cold juice, the quiet water, and the afternoon light made the moment unforgettable.

5 Reflection التأمل الشخصي

What did this experience teach you about yourself? About leisure? This is the diary's message – make it personal and honest.
ماذا علّمتك هذه التجربة عن نفسك وعن وقت الفراغ؟ هذا هو المحتوى الأساسي من تدوينة اليوميات، لذلك اجعله شخصيًا وصادقًا.
Useful expressions:
• This experience taught me that ...
• I learned that ...
• Now I understand that ...
• I used to think..., but now I believe ...
• The day changed my understanding of ...
• I discovered that ...
Example:
This experience taught me that leisure is not always about doing nothing. Sometimes, real enjoyment comes from effort, challenge, and discovery that you are stronger than you thought.

6 Final Sentence الجملة الختامية

End with a memorable line that captures your key insight. It can be funny, philosophical, or both.
اختم بجملة مميزة تعبر عن أهم فكرة أو درس توصلتَ إليه. يمكن أن تكون الجملة طريفة أو فلسفية أو تجمع بينهما.
Useful expressions:
• What a day!
• I will never forget this experience.
• It was difficult, but it was worth it.
• Maybe I am more capable than I thought.
• Not exactly relaxing — but definitely meaningful.
Example:
It was not exactly a relaxing day, but it was definitely a meaningful one.

. . General Diary Entry Checklist : :
قائمة مراجعة كتابة اليوميات
Before finishing your diary entry, check that you have:
٠ I wrote the date at the beginning. كتبت التاريغ فى البداية.
٠ 1 started with a suitable greeting, such as “Dear Diary, ...”
»... مناسبة, مثل : «عزيزتى, مذكراتى, toy بدأت

• I clearly described a personal experience. قدمت وصفا واضحًا لتجربة شخصية.
٠ 1 explained what happened. شرحت ما حدث.
٠1 expressed my feelings and thoughts clearly. عبرت عن مشاعرى وأفكارى بوضوح.

Diary Entry Structure ترتيب تدوينة اليوميات
Date —> Salutation > Opening > Events (story) > Turning Point > Ending > Reflection
— Final Sentence
التحية -> الافتتتاحية -> الأحداث (القصة) -> نقطة التحول -> النهاية -> التأمل -> الجملة الختامية. >- ay Wl

1. Which statement best describes the purpose of a diary entry?
a. To present facts about a topic without personal feelings
b. To record personal experiences, thoughts, feelings, and reflections

d. To compare several people’s opinions about an issue

2. Which opening is MOST suitable for a diary entry about an unexpected cycling trip?
a. Cycling is an activity that many people enjoy for different reasons.
b. There are several advantages and disadvantages to outdoor activities.
c. Dear Diary, I thought today would be a quiet day at home, but my friend had other plans.
d. In conclusion, the cycling trip taught me an important lesson.
3. Which sentence MOST clearly introduces a turning point in a diary entry?
a. At first, the road was quiet and the air was cool.
b. We started our journey early in the morning.
c. Then, things began to change when the heat became difficult to bear.
d. In the end, I was exhausted but proud.

4. Which sentence is a reflection rather than simply an event from the story?
a. We stopped beside the Nile and drank some cold juice.
b. My legs began to ache as the road became longer.
c. We took a short break under a tree before continuing.
d. I realized that meaningful experiences do not always have to be comfortable.

5. Which order BEST follows the main parts of a diary entry in the material?
a. Reflection << Opening —> Story —> Final sentence —> Turning point —> Ending
b. Opening —> Turning point —> Story —> Reflection —> Ending —> Final sentence
c. Opening —> Story —> Turning point —> Ending —> Reflection —> Final sentence
d. Story —> Opening —> Ending —> Turning point —> Final sentence —> Reflection

6. A writer begins a diary entry with: “Yesterday I went cycling.” Which revision
develops the opening most effectively according to the material?
a. Yesterday, cycling took place and several things happened during it.
b. Cycling is a popular activity that can sometimes be difficult.
c. Dear Diary, yesterday morning I set off along the Nile expecting a relaxing ride, but
I already felt unsure about how far we would go.
d. Yesterday, I went cycling, and in conclusion it was an important experience.

7. Read this sequence: “My legs were aching badly. This experience taught me that I was
stronger than I thought. We then stopped beside the Nile and rested.” What is the main
organisational problem?

a. The opening contains too much information about the setting.
b. The reflection appears before the experience has fully ended.
c. The story uses too many first-person pronouns.

d. The final sentence is too memorable for a diary entry.

8. The writer wants to strengthen the turning point in this extract: “The first hour was
enjoyable. The road became hotter. We continued cycling.” Which revision BEST
achieves this?

a. The first hour was enjoyable because cycling can be a pleasant activity.

b. The road became hotter, and outdoor activities are sometimes tiring.

c. Then, things changed. The shade disappeared, the heat grew stronger, and I began to
wonder whether I could continue.

d. Finally, the road was hot, but the experience taught me an important lesson.

9. A diary entry describes every event clearly but says nothing about what the writer
thought or learned. Which addition would improve it most according to the diary-entry

checklist?

a. “The road was about ten kilometres long, and we travelled beside the river.”

b. “After that, we stopped, drank some water, and started cycling again.”

c. “The trip took place in the morning and finished later in the afternoon.”

d. “Looking back, I realised that the difficult moments had shown me I was more
capable than I had believed.”

| Definitions

• Choose the correct answer from a ,b,cord:

1. “A massive stone statue stood beside the temple entrance, rising far above everyone who
walked past it.” Which definition describes the meaning of “massive” in this sentence?
a. extremely old and historically important
b. extremely large in size
c. unusually heavy for its material
d. beautifully shaped and carefully decorated
2. “At the temple, Mariam photographed a ram-headed sphinx.” Which meaning best
matches “ram-headed sphinx” in this sentence?
a. an ancient Egyptian statue showing a complete ram beside a lion
b. an ancient Egyptian statue with a human body and a ram’s head
c. an ancient Egyptian statue with a lion’s head and a ram’s body
d. an ancient Egyptian statue with a lion’s body and a ram’s head
3. “Nada showed great devotion to the old woman, visiting her regularly and caring for
her whenever she needed help.” What does “devotion” mean in this context?
a. a Strong wish to receive praise from someone
b. a feeling of worry about losing someone
c. a duty that someone performs unwillingly
d. strong love and care for someone or something
4. “On the map, different colors and symbols encoded information about roads, rivers,
and important places, allowing readers to understand the area quickly.”
What does “encoded” mean in this context?
a, represented information through signs or symbols that carried meaning
b. arranged information according to its level of importance
c. removed information that was unnecessary for the reader
d. translated information from one language into another

5. “Over the years, old letters, photographs, and family documents accumulated in
a large wooden chest until there was hardly any space left.”
Based on the sentence above, what does “accumulated” mean?
a. were removed after being stored for a short time
b. were gradually collected or built up over time
c. were carefully arranged according to their size
d. were moved from one place to another

◆ القواعد اللغوية | Grammar Focus

Prepositional Phrases of Place & Time    عبارات الجر الدالة على المكان والزمان

Prepositional phrases of place and time are essential for establishing location, sequence, and duration in historical and descriptive writing.
تُعد عبارات الجر الدالة على المكان والزمان ضرورية لتحديد الموقع وترتيب الأحداث ومدة في الكتابة التاريخية والوصفية.

1 What is a prepositional phrase?    ما تشبه الجملة الظرفية (عبارة الجر)؟
→ A prepositional phrase = a preposition + a noun.    تشبه الجملة الظرفية = حرف جر + اسم.
→ Place phrases answer where?    أشباه جمل المكان تجيب عن «أين؟»
→ Time phrases answer when?    أشباه الزمان عن «متى؟»
e.g. She waited at the entrance during the morning.

2 Prepositional Phrases of Place (at / on / in)    عبارات الجر الدالة على المكان
Prepositional phrases of place tell us where something is, was, or happened.
تخبرنا عبارات الجر الدالة على المكان أين يوجد الشيء أو أين كان أو أين وقع الحدث.
→ at = a point    → on = a surface    → in = an enclosed space.

نقطة محددة    سطح أو خط    حيز مغلق

Prep.    Use it for…    Examples
at    a precise point/specific location    - at the entrance / at the top of the hill
نقطة محددة أو موقع معين
address    - at 21 Ramses St.
on    a surface    - on the wall
line/street    - on the avenue
floor    - on the 7th floor
in    an enclosed space / area    - in the hall, in the fridge, in the bottle
داخل مكان مغلق أو منطقة مغلقة
city    - in Luxor, in New York
country    - in Egypt, in the USA

3 Prepositional Phrases of Time    عبارات الجر الدالة على الزمان
Prepositional phrases of time tell us when something happened or for how long.
تخبرنا عبارات الجر الدالة على الزمان متى وقع الحدث أو المدة التي استغرقها.

— at =a precise moment

— on = a day/date

— in =a longer period

Prep. Use it for... Examples
clock times - at five
| ‘| - at half past two
a festivals 7 - at Christmas
at (night, noon, midday, midnight, sunrise ...)
days - on Sunday
on dates - on 8 March
named day-parts - on Friday morning
months - in (March, June, September)
: seasons - in spring, in summer, in winter
"| years - in 1976, in 2012
day-parts - in the (morning, afternoon, evening)

@ Range, duration & extent

These show how long, how far, and between which limits.
الحدث. lain 26) تبين هذه الحروف المدة والمسافة والحدود التي

المدى والمدة والامتداد

- Turn left at the corner. 7 - The lamp is in the corner.

the whole night (general) one moment during the night

لثلاثة فأكثر, وسط جماعة. لشيئين أو شخصين.

- We arrived in time for tea. - The train left on time.

where something stops الشيء الفعلية. ala | finally, after everything في آخر المطاف

— in a car / taxi : | بج ض ona 5 / train / plane | |
a small, enclosed vehicle a large vehicle you walk in
- She is in a taxi. - He is on the bus.
جب arrive at (a point / building / event) — arrive in (a city or country)
يصل إلى مدينة أو دولة. يصل إلى نقطة أو مبنى.
- We arrived at the temple. - We arrived in Egypt.
© Other Exceptions استثناءات أخرى
a. No preposition before:
last, next, this, every, tomorrow, yesterday. لا يوضع حرف جر قبل الكلمات السابقة كظروف زمان
e.g. I saw it last June. (not in last June)
b. Holidays:
at = whole festive period on = the single day
e.g. at Christmas on Christmas Day

c. A preposition may end a question or relative clause.
e.g. Which hotel are you staying at?
Lig الجملة الموصولة, وهو صحيح gi قد يقع حرف الجر في نهاية السؤال
d.at the weekend vs on the weekend.
on the weekend في الإنجليزية الأمريكية يقال: ٠ at the weekend في الإنجليزية البريطانية يقال: ٠

avenue of ram-headed sphinxes”) .ٍ
stretches southward in near-perfect stillness“, each one carved from sandstone that
was already ancient”) when Cleopatra walked beside it. In the space between the
columns, with the first light falling at a low angle across the hieroglyphics, it feels as if
time has not moved at all.
Karnak is not a single building but a landscape“'”’ of accumulated''*) memory.
Built over more than two thousand years from the reign‘'*) of Senusret in the Middle
Kingdom to the Ptolemaic era - the complex represents the longest continuous building
project in human history. Each pharaoh added, changed, or inscribed“'*) something,
leaving layers of devotion '® and ambition encoded '” in stone. The Hypostyle Hall
alone contains 134 massive''®) columns, decorated from floor to ceiling. This space was ٠
designed not simply to impress but to communicate): it belonged to the gods, and the
Pharaoh was their representative on earth. : .

What I find most significant?” however, is not what has survived, ie

but the question the site invites us to ask as Egyptians today. Karnak _ sve 2
was not built for tourists; it was built for a civilization®”. Yet as we att ns a
walk through its vast spaces, we are reminded that history is not only Jou - يواصل (24)
something we observe, but something we interpret? and مجرد - نظري (25)

carry forward”. If a site of memory belongs to everyone, how

should it be understood, and whose voices should shape the way it is told? These are
not abstract) questions, they are the questions that every Egyptian student standing
between those columns should feel encouraged to explore.

1. Which historical attraction is described in the passage?

◆ تدريبات المفردات | Exercises on Vocabulary

• Choose the correct answer from a , b , c or d :

◆ المفردات الرئيسية | Key Vocabulary

1. The museum provides ............ labels in Arabic, English, French, and German.
a. monolingual     b. bilingual     c. multilingual     d. unilingual
2. The museum's exhibitions are ............ to visitors of all ages, including those with disabilities.
a. accessible     b. conserved     c. chronological     d. multilingual

oe Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

3. “The city’s new information website is accessible to people with different needs. Users
can get the information they need on it.” Which situation best reflects the meaning of
“accessible”?

a. A sports center allows members to enter only during two short periods each week.

b. A cinema keeps some seats unavailable even when no one has booked them.

c. A public garden provides smooth paths and clear signs so visitors with different
needs can use it comfortably.

d. A library stores its most popular books in a room that visitors cannot enter.

4. “A website presented the town’s history in chronological order. Visitors first read about
events from the nineteenth century before moving through each later period.” Which
situation best reflects the idea of ‘chronological’?

a. A music app groups songs according to whether they are calm, fast, or energetic.

b. A shop displays clothes according to colour rather than when they were made.

c. A photo album places family pictures into separate sections for holidays and birthdays.

d. A documentary tells what happened on the first day of a journey before showing the
events of the following days.

5. “A rare old manuscript was placed in a special case where light and moisture could
not damage it. These measures were part of its conservation.” Which situation is the
clearest example of “conservation’’?

a. A family photographs an old wooden boat before selling it to another owner.

b. A town strengthens a historic fountain and limits anything that could cause further
damage to it.

c. A café replaces its traditional wooden tables with modern plastic ones.

d. A visitor buys a small model of an ancient building from a gift shop.

6. “As the museum’s curator, Yasmeen regularly checks the collection, studies the history
of its objects, and decides which stories future displays should tell.” Which person has
a similar role?

a. Hossam welcomes visitors at the entrance and shows them where to buy tickets.

b. Nada leads groups around a cultural center and explains what they can see.

c. Fouad is responsible for a gallery’s collection and decides which works should
appear in its major displays.

d. Arwa repairs damaged frames and broken display cases when necessary.

7. “The curation of the new museum room took months. By opening day, every object
had a clear purpose in the story the exhibition told, and nothing had been included by
chance.” Which situation best reflects “curation”?

a. Workers repaint a gallery and replace several broken lights before visitors arrive.

b. Visitors vote for the historical object they find most attractive during their tour.

c. Amuseum moves all its stored objects into one large room without deciding how to
present them.

d. A cultural center carefully chooses material for a heritage display and plans how the
pieces will work together for visitors.

8. “The hospital uses multilingual signs so that patients from different countries can

find important rooms without always needing someone to translate for them.” Which

situation best reflects the same idea?

a. Arestaurant uses photographs beside every dish to help customers choose their meals.

b. Acinema shows the same film at several different times during the day.

c. A tourist center prepares one welcome guide that visitors can read in Arabic, English,
French, and German.

d. A bookshop places books from different countries on separate shelves according to
their subject.

. “In the family album, the first pages show Rodayna as a baby, the middle pages show

her childhood, and the final pages contain recent photographs. The pictures are arranged

b. according to how important each photograph is

c. according to the place where each photograph was taken

d. according to the number of people shown in each photograph

. “Specialists placed the old manuscript in a special case away from strong light and

moisture. These measures are part of its conservation.” In this context, “conservation’

a. the process of displaying something so more people can see it

b. the process of protecting something valuable from being damaged or lost

c. the process of copying something before returning the original

d. the process of changing something old to make it look modern

. “The ministry redesigned its website so that people could sign in and use it without

c. easy for people with different needs to use and understand

d. designed to contain as much information as possible

. “The museum displayed statues of three rulers from one dynasty: a grandfather, his

b. a period when several unrelated leaders share power

c. a line of rulers from the same family over a period of time

d. a group of countries controlled by one powerful king

. “When a valuable painting arrived at the museum, the curator decided where it should

c. a person who explains exhibits to groups during a visit

d. a person responsible for managing and organizing a museum collection

b. a natural object found in an area of historical interest
Cc. an object made or used by people in the past and valued for its history
d. an ordinary object that has recently been bought for decoration

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

Concession Clauses  عبارات التناقض أولااستدراك ( التعابير عما هو غير متوقع)

→ Good academic and journalistic writing acknowledges counterarguments before addressing them. This is done through concession clauses – they show that the writer is aware of other viewpoints.

تعترف الكتابة الأكاديمية والصحفية الجيدة بالحجج المضادة قبل مناقشتها والرد عليها. ويتم ذلك باستخدام جمل الإقرار بالرأي الآخر؛ فهي توضح أن الكاتب على دراية بوجهات النظر الأخرى.

1 What are concession clauses?

→ Concession clauses are language structures used to introduce an opposing idea politely before presenting your own idea.

جمل الإقرار بالرأي الآخر هي تراكيب لغوية تُستخدم لتقديم الفكرة المعارضة بطريقة مهذبة قبل عرض مفكرتك أو وجهة نظرك.

• Counterargument  الحجة المضادة

→ The opposite opinion or idea that argues against your point of view.

الرأي أو الفكرة المعارضة لوجهة نظرك ومن أهم روابط الاستدراك والإقرار بالرأي الآخر.

- although, though, even though, despite, in spite of, nevertheless

- however (showing unexpected result)

e.g. - Although he studied hard, he failed the exam.

- Despite being tired, she continued working.

2 In Brief

١ الروابط التالية يأتي بعدها جملة وتُستخدم في الوسط (وبعضها قد يبدأ جملة منفصلة للفظ متصلة المعنى):

subj./obj. + verb …  →  subj./obj. + verb …

but    لكن    - She is rich; however, she is unhappy.
yet    ومع ذلك    = She is rich. However, she is unhappy.
however    ومع ذلك    - The post contained false information; nevertheless,
nevertheless    ومع هذا    millions of users shared it.
nonetheless    ومع ذلك    = The post contained false information. Nevertheless,
still    رغم ذلك    millions of users shared it.

٢ الروابط التالية يأتي بعدها جملة (ثانوية) وتستخدم في البداية أو الوسط:

+, + subj./obj. + verb ...

| | although | رغم أن | - Although she is rich, she is unhappy.
| though | رغم أن | = She is unhappy although she is rich.
| even though | رغم أن | - Even if he is wrong, he won't apologize.
| even if | حتى لو | = He won't apologize even if he is wrong.
| While | في حين أن | - Whereas he is fit, I am overweight.
| whereas | في حين أن | = I am overweight whereas he is fit.

٢ حروف الجر التالية يأتي بعدها عبارة اسمية (اسم أو فعل مضاف له ing) بشرط أن تبدأ الجملة الثانية بنفس فاعل أو مفعول الجملة الأولى وتستخدم في البداية أو الوسط:

+, + noun / gerund ...

| Despite | رغم | - Despite being rich, she is unhappy.
| In spite of | بالرغم من | = She is unhappy despite being rich.
| Regardless of | | - Notwithstanding his mistakes, he won't apologize.
| |بغض النظر عن | = He won't apologize notwithstanding his mistakes.
| notwithstanding | رغم

Structure Comparison مقارنة التراكيب

| Correct | Incorrect |
|---|---|
| Although the post was popular, it was inaccurate. (✓) | Despite the post was popular, it was inaccurate. (✗) |
| Despite the popularity of the post, it was inaccurate. (✓) | Although the popularity of the post, it was inaccurate. (✗) |

Notes on some concession expressions ملاحظات على بعض تعبيرات الاستدراك

1 While

While + subj. + verb, ... + main clause.

تأتي (while) وبعدها جملة بمعنى (رغم أن / بينما) وفي هذه الحالة لا تغير رابطة زمنية، بل تُستخدم لإظهار التباين بين فكرتين.

eg. - While social media can be useful for communication, it can also create pressure on young people.

2 While it is true that

While it is true that + subj. + verb, ... + main clause.

هذه الصيغة أيضًا يتبعها جملة وتعني (رغم أن ... صحيح) وهي صيغة أكثر رسمية وتأثيرًا. وتوضح للقارئ: صحيح أن الفكرة الأولى صحيحة، ولكن توجد اعتبارات أخرى يجب وضعها في الحسبان.

ex. - While it is true that social media can be useful for communication, it can also create pressure on young people.

هذه الصيغة تؤكد الفكرة الأولى بشكل أكبر، وهي مناسبة للمقالات، والكتابة الجدلية، والمناقشات الرسمية.

3 Admittedly

Admittedly, + full sentence. However, / but + contrast.

تأتي (Admittedly) بمعنى (لا شك أن / من المسلم به أن / صحيح أن) وتُستخدم للإقرار بأن فكرة ما صحيحة، ثم غالبًا نضيف فكرة أخرى مخالفة أو مُخالفة لها.

ex. - Admittedly, online shopping is convenient. However, buyers should compare prices and delivery costs.

4 It may be argued that

It may be argued that + subject + verb.

تأتي هذه العبارة بمعنى (يمكن القول إن / يمكن المجادة بأن / قد يُقال إن) وتُستخدم في الكتابة الرسمية لعرض رأي أو وجهة نظر دون التأكيد أنّها حقيقة مؤكدة.

ex. - It may be argued that social media has changed the way young people learn about historical events.

3 Rewrite Note

لا يمكن استخدام الروابط التي يأتي بعدها (n/g) بدلًا من الروابط التي يأتي بعدها جملة إلا إذا كانت الجملة الثانية تبدأ بالفاعل / المفعول المذكوف من الجملة الأولى:

Although the post was popular, it was inaccurate. (Despite) | Despite being popular, the post was inaccurate. (✓)

Although the post was popular, I know it was inaccurate. (Despite) | Despite being popular, I know the post was inaccurate. (✗)

⚠ Attention انتبه

Avoid Double Contrast تجنّب التناقض المزدوج

- although + but (✗)    - despite + but (✗)

Correct | Incorrect

Although the post was viral, it was false. (✓) | Although the post was viral, but it was false. (✗)

Despite being viral, the post was false. (✓) | Despite being viral, but the post was false. (✗)

4 Punctuation Notes ملاحظات الترقيم

1. Use a comma after an introductory clause

استخدم الفاصلة بعد الجملة الافتتاحية (عندما تستخدم الرابطة في البداية)، ولا تستخدمها إذا كانت الرابطة في الوسط:

e.g. - Although the post looked convincing, it was fake.
- The post was fake although many people believed it.

2. Before 'however,' you can use a (.), or a (;) and a (,) after it.

e.g. -The image became popular. However, historians criticized it. (✓)

e.g. - The image became popular; however, historians criticized it. (✓)

لا تستخدم (،) فقط قبل (however) إذا كانت تربط جملتين كاملتين:

e.g. - The image became popular, however historians criticized it. (✓)

لكن تستخدم (،) قبل وبعد (however) إذا كانت في منتصف جملة واحدة:

e.g. - Sama liked the idea. Ahmed, however, rejected it. (✓)

Advanced Note (1): Subject or verb — ملاحظة (1) للفائقين: فاعل أو فعل

قد يكون الفعل المضاف له (ing) بعد النقط فاعلا او مفعولا وليس فعل، وفي هذه الحالة تحتاج لجملة:

1. ............ eating a lot of food, Rodayna still feels hungry.
a. Although — b. But — c. Despite — d. However

2. ............ eating a lot of food comforts me. It makes me gain weight.
a. Although — b. But — c. Despite — d. However

في الجملة الاولي بعد النقط توجد عبارة اسم الفعل (eating a lot of food) وهي ليست جملة، إذا فھي تأخذ (Despite)
في الجملة الثانية بعد النقط توجد عبارة (eating a lot of food comforts me) وهي جملة فاعلها (eating a lot of food) وفععلها (comforts)، إذا فھي تأخذ (Despite)

Advanced Note (2): Omission — ملاحظة (2) للفائقين: الحذف

يمكن حذف الفاعل/المفعول والفعل من بعد الرابطة مباشرة إذا كان المعنى واضحا وكانت الجملة الثانية تبدأ بالفاعل/
المفعول المحذوف من الجملة الاولي:

e.g.- Although this car is very old, it is expensive.
= Although very old, this car is expensive.
e.g.- Although I was in a hurry, I waited for her.
= Although in a hurry, I waited for her.

Advanced Note (3): Inversion — ملاحظة (3) للفائقين: التقديم والتأخير

لاحظ صيغ التقديم والتأخير التالية في اللغة الرسمية

Structure — e.g.

Although + subj. + be + adj/n ...
= Adjective/Noun + though/as + subj. + be ...
Although + subj. + verb + adj/adv...
= However + adj/adv + subj. + verb ...

Although he was exhausted, he helped us.
= Exhausted as he was, he helped us.
Although he was exhausted, he helped us.
= However exhausted he was, he helped us.

Advanced Note (4): the fact that — ملاحظة (4) للفائقين

لاحظ صيغ التقديم والتأخير التالية في اللغة الرسمية :

عند استخدام (the fact that) أو (that) بعد (despite, in spite of, regardless of) يأتي بعدها جملة وليس (n/g):

e.g. Despite being busy, she always finds time to help me.
= Despite the fact that she is busy, she always finds time to help me.

◆ الكتابة العملية | Writing in Action

DISCURSIVE PARAGRAPH الفقرة النقاشية

1 What Is a Discursive Paragraph? ما الفقرة النقاشية؟

A discursive paragraph is a piece of writing that discusses different ideas or opinions about a topic in a balanced and objective way before reaching a conclusion or expressing a personal opinion.

الفقرة النقاشية هي نوع من الكتابة يعرض آراء أو وجهات نظر مختلفة حول موضوع معين بطريقة متوازنة وموضوعية، ثم ينتهي برأي شخصي أو استنتاج مناسب.

2 What is a discursive paragraph used for? ما الغرض من الفقرة النقاشية؟

1. Discussing different viewpoints about a topic. مناقشة وجهات النظر المختلفة حول موضوع ما.
2. Presenting arguments and counterarguments. عرض الحجج والآراء المضادة.
3. Comparing different perspectives. مقارنة الأفكار ووجهات النظر المختلفة.
4. Encouraging critical thinking. تنمية التفكير النقدي.
5. Reaching a balanced conclusion based on evidence and reasoning. الوصول إلى استنتاج متوازن قائم على الأدلة والمنطق.

Main Parts of a Discursive Paragraph
الأجزاء الرئيسية للفقرة النقاشية

A discursive paragraph consists of five main parts as modelled in the lesson.
تتكون الفقرة النقاشية من خمسة أجزاء رئيسية وفقًا لنموذج الكتابة الوارد في الدرس.

1 Topic Sentence الجملة الافتتاحية
Introduces the issue or topic clearly. تقدم الجملة الافتتاحية التي ستُناقش بوضوح.
• Useful expressions:
Many people have different opinions about preserving history.
..........

2 First Point / First Perspective الرأي الأول / وجهة النظر الأولى
Presents one side of the argument with explanation or examples.
يعرض الكاتب وجهة نظر معينة ويدعمها بالتفسير أو الأمثلة.

• Useful expressions:
- On the one hand, ...
- Some people believe that ...
- One argument in favour of ... is that ...

3 Contrasting Point / Opposite Perspective
الرأي المقابل / وجهة النظر الأخرى
Presents the opposite viewpoint using concession clauses and contrast language.
يعرض الكاتب الرأي المخالف مستخدماً تراكيب المخالفة وأدوات التباين.
• Useful expressions:
- On the other hand, ... - However, ...
- Nevertheless, ... - It may be argued that ...

4 Personal Opinion / Balanced View
الرأي الشخصي / الرؤية المتوازنة
Expresses a personal opinion or presents a balanced judgement after considering both sides.
يعبر الكاتب عن رأيه الشخصي أو يقدم رؤية متوازنة بعد مناقشة الرأيين.
• Useful expressions:
- In my opinion, ... - From my perspective, ...
- It seems that ... -A balanced approach may be the best solution.

5 Concluding Sentence
الجملة الختامية
Ends the paragraph with a final statement that summarises the discussion.
تُنهي الفقرة بجملة ختامية تُلخص الأفكار الرئيسية التي تمت مناقشتها.
• Useful expressions:
In conclusion, ... / To sum up, ... / Overall, ...

Grammar Commonly Used in Discursive Paragraphs
التراكيب اللغوية المستخدمة في الفقرة النقاشية

• Concession Clauses:
تراكيب المخالفة:
to acknowledge opposing viewppints.
لعرض الرأي المقابل بصورة متوازنة.
• Prepositional Phrases:
شبه الجمل:
to add details and support ideas.
لإضافة التفاصيل ودعم الأفكار.
Structure
Although + clause بالرغم من أن + جملة
- Although museums are expensive to maintain, they preserve valuable heritage.
بالرغم من أن المتاحف تُكلف كثيراً للصيانة، إلا أنها تحفظ التراث الثمين.

Despite + noun phrase على الرغم من + اسم
- Despite technological advances, many people still prefer visiting museums in person.
Prepositional phrases of place & time شبه الجمل الدالة على المكان والزمان
- In my opinion, museums play an essential role in preserving identity.
Quick-Reference: Structure at a Glance

1 2 3 4 5
Topic Sentence / الجملة الافتتاحية
First Point / الرأي الأول
Contrasting Point / الرأي المقابل
Personal Opinion / الرأي الشخصي
Concluding Sentence / الجملة الختامية

◆ تدريبات Writing | Exercises On Writing

Choose the correct answer from a,b,c ord:

1. Which sentence would work best as the topic sentence of a discursive paragraph about
museums and cultural identity?
a. The Grand Egyptian Museum is located near the Pyramids of Giza.
b. Many people have different opinions about the role museums play in preserving
cultural identity.
c. On the other hand, some people prefer learning about history online.
d. In conclusion, museums remain important places for understanding the past.

2. Which sentence most clearly introduces the first perspective in a discussion about
preserving historical places?
a. In conclusion, both modern development and preservation deserve careful
consideration.
b. However, maintaining historical places can require considerable effort.
c. From my perspective, protecting important sites should remain a priority.
d. On the one hand, preserving historical places helps future generations understand
their heritage.
3. Which expression is most suitable for introducing an opposite perspective in
a discursive paragraph?
a. In my opinion, b. On the other hand, c.Inconclusion, d. For example,
4. Which sentence correctly uses a concession clause to acknowledge an opposing point?
a. Although museums can be expensive to maintain, they preserve valuable heritage.
b. Museums preserve valuable heritage because they contain ancient objects.
c. Museums contain ancient objects, and many visitors enjoy seeing them.
d. Museums preserve valuable heritage; for example, they protect historical artefacts.

5. Which sentence would be MOST suitable as the concluding sentence of a balanced
discussion about museums?
a. Some people believe that online museums are easier to visit.
b. On the one hand, museums protect important historical objects.
c. Overall, museums remain valuable because they preserve heritage while helping
people understand the past.
d. In my opinion, museum visits are particularly enjoyable.
6. A writer has already explained why museums are important for preserving artifacts.
Which sentence best introduces a contrasting point?
a. For example, museums can display objects from different periods of history.
b. In my opinion, museums should continue to receive public attention.
c. On the other hand, some people argue that digital resources can make historical
knowledge more widely accessible.
d. In conclusion, museums have several important cultural functions.

7. Which sentence best supports the first perspective with a relevant example?
a. On the other hand, some people prefer to explore history online.

b. For example, museums can preserve ancient objects and display them for future
generations.

c. In conclusion, different methods of preserving history have advantages.
d. From my perspective, museums play an essential cultural role.

8. Which sentence is most appropriate for the personal opinion or balanced-view part of
a discursive paragraph?
a. On the one hand, museums offer visitors direct access to historical objects.
b. However, virtual museums can make information easier to reach.
c. For example, people can explore digital collections from home.
d. From my perspective, both physical and digital museums can contribute to
preserving and understanding history.

9. A paragraph has presented the advantages of physical museums and the benefits of
virtual museums. Which sentence would best follow before the conclusion?

a. In my opinion, physical museums remain essential, although digital alternatives can
provide useful additional access to history.

b. On the one hand, museums contain objects from the past.
c. For example, some museums use modern technology.
d. However, museums display historical objects.

10. Which sentence uses a prepositional phrase of place to add a clear supporting detail?
a. Although the museum is modern, it preserves objects from the past.
b. In conclusion, museums connect people with their history.

c. Near the Pyramids of Giza, the museum offers visitors access to an important
collection of ancient objects.

d. On the other hand, some people prefer learning about history online.

SQ Write a discursive paragraph of about (150-180) words about:

To what extent do museums play an important role in preserving history and identity?
— Introduce the topic clearly

— Present one side of the argument and the opposite viewpoint
— use concession clauses

— state your opinion

◆ المفردات في السياق: أسئلة الموقف | Vocabulary in Context: Scenario-based Questions

7.“A news website made a claim that a young player had been chosen for the national
team. People waited for an official announcement before accepting the news as true.”
Which situation best illustrates the meaning of the word “claim’’?

a. A traveler asks whether a new train service will begin next month.

b. A photographer shows several pictures taken during a city festival.

c. A producer tells customers that his new product lasts twice as long as the old one.
d. A reporter compares two official documents before writing an article.

8. “For several months, Nada has called her grandmother every Friday evening.

She communicates with her consistently.” Which situation demonstrates the same

meaning as “consistently”?

a. Arunner trains on several days one week but does nothing for the next two weeks.

b. A café changes the way it prepares the same drink whenever a different worker is
present.

c. Areader finishes three books during one holiday but rarely reads during the rest of
the year.

d. A baker checks the temperature before every batch so that each one is prepared in
the same careful way.

9. “Restoring the old temple was significant because it protected an important part of the
area’s history and allowed younger people to learn how it had once been used.” Which
situation best illustrates “significant’’?

a. A new bus route gives several distant villages much easier access to the nearest
hospital.

b. A family moves the dining table to the other side of the same room.

c. A café prints its menu on slightly thicker paper than before.

d. A boy changes the picture used as the background on his phone.

10. “Mohannad saw a short video that seemed to show a serious argument, but he noticed
that the beginning had been cut out. His media literacy led him to watch the full clip
before deciding what had really happened.” Which situation is the clearest example of
“media literacy”?

a. Rokaya follows a travel account because its photographs are attractive and colorful.

b. Aya notices that an online post is a paid advertisement and looks carefully at its
message before deciding whether to believe it.

c. Marwan saves a funny video so that he can show it to his friends later.

d. Hady watches the same news program each evening because he likes the presenter.

11. “Before paying for a used phone, Adam compared the serial number on the box with
the number shown in the phone’s settings to verify that they matched.” Which situation
best demonstrates the same meaning as “verify”?

a. A traveler chooses the shortest route because it looks easiest on the map.

b. A customer keeps a receipt in case the item needs to be returned later.

c. A woman reads the amount on an electricity bill twice and compares it with the
meter reading before making the payment.

d. A man accepts the delivery time because the message looks similar to one he
received before.

A historical post may become popular very quickly, but popularity is not proof.
A strange statement about an ancient monument can attract millions of views. Careful
readers should check the source and ask whether the evidence is reliable. This is an
important matter, because false history can affect identity. For that reason, accuracy must
become an importance in education. Students also need critical media awareness so that they

Was do not accept every viral post blindly.

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

claim (n) ادعاء

Goes with:
• claim about ادعاء بشأن
• claim that ادعاء بأن
• false claim ادعاء زائف

Word Family

Word | Example
claim (n) = statement ادعاء | The post made a false claim.
claim (v) = to state as true يدعي | He claims that I have broken his tablet.
claimant (n) = a person who makes a claim مدع (صاحب الدعوى) | The claimant had no evidence.
claimed (adj) = stated as true (unproven) مدعي/مزعوم | The claimed discovery was not verified.

© Don’t mix
- claim (said as true, not necessarily proven)
- fact (proven to be true)
A claim needs verification; a fact is already proven.
فهي حقيقة مثبتة. (fact) Lol يحتاج إلى تحقق؛ clos! يعني (Claim) الإسم

consistently (adv) باستمرار
و Goes with:
* consistently show ... تظهر ... باستمرار ¢ consistently prove ... يثبت ... باستمرار
® Word Family
Word Example

STE RG ووم موزل reading and speaking.

يتكون من
consistent (adj) = always the : . :
(adj) y The evidence is consistent.
cull / متسق same

consistency (n) = the quality

; Accuracy needs consistency.
of being the same)  تابث / اتساق 7 7

© Don’t mix
consistently = always / repeatedly in the same way
ع constantly = very often, sometimes annoyingly
‘Consistently’ suggests a regular pattern; ‘constantly’ suggests nonstop frequency.
فتعني تكرارًا مستمرًا دون توقف. (constantly) تعني نمطا منتظفًا؛ أما (consistently) aols

media literacy (n) الثقافة الإعلامية
© Goes with:
• develop media literacy يطور مهارات الثقافة الإعلامية
* improve media literacy تحسن الثقافة الإعلامية
80 Word Family
Word Example
media (n) = channels of communication Social media spreads information quickly.
وسائل الإعلام
literate (adj) = able to read or understand She is media-literate.
a field ملم / قادر على القراءة

IV / LISTENING & READING

The Viral Past

To “go viral” means to spread rapidly and widely online (often
within 24 hours). This spread is driven by emotional engagement”
(surprise, pride, or nostalgia“) rather than historical accuracy or truth.

The Algorithm and the Archive

Why Viral History is Not Always True History

Never before in human history have historical images, artifacts ,

' and claims® reached many people so quickly. A single post about an

_ Egyptian pharaoh can accumulate millions of views within

: twenty-four hours; a colorized” photograph of ancient Alexandria can be
| shared across continents before a single historian has had the opportunity
ما verify® its accuracy”. The scale” and speed of this spread are, in

! themselves, remarkable. The problem is that scale and speed have no real
- relationship to truth.

The way social media platforms work increases this risk considerably.
These platforms are designed not to prioritize’) accuracy but to promote
engagement. Research consistently” shows that engagement is driven
by emotion. Content” that causes surprise, pride, nostalgia, or strong
reaction spreads more rapidly”) than content that is
_ measured, detailed, or carefully qualified". A viral
post may claim that the Great Pyramid was built in a
single decade by a team of fifty engineers. Such posts
often accumulate more shares than carefully sourced
corrections. In reality, the actual construction” process
unfolded” over decades and involved tens of thousands
of workers.

The consequences ®) for our understanding of
history are significant. In Egypt, where the relationship
: between ancient heritage” and national identity is
particularly important, the viral spread of historical
myths” and false claims risks distorting” the public’s
understanding of who they are and where they come

consulting multiple perspectives™
: actually happened and the past that merely trends.

| 2) Vocabulary in context : Scenario-based questions

7. “A football website reported that a match ended 3-1, but the official result was 2-1.
Readers questioned the accuracy of the website’s information.” Which situation best
illustrates “accuracy”?

a. A traveler describes a journey in an entertaining way but leaves out several details.
b. A photographer chooses the brightest picture from a group of similar photos.

c. Amap shows every road name and distance correctly, so drivers can rely on it.

d. A shop changes its opening hours during a public holiday.

8. “Before selling an old family house, the owners collected previous contracts. This
documentation helped show how the property had changed over the years.” Which
situation best reflects “documentation”?

a. A traveler tells relatives about a memorable holiday after returning home.

b. A sports club keeps signed forms, match records, and dated reports from each season.
c. A family replaces old furniture before guests arrive for a celebration.

d. A visitor chooses several favorite pictures from an exhibition.

9. “The old photograph had become fragile with age, so its edges tore when someone
handled it carelessly.” Which situation best reflects “fragile”?
a. A thick travel bag remains undamaged after being dropped several times.
b. A very old paper letter begins to tear when it is unfolded too roughly.
c. Aheavy wooden door needs two people to lift it from its frame.
d. A rubber ball changes shape when pressed but returns to normal.

10. “Despite being only twelve years old, Salma completed a detailed painting that visitors
thought had been made by an experienced artist. Her achievement was remarkable.”
Which situation best illustrates “remarkable”?

a. A singer performs a familiar song correctly at a family celebration.

b. A cyclist finishes a normal daily ride at his usual pace.

c. A young inventor creates a simple device that helps an entire country save water.
d. A baker prepares the same type of bread that he makes every morning.

11. “After the boat sank near the shore, most of it remained submerged, with only a small
part visible above the surface.” Which situation best demonstrates “submerged”?
a. A bicycle is left outside during heavy rain and becomes completely wet.

b. A box is placed beneath a bed so that it cannot be seen easily.
c. Aswimmer stands in shallow water that reaches only to his knees.
d. A fallen car lies beneath floodwater so that only its roof can still be seen.

1 Verbal Collocations — الاندزات اللغظية

analyze — primary evidence — يحلل دليل أوليا
report — archaeological discoveries — يعن عن اكتشافات أثرية

create — a lasting record — ينشى سجل دائم
share — events with the public — يشارك الأحداث مع الجمهور

document — historical events — يوثق أحدأثًا تاريخية
shape — the audience's understanding — يشكل فهم الجمهور

erect — a protective screen — يضع حاجزًا واقيا
suggest — a possible connection — يشير الى صلة محتملة

keep — people updated — يبقي الناس على اطلاع
uncover — a royal tomb — يكشف مقبرة ملكية

locate — a sealed door — يحدد مكان باب مغلق
verify — primary evidence — يتحقق من دليل أولي

record — events as they happen — يسجل الأحداث فور وقوعها

as they happen — فور وقوعها

4 Language Notes ملاحظات لغوية

accuracy (n) دقة

@ Goes with:

* historical accuracy الدقة التاريخية e accuracy of information | دقة المعلومات
• check accuracy دقة yo يتحقق  * ensure accuracy يضمن الدقة

€) Word Family

Word Example
accurate (adj) - The report gives accurate details. دقيق
accurately (adv) - The discovery was reported accurately. بدقة
accuracy (n) - Accuracy is important in documentation. دقة
© Don’t mix
- accuracy = correctness of information | الدقة
- reliability = trustworthiness of the source الموثوقية
documentation (n) توثيق
و Goes with:
• careful documentation 615) توثيق
* historical documentation توثيق تاريخي
٠ real-time documentation توثيق فوري
• documentation process عملية التوثيق
© Word Family
Word Example
document (v) - They document discoveries online. aig
document (n) - The document records the event. وثيقة
documentation (n) - Documentation protects evidence. توثيق
documented (adj) - The event was well documented. Gigo

© Don’t mix
- documentation = recording and keeping details توثيق
- description = saying what something is like cag

excavation (n) تنقيب

© Goes with:

• begin an excavation يبدأ التنقيب e full excavation حفر كامل
* excavation site موقع الحفر ° excavation work عملية التنقيب

Word Family

- excavation = digging to find ancient remains
- discovery = finding something important

Word Example
excavate (v) - Archaeologists excavate ancient sites. ينقب
excavation (n) - The excavation will begin soon. تنقيب
excavated (adj) - The excavated objects were preserved. dic مستخرج / منقب
© Don’t mix

هش / قابل للكسر fragile (adj)

© Goes with:

• fragile artifact تحفة هشة ¢ fragile material مادة هشة

Word Family

Word Example
fragile (adj) - The artifact is fragile. هش
fragility (n) - The fragility of the papyrus requires care. هشاشة
©9 Don’t mix
- fragile = easily broken or damaged هش
- weak = not physically strong or not effective ضعيف
remarkable (adj) ملحوظ / jl
و Goes with:
eremarkable achievement إنجاز بارز e remarkable discovery اكتشاف مهم
* remarkable moment لحظة فارقة ° remarkable site موقع بارز
Word Family
Word Example
remarkable (adj) - It was a remarkable discovery. مدهش
remarkably (adv) - The tomb was remarkably well preserved. loro على نحو
© Don’t mix
- remarkable = impressive or unusual بارز
-important = having great value or significance هام

مغمور / غارق تحت الماء submerged (adj)

@ Goes with:

* evidence of submergence دليل علي الغرق ¢ submergence of a city المدينة Gye
e submergence under water الغرق تحت الماء

€) Word Family

Word Example
submerge (v) - Water can submerge ancient ruins. يغمر
submerged (adj) - Asubmerged city was found near Alexandria. مغمور
submergence (n) - Submergence may hide ancient sites. joc

© Don’t mix
- submergence = being under water
-excavation = digging underground

[News Report  يربخلا التقرير |

1. What is a News Report? ما التقرير الخبري
A news report is a short piece of writing that informs people about a recent event.

التقرير الإخبارى نص قصير يقدم للناس معلومات عن حدث وقع مؤخرًا.

! 1. Headline العنوان

Main Parts of aNews Report — م

Function

- It should be short and interesting. ينبغى أن يكون قصيرًا وجذابًا.
- It tells the main event quickly. Jlaislg القارئ بالحدث الرئيسى بسرعة psu
e.g. - Ancient Tomb Discovered in Luxor.

2. Lead المقدمة

- It is an opening sentence answering What? Where? When?
(Vio 7 افتتاحية تجيب عن الأسئلة (ماذا / أين aloo
e.g. - A royal tomb was discovered in Luxor this week.

Who discovered it, what it is, why it is important.

3. Details التفاصيل pao توضح من شارك فى الحدث, ماذا حدث ولماذا يعد |
e.g. - Experts say the tomb may belong to a royal family.
4, Backeround - Extra information to help readers understand.
معلومات إضافية تساعد القراء على فهم الحدث. الخاقزة

e.g. - In the past, discoveries were reported slowly.

5. Closing الخاتمة

- A short final sentence that ends the report. التقرين Lai جملة ختامية قصيرة
e.g. - Experts say more details will be revealed soon.

Read the following passage, then answer the questions.

Computers are machines that can help us in many ways; however, they cannot think or do
things on their own. Humans have to feed them with information and tell them what to do
with it. They can save much time and work. For example, all the information and the office
files can be stored in a computer’s ‘memory’.

The first computers were huge and costly. They filled up almost the whole floor of large
offices. Later, because of the usefulness and demand for computers in business, scientists
soon found ways to produce cheaper and smaller computers. They invented chips which
made it possible to store more information in less space.

Today, computers are not only cheaper, but also more compact. They can just be placed
on top of an ordinary writing table. They can even be carried from place to place easily.
Computers are not only used in offices by companies, but they are also used at home, by
most families who can easily afford them.

Robots, on the other hand, are not mechanical people. They are only moving parts
controlled by a computer. A robot can do the same work for twenty-four hours, and yet it
does not complain or get tired. In Japan and some places in America, robots are used in
factories to assemble cars. As computers have become more common in businesses and
factories, people fear that one day computers and computer-controlled robots will put human
workers out of work.
